Purely a structural split — no field's save handler, validation, or edit/display toggle logic changed; the full suite (143 files, 1505 tests) needed no changes beyond what the mechanical extraction itself required. |
| `domain/plannedMeal/` (new: `PlannedMeal.ts`, `PlannedMealRepository.ts`), `infrastructure/persistence/indexeddb/plannedMealRepository.ts` (new), `db.ts` (v14), `stores/plannedMealStore.ts` (new), `daily-log/PlannedMealsSection.tsx` (new), `DailyEntryFormTop.tsx`, `todaySectionsCollapseStore.ts`, `exportBundleSchema.ts`/`exportBundle.ts`/`exportActions.ts` (#614) | Lightweight "stage a meal for a future day" drafts — resolved a design fork via `AskUserQuestion` before coding (drafts as a separate store vs. real `DailyEntry` rows flagged `isPlanned`); chose the separate store specifically so a draft can never be summed into any day's totals/analytics/export by accident, unlike a flagged `DailyEntry` row which would need every consumer (correlations, exports, dashboards) to remember to filter it out. New `PlannedMeal` (`{ id, date, name, amountKcal?, createdAt }`) is deliberately much smaller than a real `CalorieEntry` — no items/macros/brand — "lightweight" is the whole point; `usePlannedMealStore` follows the same IndexedDB-backed shape `useCustomMetricStore`/`useWeeklyNoteStore` already use (not the `persist`-to-localStorage shape reserved for UI preferences), backed by a new `plannedMeals` Dexie table (`id, date`, `db.ts` v14, no `.upgrade()` — new store only). `PlannedMealsSection.tsx` mounts on `DailyEntryFormTop.tsx` right after Meals (own bordered `Collapsible`, new `plannedMeals` key in `todaySectionsCollapseStore.ts`, same accordion shape as Water/Custom Metrics): any drafts already staged *for* the currently-open date show with "Add to log"/discard, and a "+ Plan a meal for tomorrow" trigger always stages a new one dated the day *after* whatever date is open — so opening today's log is enough to plan tomorrow ("materializes into the day when opened", per the issue's own framing, on the promote side). Promoting a draft calls the exact same `onChange`-then-`persist()` closure `MealList.tsx` already uses for a normally-added meal (not a separate direct-IndexedDB write) — avoids the #285 class of bug where a write bypassing the live loaded form state could get silently overwritten by a later autosave. Purely additive to the export bundle (`plannedMeals?: PlannedMeal[]`, `exportBundleSchema.ts`) — no version bump, no unique secondary index beyond `id` so import has no `ConstraintError` risk to guard against (same reasoning `recipes`/`customMetrics`/`customCorrelations` already established). **`loadAll()` guard**: since `PlannedMealsSection` mounts unconditionally on the Day screen's most-frequently-remounted path (unlike `CustomMetricLogSection`, which is at least still mounted the same way but only actually renders content once metrics exist), an unguarded per-mount `loadAll()` reproducibly caused non-deterministic `TodayScreen.test.tsx` failures — a different, seemingly-unrelated test failing each run once the file's ~97 tests were all mounting/unmounting it in quick succession (confirmed via bisection: disabling just the effect's `set()` call after the Dexie read fixed it, disabling the whole component didn't reproduce it, and the same file passed cleanly twice on the pre-#614 baseline). `loadAll` now short-circuits when `status` is already `'loading'`/`'ready'`, collapsing what used to be dozens of concurrent redundant Dexie reads across a single test run down to effectively one — also just a legitimate efficiency fix on its own, independent of the test flakiness it happened to eliminate. |
| `shared/ui/textarea.tsx` (new), `DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx` (#417) | Reported live: a longer day note stayed on one horizontally-scrolling row in the plain `Input` it used, hard to edit. New `Textarea` component (`shared/ui/`, same `forwardRef` shape/Tailwind classes as `Input`) auto-grows via a plain `onInput` height recalculation (`el.style.height = 'auto'`, then set to `scrollHeight`) rather than the newer CSS `field-sizing: content` property — this app is used on iOS Safari in practice, where that property isn't reliably supported yet, so a JS-based resize is the one approach guaranteed to work everywhere. Wired into the Note field's edit-mode JSX in place of `Input`; the field's old Enter-submits `onKeyDown` handler was removed entirely — Enter now inserts a newline (the textarea default) instead of saving, since fighting normal multi-line text editing with an Enter-to-save shortcut would be surprising once the field actually supports multiple lines. Saving still only happens via the existing checkmark Save button. Display-mode rendering (already `min-h-12`-wrapping since #189) is unchanged. **#449**: reported live — a previous day's already-long note stayed collapsed at the 1-row minimum height until the user typed something, instead of showing fully grown from the start. Root cause: the component's own `ref` callback called `resize(el)` (reading `scrollHeight`) *before* forwarding to the caller's `ref` — for an uncontrolled field, react-hook-form's `register()`-returned `ref` is what actually writes the field's initial value onto the DOM element (no React `value` prop does it), so the very first resize always measured an empty textarea regardless of the real loaded content. Fixed by forwarding the ref first, then resizing. |
| `DailyEntryFormMorning.tsx` (new), `DailyEntryFormStateContext.tsx` (new), `dailyEntryFormStateContextValue.ts` (new), `useDailyEntryFormStateContext.ts` (new), `DailyEntryFormTop.tsx`, `DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx`, `DailyEntryForm.tsx`, `TodayScreen.tsx` (#419) | User directly corrected #415 — the actual ask was moving the Morning entries group (Weight/Sleep/Body measurements/Body composition), not the BMI card, to sit right after the Goal target card; confirmed scope via `AskUserQuestion` (just Morning entries move; #415's BMI move stays as its own small unrelated improvement). This needed a further split beyond #416/#418's `DailyEntryFormTop`/`DailyEntryFormBottom`: the Morning group now renders in a completely different part of `TodayScreen.tsx`'s tree (right after the Goal card, *before* BMI/the deltas/the whole reorderable stat-card group/the banners) from the rest of the form (Meals/Water/Custom Metrics/Evening, further down, unchanged in position) — two non-adjacent render spots that still need to read/write the *same* live `useDailyEntryFormState` instance, which plain prop-passing can't do across intervening unrelated JSX. New `DailyEntryFormMorning.tsx` holds the extracted Morning group JSX (identical content, just no longer bundled with Meals/Water); `DailyEntryFormTop.tsx` narrowed to Meals + Water only. A small `DailyEntryFormStateContext` (split across 3 files — provider component, the plain context object, and the consumer hook — since `react-refresh/only-export-components` flags a file exporting both a component and a plain function, same reasoning `app/lazyRoutes.ts` already documents for its own split) replaces the `{ state }` prop every sub-component used to take; `DailyEntryFormMorning`/`DailyEntryFormTop`/`DailyEntryFormBottom` all read state via `useDailyEntryFormStateContext()` instead. `DailyEntryForm.tsx` (the combined default, History's `EntryRow.tsx`) wraps all three in one `DailyEntryFormStateProvider` and renders them back to back, unchanged in behavior. `TodayScreen.tsx` gates its *entire* Goal-card-onward block (Morning group, BMI, deltas, the reorderable group, banners, and the rest of the form) on `entryStatus` having resolved, not just the Morning slot — caught by 2 failing `TodayScreen.test.tsx` tests (`findByText('80 kg')` timing out) before the fix: `useDailyEntryFormState`'s `initialValues`/`entryIdentity` are memoized once on mount (`[]` deps), so the provider must not mount until `existingEntry` is the real loaded value, the same guarantee a single bottom-of-page loading gate used to give it before the form was split across non-adjacent locations. The `key={date}` that used to sit on the whole form now sits on the provider specifically, remounting everything nested inside it (including BMI/deltas/the reorderable group/banners, which don't themselves need a reset) on every date change — confirmed harmless, since none of that content holds meaningful state of its own beyond what's already derived fresh from props/stores each render. `tsc -b`/lint/daily-log suite (380 tests)/history suite (90 tests)/full suite (143 files, 1505 tests) green. **#427**: reported live with a screenshot — Body composition's edit mode has 5 sub-fields (unlike Sleep's 2/Body measurements' 2), which don't fit one `flex flex-wrap` row alongside the Save button; the wrap lands unevenly (2 then 3), stranding the button on its own third line. Direction resolved via `AskUserQuestion` (consistent grid over an icon-only-labels redesign): the 5 fields render in a fixed grid instead of `flex flex-wrap`. Landing on the Save button's exact position took 5 live-screenshot iterations — plain `grid-cols-2` left each column far wider than its narrow (`w-16`) field, stranding the button in the leftover space; `w-fit` fixed that but the button then sat on its own row below the grid; making it the grid's 6th cell aligned it with only the last field's row, not the whole block; centering it against all 3 rows landed near row 2 by coincidence (the geometric center of 3 equal rows) but wasn't explicitly *aligned to* row 2. Final layout: `grid-cols-[auto_auto_auto]` with each field explicitly placed via `col-start-*`/`row-start-*` (not relying on auto-flow, which would've placed the button right after field 5 instead of at row 2), Save button explicitly at `col-start-3 row-start-2 self-end` — bottom-aligning it within row 2's cell lands its top border flush with that row's own input top border (confirmed via a Playwright `boundingBox()` measurement, exact 0px difference), since the button (`size-12`) and each input (`h-12`) are both exactly 48px tall. Sleep/Body measurements' own `flex flex-wrap` layout is untouched, since neither has this problem. Confirmed on-device; a regression test was added afterward asserting the Save button carries the exact `col-start-3`/`row-start-2`/`self-end` classes the placement depends on (jsdom can't verify real rendered position, but this guards against a future refactor silently reverting to grid auto-flow, the original bug). **#424**: Save/Delete-only exits were also missing on the daily-log fields themselves (Weight/Sleep/Body measurements/Steps/Body composition — reported live from Body composition, scope widened to all 5 via a comment), same gap `MealList.tsx`'s #169 `cancelEditMeal()` already solved for meals. Each field gained its own `cancelEditX()` in `useDailyEntryFormState.ts` — reverts to `initialValues` (memoized once on mount), clears errors, and (Body composition only) dismisses the pending #401 unusual-value warning too — wired to a new ghost `X` button (`icon-xl`, matching each field's own 48px row height rather than `MealList`'s shorter `icon-sm`). Gated on a new `canCancelXEdit` flag (true whenever that field/group already has a saved value, or the form is `alwaysEditable`) — a field with nothing saved yet auto-opens in edit mode with no display-mode render to safely fall back to, so Cancel stays hidden there rather than flipping to a broken display render. Body composition's grid (#427, above) gained a 4th column at Save's own row for Cancel, rather than restructuring the already-hard-won layout — verified via Playwright `boundingBox()` that Save's position is pixel-identical to before and Cancel sits directly beside it (same row, 24px gap). **#437**: two more fields had the identical gap — the day note (its own `DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx`/`Textarea` edit-mode, #417, not part of the 5-field group above) and the custom-metric per-day note (`MetricValueRow`, #364, a completely separate component with local `useState`, not react-hook-form). Day note: new `cancelEditNote()`/`canCancelNoteEdit` in `useDailyEntryFormState.ts`, same `initialValues`-revert shape the other 5 fields use. Custom-metric note: its own local `cancelEditNote()` (reverts the local `noteDraft` state back to the `note` prop, since this component has no `initialValues`/react-hook-form concept at all) in `CustomMetricLogSection.tsx`. Both reuse the identical ghost-`X`-button visual treatment, gated on there already being a saved note to revert to. Two separate Dictionary keys (`dailyEntry.cancelEditNoteLabel`/`customMetrics.cancelEditNoteLabel`) rather than one shared one, since the two features don't otherwise share Dictionary sections. **#619**: the custom-metric note's Cancel button was still hidden for a brand-new note with nothing saved yet — the one case #437 deliberately left ungated in `CustomMetricLogSection.tsx`, since back then there was no display-mode render to fall back to. Removed the `Boolean(note)` guard; `cancelEditNote()` already reverted `noteDraft` to `note ?? ''`. Scoped to custom-metric notes only, matching the issue as filed — the day note's equivalent gap (`canCancelNoteEdit` in `useDailyEntryFormState.ts`) is untouched. **#620**: reported live right after confirming #619 on-device — canceling a fresh note fell through to the *existing* read-mode branch (empty `<span>` + "Edit note" pencil), which looked like a blank note had actually been saved, since that branch was written assuming it only ever shows real saved content. `MetricValueRow`'s non-editing render is now three-way instead of two: editing, a real saved note (`note` truthy — unchanged read-mode box), or a new idle "+ Add note" ghost button (`t.customMetrics.addNoteLabel`) when nothing was ever saved — a genuine "nothing logged yet" state instead of reusing read-mode's empty-string rendering. Initial auto-open-when-empty behavior (#364 reopened) is unchanged; the new idle state is only reachable via Cancel. **#436**: every field validator in this hook (`saveWeight`/`saveNote`/`saveSleep`/`saveSteps`/`saveBodyMeasurements`/`saveBodyComposition`/`validateBodyCompositionFieldOnBlur`, #435) set its error `message` straight from zod's own `safeParse` issue text — always raw, untranslated English, unlike every other user-facing string in the app. Fixed by replacing all 13 call sites with one new shared `t.dailyEntry.invalidValueMessage` ("Invalid value."/"Неверное значение."), resolved directly as the simplest of 3 options put to the user (plain translated wording, over per-field bounds copy mentioning each field's exact numeric limits, or a shared generic non-field-specific message) — fewer strings to write/maintain than field-specific bounds copy, at the cost of the message not stating the actual allowed range. **#446**: reported live — Body composition's grid (#427) misaligned when editing a previous day, first suspected as related to #424's new 4th Cancel column. Initial investigation (English locale) couldn't reproduce it at all. User pushed back with 2 more live screenshots proving it reproduces with perfectly ordinary values, and — critically — even on a brand-new empty entry with *no* Cancel button rendered at all, ruling out #424 as the actual cause. **Real root cause**: none of the 5 field labels (Muscle mass/Visceral fat/Body water/Bone mass/Body fat) have any height constraint of their own — each label+input pair is an independent `flex-col`, so whenever one row's Russian label text wraps to 2 lines while its sibling in the same row doesn't (their English equivalents are short enough to never wrap, which is exactly why the first English-locale test missed this), that column's input gets pushed down relative to its neighbor, since nothing keeps sibling flex-cols aligned to each other. Fixed by giving all 5 label `<span>`s a shared `min-h-8` (32px, the height 2 wrapped lines take at this `text-xs` size) — reserves the same vertical space regardless of whether a given label actually wraps, so every row's inputs now start at the same Y. Confirmed live via a Russian-locale Playwright script measuring each input's own Y coordinate before/after (previously mismatched whenever exactly one label in a row wrapped; now identical). **#447**: reported live with 2 screenshots — all 5 Body composition fields filled with wildly out-of-range values, Save visibly disabled-looking, yet the invalid values showed as saved after a refresh. The Save button was never actually programmatically `disabled` (just muted-looking via its `outline` variant), and #435's on-blur handler doesn't call `persist()` either — the real bug: *every* save handler in this hook calls `persist(getValues())`, and a bare `getValues()` returns every registered field at once, not just the one actually being saved. Typing garbage into Body composition, then saving a *different* field (Weight/Sleep/Steps/Note/etc.), silently smuggled the still-invalid Body composition draft through, since its own validation only ever runs inside `saveBodyComposition()`. Fixed with one shared `sanitizeForPersist()` step inside `persist()` itself — the single choke point every handler already goes through — falling back to `initialValues` for any schema-backed field that currently fails its own zod schema, leaving the field actually being saved untouched (already validated by its own handler). New regression test confirmed to genuinely fail without the fix (reverted it temporarily to check) and pass with it. **#622**: reported live right after #620 shipped — canceling a brand-new custom-metric note worked in the moment, but the editor "kept re-appearing." Root cause: `MetricValueRow`'s initial `isEditingNote` state was computed purely from `!note`, and a canceled draft leaves `note` at exactly the same `undefined` a never-touched entry has — so any remount (date navigation away and back re-creates the row since the parent's `key` includes `date`; the section's own Radix `Collapsible` unmounts its content by default when closed) recomputed `isEditingNote` as `true` again, with no way to tell "never touched" apart from "explicitly dismissed." Fixed with a new tiny store, `stores/customMetricNoteDismissalStore.ts` (persisted `Record<'${metricId}:${date}', true>`, same `persist`/`localStorage` shape as `todaySectionsCollapseStore.ts`) — `cancelEditNote()` (and `saveNote()`, when the draft trims to empty, since the store's own `setEntryNote` already normalizes a blank string to `undefined`, the identical end state as Cancel) marks the pair dismissed before the editor closes; the initial-state check became `!note && !noteDismissed`. Living outside the per-row component state means it survives exactly the remounts that broke it, without needing to write a placeholder value into the actual note data. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#169) | Save/Delete were the only ways out of a meal's edit mode — an accidentally-opened edit state, or a changed mind mid-edit, had no way back without committing or destroying something. New `cancelEditMeal()` (just clears `editingMealId`/`openEditItemId`; `editItems`/`editGroup*` are local staging state already overwritten fresh by `startEditMeal` next time, so nothing else needs resetting) wired to a new ghost `X` button in the edit header, next to Delete. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#153) | Both places "Find food" and "+ Add item" sit side by side (the bottom add row, and an existing meal's edit-mode row, #124) had manual entry first and "Find food" as a same-weight afterthought below it (#106). Reversed on both: "Find food" is now a full-width `size="lg"` primary button (`h-12 w-full text-base`, same treatment as #158's Save) rendered first, then the "or" divider, then "+ Add item" shrunk to a plain `ghost`/`sm` link-style trigger (the add row's own was previously `outline`/`h-12 flex-1` — now unstyled down to match the edit-row's own "+ Add item", which didn't change size, just position) — reasoning: search first reduces free-typed names ending up inconsistent with the curated catalog (see #150), and matches which action most users take first. The add row's own group-note input ("Anything else about this meal?") moved to *after* both CTAs — it used to sit between "+ Add item" and "Find food", interrupting the scan path between the two ways to add a dish. The `addAmountPreview` clear button (#151) shrunk from `icon-xl` to `icon-sm` to match the now-smaller trigger it sits next to. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#158, revisits #146) | #146's fix only changed the meal-group edit header's Save button from `variant="ghost"` to `variant="outline"` — still a small `icon-sm` checkmark-only button next to the name input, which the reporter clarified still didn't read as "the button that saves everything in this card." Replaced with a full-width, text-labeled `size="lg"` Save button (`h-12 w-full text-base`, default/filled variant) at the *bottom* of the edit card, after every field — same size/prominence `MealItemEditorSheet` already uses for its own Save. The header row now only has the name input + Delete; the `Check` icon import (now unused) was removed. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#151) | The collapsed "+ Add item" trigger (the compact button that opens `MealItemEditorSheet`, #122) gained a clear (`X`) button next to it, shown only once a staged draft has a valid amount (`addAmountPreview > 0`, the same condition that already made the trigger show a name/macros preview instead of the placeholder). Before this, discarding an in-progress item draft required reopening the full sheet and erasing every field by hand. New `resetItemDraft()` factors out the per-item field reset (name, amount, macros, mode, emotion) that `addMeal()`'s post-save reset already did — the clear button and the post-add reset now share it; `addMeal()` still separately resets the group-level note/time fields, unrelated to this button. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#600) | Short-lived undo toast on top of (not instead of) the existing two-step confirm before a meal delete commits. A `role="status"` bar (same visual weight as the existing `fastingWindowToastHours` note above it) shows `t.dailyEntry.mealDeletedToastMessage` + an Undo button; `undoDeleteMeal()` re-splices the snapshot back at its original index. Auto-clears after `MEAL_DELETE_UNDO_WINDOW_MS` (9s, inside the issue's 8-10s ask) via a `useEffect`/`setTimeout` keyed on the snapshot object, same transient-confirmation shape `GoalForm.tsx`'s `justSaved` established — deleting a second meal while the toast is still up replaces the snapshot (and restarts the timer) rather than stacking two undos. Settings' Clear-all-data stays out of scope, unrelated destructive-action category with its own permanent two-step confirm. |
| `mealDraftStorage.ts` (#221) | `loadMealDraft<T>(date)`/`saveMealDraft<T>(date, draft)`/`clearMealDraft(date)` — thin, best-effort localStorage wrapper (try/catch around every call; quota errors or private-browsing restrictions just mean draft recovery silently doesn't happen, not a thrown error) keyed by `` `turtle-steps-meal-draft:${date}` ``. Generic over the stored shape so it stays decoupled from `MealList.tsx`'s own `AddRowDraft`/`EditItemDraft` types. Resolved a genuine scope fork via `AskUserQuestion` first — the issue itself flagged "which fields, when a draft becomes real" as undecided; user chose recovering the add-row's in-progress dish specifically (the riskiest single spot: most typing, easiest to back out of accidentally), not a wholesale removal of the explicit-Save-button pattern used everywhere else on this form (#31), and not a broader multi-field draft mechanism. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#221) | New `AddRowDraft` interface bundles everything the add-row holds pre-save: `itemName`/`amount`/`protein`/`fat`/`carbs`/`amountG`/`macroMode`/`itemEmotion` (the current in-progress item) plus `stagedItems`/`groupNote`/`time` (the rest of the not-yet-saved meal group). A lazy `useState(() => loadMealDraft<AddRowDraft>(date))` reads once on mount (this component remounts on date change via its parent's `key={date}`, so there's no need to react to `date` changing afterward) and seeds every add-`*` field's own initial value from it instead of a blank default. A single `useEffect` (deps: every add-`*` piece of state) persists the current values on every change, and calls `clearMealDraft` instead once every draftable field is back to blank — `addMeal()`'s own existing post-save reset (`resetItemDraft()`, `setAddStagedItems([])`, etc.) already drives that same effect to the blank branch, so no separate explicit clear call was needed at the save site itself. `addAmountG`/`addMacroMode` are deliberately excluded from the "is this blank" check since they always carry a non-empty default (`'1'`/`'per100g'`) even with nothing actually typed. **Test-suite fallout**: several existing test files (`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`, `MealEditScreen.test.tsx`, `history/DayDetail.test.tsx`) reuse the same fixture date (`2026-03-01`) across many tests without ever clearing `localStorage` between them — harmless before this, but a persisted-by-default add-row draft meant one test's half-typed, never-saved item could leak into the next test's fresh render for the same date. Fixed by adding `localStorage.clear()` to each file's own `beforeEach`/`afterEach`, same pattern already used there for IndexedDB. |
| `MealList.tsx`, `MealEditScreen.tsx` (new), `router.tsx`, `lazyRoutes.ts` (#157; **#461 removed the route**) | Reversed #145's "keep editing inline" decision — a meal's pencil navigated to a dedicated route (`/entry/:date/meal/:mealId`, `MealEditScreen.tsx`) instead of expanding inline. `MealList` gained `date`/`focusMealId`/`focusMealPosition`/`onFocusedMealDone` props to drive that focused mount; `MealEditScreen` fetched the day via `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getByDate`, passed a single-element `calorieEntries` array, and `navigate(-1)` on done. **#461** deleted the route, `MealEditScreen`, and those focus props: after #459 the screen only ever rendered a portalled `AddMealDialog`, leaving `<main>` empty and depending on standalone-iOS-PWA history to remount Today (blank until tap). Pencil now opens `AddMealDialog` as a state-controlled overlay inside `MealList` (`editingMealId`), same as add-new-meal — Today/History never unmount. |
| `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` | Quantity-based entry against the static food list (#62): a Radix Dialog (`shared/ui/dialog.tsx`, #55) with a search input filtering `src/data/foods.ts` by the current locale's name, a result list, and a grams-quantity field that appears once a food is picked. Each list row shows a muted per-100g kcal/macros subtitle (#75, `macrosSummaryTextCompact`) so the numbers can be sanity-checked before picking, not just after. The list originally had **no independent scroll region** (#74) — it grew to full content height and let `DialogContent`'s own scroll handle it — but #275 reintroduced a bounded one specifically here; see below. On confirm, scales the food's per-100g kcal/protein/fat/carbs by `quantity / 100` and hands the computed numbers plus the food's localized name back to `DailyEntryForm` via an `onAdd` callback — `addFoodEntry()` there adds it as a new single-item meal group (`CalorieEntry { items: [{ name, amountKcal, ... }] }`, #81), same as the manual add row's default behavior. Does **not** call `touchMealItem` — the food list is a separate, curated reference source, not the user's own meal-name history (`useMealItemStore`), so picking a food doesn't pollute that autocomplete library. **Lazily mounted in `DailyEntryForm`** (`{isFoodPickerOpen && <FoodPickerDialog .../>}`, #78) — with the list at 300+ items, rendering it unconditionally (even closed) was measurably slow under full-test-suite parallel load; only mounting it while open keeps the closed case free. **#86**: search now also merges in `mealItems` (a new required prop, passed down from `DailyEntryForm`) — one place to find anything ever added, not just the curated database. Only `MealItem`s with a recorded `lastAmountKcal` are included (nothing to reuse otherwise); a `PickableItem` discriminated union (`{source: 'food', food}` \| `{source: 'mealItem', mealItem}`) drives both the merged result list and `handleAdd()`'s branching — curated foods still scale by quantity, personal items reuse their last-logged absolute kcal/macros as-is (no quantity field shown for them) and are visually distinguished by a "last logged" subtitle instead of "per 100g". **#90**: before searching, `foods.ts` is passed through `applyFoodOverrides()` (reads `useFoodOverrideStore`, loaded on mount) so a hidden item never appears here and a corrected item shows its overridden numbers — the dialog has no idea overrides exist beyond that one call. **#92**: the selected row's highlight is `border-2 border-primary bg-primary/15` (not `bg-muted`) — same dark-mode contrast fix as #84's `EmotionPicker`. **#91**: the quantity field + Add button originally sat in a `sticky bottom-0` footer (`bg-card`, `border-t`) rather than flowing after the list — with 300+ results and no independent list scroll (#74), the confirm action used to sit below the entire list, off-screen until scrolled all the way past it. **#275**: that `position: sticky` footer had a real, confirmed rendering bug — reproduced live via Playwright (full-page *and* element-scoped screenshots, ruling out a screenshot artifact), isolated to `position: sticky` specifically interacting with `DialogContent`'s `fixed` + `overflow-y-auto` (list rows past the sticky element's own clipped bounds stayed visible underneath it). Several standard mitigations were tried and *failed* to fix it while keeping the footer sticky: `isolation: isolate` + z-index, `transform: translateZ(0)`, `contain: paint` on the scroll container, and matching `MealItemEditorSheet`'s own edge-to-edge negative-margin footer treatment. The fix instead removes `position: sticky` entirely: the list (`<ul>`) reverses #74 and gets its own bounded `max-h-72 overflow-y-auto` region (≈6 rows, matching #74's own "first ~6 visible rows" sizing) — this footer is now a plain flex child, unconditionally visible below that bounded list with no positioning trickery at all. A JS-computed `position: fixed` footer (recalculated via a resize/scroll listener) also fixed it in testing but was rejected as a fragile, more complex alternative — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`. **#276**: either source can now be favorited via a star toggle per row (`FoodOverride.favorite?`/`MealItem.favorite?`, both purely additive) — `useFoodOverrideStore.setFavorite`/`useMealItemStore.toggleFavorite`, mirroring the existing hide/delete action shapes. `sortFavoritesFirst` (a stable sort applied *after* `rankBySearchMatch`) puts favorites first while preserving the existing exact/whole-word/substring ranking within each favorite/non-favorite group — so a favorite still surfaces first specifically among a query's own matches, not just first overall. **#96**: `onAdd` now also carries the `amountG` the totals were computed from — the picked quantity for a curated food, or the reused item's own `lastAmountG` for a personal one (may be `undefined`) — so `DailyEntryForm`'s `addFoodEntry()` can store it on the created `CalorieItem`, letting that item later be edited the same per-100g + quantity way a manually-entered one can. **#340**: reported live — a personal item just logged minutes earlier was buried mid-list under its own alphabetical position. `allMealItems` now sorts by `MealItem.updatedAt` descending (most recently touched first) before merging with curated foods, instead of inheriting `mealItemRepository.getAll()`'s alphabetical order — that repository call itself is unchanged (Settings' own management list still wants alphabetical). Curated foods have no per-user recency signal (`touchMealItem` deliberately never fires for one, see `MealList.tsx`'s `curatedFoodNames` note above) so they're unaffected; `sortFavoritesFirst` still applies on top, unchanged. **#341**: `PickedFoodValues` gained an optional `fiberG` — a curated food's own `food.fiber100` (currently always `undefined`, see `data/foods.ts`'s own note) or a personal item's `mealItem.lastFiberG` via `ratesFromAbsolute`'s now-optional 6th parameter, scaled by quantity the same way protein/fat/carbs already are in `scaledValuesFor`. |
| `mealItemStore.ts`, `MealItemsSection.tsx`, `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`, `MealList.tsx` (#279) | Follow-up to #276 — favoriting was only reachable from `FoodPickerDialog`'s own star. `useMealItemStore.touch` gained an optional third `favorite?: boolean` argument (omitted preserves whatever the item already had, so every pre-#279 call site is unaffected); `MealItemsSection.tsx`'s `MealItemRow` (the Settings saved-dishes list from the user's own screenshot) gained the identical star `Button` #276 already used in `FoodPickerDialog`, wired straight to the existing `toggleFavorite(id)` action, and its `AddMealItemForm` (create-a-new-dish-in-Settings) gained its own star checkbox feeding `touch`'s new argument. `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx` (the manual "Add dish" sheet reached via "+ Add item" during daily logging) gained a `favorite`/`onFavoriteChange` prop pair and a star button next to the Dish name label, shown unconditionally for both the add-row and existing-item-edit contexts — threaded through `EditItemDraft.favorite`/`AddRowDraft.itemFavorite` in `MealList.tsx` the same way #129's `emotion` field was. **Safety detail**: since favorite isn't a `CalorieItem` field (it belongs to the reusable `MealItem`/`FoodOverride`, not one day's logged instance) and `itemDraftFrom` always seeds an *existing* item's draft with `favorite: false` regardless of its real stored status, every `touchMealItem(...)` call site passes `draft.favorite || undefined` rather than the raw boolean — this can only ever force favorite *on*, never silently force it back *off* on an edit save where the star was simply never touched. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#288) | Follow-up to #256 — the barcode scanner was only wired into the bottom add-row, not an existing meal's own edit mode. New parent-owned `scanBarcodeIntoEditItems(barcode)`: same `lookupBarcode` local-first/Open-Food-Facts flow as the add-row's `handleBarcodeScanned`, but since editing an existing meal has no single "current draft" the way the add row's own fields do, a scan always creates a brand new `blankItemDraft()`-based `EditItemDraft` (prefilled on a match, barcode-only on a miss) and appends it to `editItems`, then hands back its id so the caller can open that draft's editor sheet — mirroring "+ Add item"'s own `onOpenEditItem(onAddEditItem())` shape exactly, just async. New `MealListItemProps.onScanBarcode` prop; the scanner dialog's own open/not-found state (`isBarcodeScannerOpen`/`barcodeNotFoundMessage`) stays local to `MealListItem`, same "not lifted higher" precedent `isFoodPickerOpen` already established there for #124. A new "Scan barcode — Meal N" ghost button sits after "+ Add item", same fallback-tier ordering as the add row's own scan button. **#300**: `scanBarcodeIntoEditItems` appending straight to `editItems` the instant a lookup resolves meant closing that item's own editor sheet via its X (rather than clicking Save) still left the draft staged — the meal's own overall Save then committed it anyway, even though X was meant to cancel it. Fixed in the sheet's `onOpenChange` (the X/Escape/backdrop path, distinct from the separate `onSave`/`onSaveAndAddAnother` click handlers): if the closing draft is both new (`isOpenDraftNew`) and scan-populated (`barcode !== undefined`), `onRemoveEditItem` runs instead of just closing. "+ Add item"'s own blank draft is unaffected (no `barcode`, so it keeps the original close-only behavior — harmless either way, since a blank draft already silently drops out of `draftsToItems` at save time). |
| `MealList.tsx`, `DayDetail.tsx`, `DailyEntryFormTop.tsx`, `normalizeTextSpaces.ts`, `myFitnessPalParser.ts` (#302, #545, #555, #559) | A logged item's view-mode row (name — kcal · amount · macros · reaction emoji) was one continuous inline text run inside a single element, in two near-identical places: `MealList.tsx`'s own item `<li>` and `DayDetail.tsx`'s duplicated copy (History/calendar-day panel). Reported live as macros wrapping unpredictably depending on content length, not scanned-item-specific. Split into two `<p>` elements per item in both files: name/kcal/amount on the first, macros + emotion on a second, always-own-row paragraph — the same "own `<p>`" pattern the meal-group-level macros summary one level up already used. No existing test asserted the old combined-string shape, so nothing else needed changing. **#545**: long dish names (e.g. imported Level Kitchen titles) overflowed the meal card instead of wrapping — flex items default `min-width: auto` (same class #156 fixed for truncate). View-mode dish name/`ul`/`li` and the meal card gained `min-w-0` + `break-words` on the name; `DayDetail` matched. **#555**: `break-words` mid-broke ordinary Russian words on WebKit (e.g. картофе\|лем, д\|жемом) — dropped it, kept `min-w-0` so names wrap at spaces. **#559**: dish `ul` still had `pl-4` (stair-step indent) — removed. Live DevTools then showed the real wrap bug: MyFitnessPal / pasted Level Kitchen titles store **NBSP (`U+00A0`)** between words, so under `break-normal` the whole Russian phrase is one unbreakable run (overflow / clip / mid-word break if `break-words` is forced). Fix: shared `normalizeTextSpaces` on MFP import (`cellToString` + Foods `description`/`brand_name`), Day/History display, and save paths (`AddMealDialog`, `mealItemStore`); meal card/dish list also use CSS `grid grid-cols-1` (`minmax(0,1fr)`); dropped `overflow-hidden`/`w-0` band-aids. |
| `BarcodeScannerDialog.tsx` (#291, #292, #294, #564) | Live-reported gaps in the same component, fixed together as they arrived. **#294**: most of a scan's perceived wait is actually the camera-decode phase itself (before a barcode is even found), which had no feedback of its own. `BrowserMultiFormatReader` is now constructed with a `DecodeHintType.POSSIBLE_FORMATS` hint restricting it to UPC-A/UPC-E/EAN-13/EAN-8 (Open Food Facts' actual barcode formats) rather than the ~18 symbologies (including 2D formats like QR/Aztec/PDF417) it checks by default — less decode work per frame. A framing-box overlay (`aspect-[5/2]`) now sits over the live camera feed as a purely visual scan-target guide (decoding still scans the full frame, unrestricted by region). A "still scanning" tip appears after a fixed 4-second delay if nothing's decoded yet. **#564**: framing box is tappable — `focusVideoTrackAtPoint.ts` applies `pointsOfInterest`/`focusMode` when supported (mainly Android Chrome), continuous AF on start, reticle + copy; quiet no-op where unsupported. **#292**: the dialog previously called `onOpenChange(false)` the instant `@zxing/browser` decoded a barcode, without waiting for the caller's async `onScanned` (the `lookupBarcode` call, #256) — the entire gap between "decoded" and "lookup resolved" had zero visible feedback, reading as "nothing happened." `onScanned`'s type widened to `(barcode) => void \| Promise<void>`; the dialog now `await`s it (showing a "Searching for this product…" message meanwhile) before closing, and stops the camera's decode loop immediately on a first successful decode (`controlsRef.current?.stop()`) so a stray second callback can't fire mid-lookup. Any throw from `onScanned` is caught and `console.error`'d rather than becoming an unhandled rejection. **#291**: reported as "fails on a different iPhone, not reproducible on my own" — rather than guessing at a camera-specific root cause, shipped the two requested mitigations. A manual barcode-number entry (text input + Search button) is now always visible alongside the camera view (and still shown if the camera errors) — same `onScanned` path either way, so it doubles as a diagnostic (manual entry working isolates a future report to camera/decoding, not `lookupBarcode`). The camera-open `catch` block previously discarded the real error; `scanBarcodeCameraErrorMessage` is now `(detail?: string) => string`, appending the caught error's `.name` (e.g. "NotAllowedError") to the existing on-screen message — already user-facing copy a report can quote directly, no new logging infrastructure. |
| `MealItemsSection.tsx` (#290) | Follow-up to #289 (built minutes into using it) — clicking "Add food" revealed `AddMealItemForm` inline at the bottom of the saved-foods list, below however many items were already there, reading as if nothing happened until scrolled all the way down. Resolved a genuine shape fork via `AskUserQuestion` (full-screen dialog vs. a dedicated route) — dialog won, reusing the same `size="fullscreen"` `Dialog`/`DialogContent`/`DialogTitle` pattern `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`/`BarcodeScannerDialog.tsx` already established, rather than a new route for what's a fairly small form. `AddMealItemForm`'s own state/logic (macro scaling, favorite, barcode scan) is completely unchanged — only its JSX shell moved from an inline `<li>` to the dialog wrapper, and its field sizing grew from the cramped `h-7`/`h-8` inline-row treatment to the same `h-12`/2-column-grid layout `MealItemEditorSheet` uses. The "Add custom food" trigger button and the saved-items list stay mounted underneath (unlike the old inline reveal, hiding them made no sense once the form is a modal overlay). |
| `MealItemsSection.tsx` (#289) | Follow-up to #256 — the barcode scanner was only wired into the daily-log add-row, not Settings' own "Add custom food" form. `AddMealItemForm` gained an icon-only "Scan barcode" `Button` next to the favorite star, a `handleBarcodeScanned` handler mirroring `MealList.tsx`'s own (local match via `lookupBarcode` prefills name/rate via `ratesFromAbsolute`, an Open Food Facts match prefills directly, no match shows a quiet muted message), and a lazily-mounted `BarcodeScannerDialog`. `onAdd`'s signature grew a 4th `barcode: string \| undefined` parameter, passed straight through to `touch`'s existing 4th argument (#256). `BarcodeScannerDialog`/`lookupBarcode`/`BarcodeLookupResult` moved from being `daily-log`-internal to also being exported from that feature's barrel (`daily-log/index.ts`) for this cross-feature reuse — same precedent `EmotionPicker` already established there for #172's History reuse — rather than reaching in via a relative import path. |

Both `DailyEntryForm.tsx` call sites (`addFoodEntry()` for the add row, `addFoodToEditItems()` for an existing meal) set it straight onto the created item/draft. |
| `FoodPickerDialog.tsx`, `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`, `MealList.tsx` (#183) | Multi-add: checking off several dishes in one Find-food session, or entering several manual dishes in one "+ Add item" session, lands them all in the same meal group instead of requiring the full add-cycle once per dish. `FoodPickerDialog`'s single `selected: PickableItem \| null` became `selectedKeys: Set<string>` — checked state deliberately survives a search-text change (the whole point of checking off several dishes), so `selectedItems`/`canAdd` are derived from an unfiltered `allItems` list, not the search-filtered `matches` list used only for rendering. Quantity/reaction fields only render while exactly one dish is checked (`singleSelected`) — matches the pre-#183 single-pick UX exactly, no regression there; a multi-pick defaults to 100g/no reaction, fine-tunable afterward via that item's own pencil. `onAdd`'s signature changed from a single `PickedFoodValues` object to `PickedFoodValues[]` (new exported type, was inlined before) — called once per confirm with the whole batch, not once per item. `MealItemEditorSheet` gained an optional `onSaveAndAddAnother?: () => void` — when passed, a second outline "Save and add one more" button renders below Save (both `disabled={!hasValidAmount}`). `MealListItem` only passes it while the open draft is genuinely new (`!entry.items.some(i => i.id === openDraft.id)`, i.e. added via "+ Add item" this session) — editing an already-existing dish has no "add one more" to offer, so `onSaveAndAddAnother` stays `undefined` there and the button doesn't render. For an existing meal's own edit mode, wiring the button was simple (`() => onOpenEditItem(onAddEditItem())`, reusing the exact expression the "+ Add item" trigger itself already used) since `editItems` was already a real staging array. The bottom "add a new meal" row had no such concept before #183 — every manual item became its own brand-new meal record the instant Save was tapped, so picking several dishes there previously created several separate meal cards, not one grouped meal. Added `addStagedItems: EditItemDraft[]` (same shape/semantics as `editItems`) plus `currentAddDraft()` (the add row's live fields as one draft) and `stageAddItem()` ("Save and add one more" — stages the current draft, resets item fields, keeps group-level note/time and the sheet itself untouched). A new module-level `draftsToItems(drafts): CalorieItem[]` (factored out of `saveEditMeal`'s inline `flatMap`, which now just calls it) is shared by both `saveEditMeal` and the rewritten `addMeal()` — `addMeal()` now folds `[...addStagedItems, currentAddDraft()]` into one new `CalorieEntry`, dropping invalid/blank rows silently same as `saveEditMeal` always has. `addFoodEntry()` (the add row's own Find-food handler) similarly builds one new meal group with one item per checked dish, as a self-contained action — it still commits immediately with no extra confirm step (unchanged from pre-#183), so it doesn't fold into `addStagedItems`; combining a Find-food pick with a manually-typed dish in one group still works the same way it always has, by editing the meal afterward. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before implementing (checkbox-then-single-confirm vs. add-per-check; what "Save and add one more" resets; whether this applies to `MealEditScreen` too — it does, for free, since that screen reuses `MealList` unchanged). |
| `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` (#209) | A personal item's row (`item.source === 'mealItem'` only — the curated database isn't user-editable) now has its own `Trash2` delete button, wired to `useMealItemStore.deleteItem` — same immediate, no-confirm-step delete `settings/MealItemsSection.tsx` already used, reached from Settings only until now. `handleDeletePersonalItem` also drops the item from `selectedKeys` if it happened to be checked, same as anything else that stops existing mid-session. The dialog's own `mealItems` prop is a plain array passed down from `MealList.tsx`'s own `useMealItemStore` subscription, not read from the store directly inside the dialog — so the row itself only actually disappears once the parent re-renders with a fresh prop (the normal, already-existing flow whenever the store's `items` changes), not synchronously within the dialog. |
| `domain/mealItem/MealItem.ts`, `mealItemRepository.ts` (Dexie v9), `lookupBarcode.ts`, `BarcodeScannerDialog.tsx`, `MealList.tsx` (#256) | Barcode scanning — local-first lookup with an Open Food Facts fallback, confirmed with the user as a deliberate, explicit extension past `PROJECT_BRIEF.md` §2's "no live third-party sync" line (same precedent as #62/#78/#83 already extending "no food database"). `MealItem.barcode?: string` (purely additive) gets a sparse unique secondary index (`mealItems: 'id, &name, &barcode'`) — like the existing `&name` index, records with no barcode simply never enter it, so no collision between items that don't have one. `MealItemRepository.findByBarcode`/`useMealItemStore.touch`'s new 4th `barcode?` argument (same "omit to preserve, only #256 callers pass it" shape as #279's `favorite` argument) round out the write path. `lookupBarcode.ts` (pure, fully unit-tested): checks `findByBarcode` first (instant, fully offline — the only thing that matters for a *repeat* scan of the same barcode), then — only while online (`useOnlineStatus()`, skipped entirely rather than waiting out a doomed request) — a plain client-side fetch to Open Food Facts' public product-by-barcode endpoint, parsed defensively (any missing product name or kcal figure, the two things this app can't do without, falls through to "no match" same as a genuine miss). `BarcodeScannerDialog.tsx` lazy-loads `@zxing/browser` (Safari has no native `BarcodeDetector`, and this app prioritizes iOS) only once opened, same on-demand pattern `exceljs` already uses — and is itself lazily *mounted* by the caller (`{open && <BarcodeScannerDialog .../>}`) rather than always-rendered-with-`open`-toggling, so a fresh camera session and cleared error state come from the natural remount instead of an effect resetting state synchronously (which trips the React Compiler's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` gate, #159). Wired into `MealList.tsx`'s bottom add-row only (not the existing-meal-edit "Find food", a deliberate v1 scope trim) — a decoded barcode prefills the add-row's fields (local match, OFF match, or a quiet "no food found" note + blank form for a genuine miss, via a new optional `MealItemEditorSheet` `infoMessage` prop) and opens the item sheet for the user to review before saving; nothing is ever auto-saved. `EditItemDraft.barcode` threads through the same "not a CalorieItem field, read at save time via a draft-id lookup map" shape #279's `favorite` already established. The issue's own explicitly-flagged open question (attach a scan to an *existing* item instead of always creating new) was resolved by its own checklist marking that "follow-up scope, not required for v1" — not implemented here. **#531**: name search via legacy `/cgi/search.pl` behind an explicit Add-meal **Search online** button (never per-keystroke — OFF rate-limits search at 10/min); shared `openFoodFactsParse.ts`; `searchOpenFoodFacts.ts` caps 12 hits / 5s timeout / abort on query change; optional Na/K/Mg fields on `FoodItem`/`CalorieItem`/`MealItem` for #530. **#535**: `searchOnlineFoods` orchestrates bundled RU staples (`ruFoodGenerics.ts`) + OFF (now returns `ok`/`empty`/`unavailable`/`aborted` so 503 ≠ “no calories”) + USDA FoodData Central fallback (`searchUsdaFoods.ts`, Foundation/SR Legacy/FNDDS, `VITE_FDC_API_KEY` or `DEMO_KEY`). Offline Search online still runs the bundled list; UI shows a distinct unavailable message. |
| `exportBundleSchema.ts` (#284) | Found while implementing #256: #276 added `MealItem.favorite?`/`FoodOverride.favorite?` to the domain models but never added `favorite` to this schema's `mealItemSchema`/`foodOverrideSchema` — so a favorited item's status was silently dropped on every export/import round-trip. Fixed alongside adding `MealItem.barcode` to the same schema (#256) — both purely additive, no version bump. |
| `data/foods.ts`, `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` (#254) | Optional named serving sizes for a food where "how many grams" isn't obvious — new `FoodServing { en, ru, grams }` on `FoodItem.servings?`, seeded for 8 foods (egg: small/medium/large; bread white/whole-wheat: 1 slice; apple/banana/orange/potato: 1 medium; rice/pasta cooked: 1 cup), using standard USDA/FDA reference weights where one exists. Not attempted for the rest of the 342-item list; no descriptor support on personal `MealItem`s at the time (deferred, per the issue's own scoping — see #603 below). Surfaced only in `FoodPickerDialog.tsx`'s single-select footer, not the unrelated per-100g/per-portion toggle in `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx` the issue's own text pointed at — that toggle drives freeform manual entry with no link to a specific curated food, whereas a specific food (and its servings) is already selected right where this actually needed to go. A new `ToggleGroup` ("Grams" + each of that food's own descriptor labels) replaces the quantity field with a "how many" count once a descriptor is picked; `gramsFor(item)`/`activeServingFor(item)` resolve grams = `serving.grams * count` there, falling back to the plain quantity field otherwise (a food with no servings seeded, or more than one dish checked — a shared descriptor wouldn't mean the same thing across different foods). **#603**: extended to personal `MealItem`s — new `MealItemServing { en, ru, grams }` (`domain/mealItem/MealItem.ts`, deliberately a separate type from `FoodServing` rather than importing it, same "own type per owner" precedent `RecipeIngredient`/`CalorieItem` already established) on `MealItem.servings?`, editable via a new "Named servings" list + add-row in `MealItemsSection.tsx`'s `MealItemRow` nutrition editor — commits immediately through a new `useMealItemStore.setServings(id, servings)` action, independent of the row's own nutrition Save/Cancel, same "doesn't wait for Save" shape favoriting already has there. The editor stores one user-typed label in both `en`/`ru` (no bilingual-authoring UX asked for). `FoodPickerDialog.tsx`'s `activeServingFor`/the serving `ToggleGroup`'s gating dropped their `item.source === 'food'` restriction in favor of a new `servingsFor(item)` helper reading either source's `servings` — `scaledValuesFor`'s existing personal-item branch (`ratesFromAbsolute` → scale by `grams/100`) needed no change, since it was already agnostic to how `gramsFor(item)` arrived at its number. `exportBundleSchema.ts`'s `mealItemSchema` gained an optional `servings` array, no version bump — same purely-additive precedent as every other optional `MealItem` field; `exportActions.ts` needed no change since meal items are already upserted as whole objects. |
| `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` (#264) | Quantity is now adjustable for *every* pick, not just a single checked curated food. Single shared `quantity` state replaced with a per-item `quantities: Record<string, string>` map (keyed same as `selectedKeys`), with `quantityFor`/`setQuantityFor` helpers and a `defaultQuantityFor` (100g for a curated food — its own per-100g reference amount; a personal item's own `lastAmountG` when it has one, else 100g too). A personal item (`source: 'mealItem'`) has no per-100g rate of its own to scale from, so `scaledValuesFor` first derives one via `ratesFromAbsolute` (the same helper the add row's own autocomplete-prefill already uses) before scaling by the new quantity — confirmed unchanged output when the quantity is left at its default (the rate round-trips back to the original logged numbers exactly). `canAdd` unified to "every selected item has its own valid positive quantity," replacing the old asymmetric single-vs-multi/food-vs-mealItem logic. UI: exactly one checked dish still gets one shared field in the sticky footer (now for either source); two or more each grow their own inline per-row field instead (`aria-label` includes the dish name to stay unique), since a single shared field can't say which dish it applies to once there's more than one. `PickedFoodValues.amountG` is now always populated (previously `undefined` for a personal item with no recorded quantity) — a deliberate side effect, not just this issue's own scope: any personal item picked through this dialog going forward carries a real quantity, making it fully quantity-editable later the same way a manually-entered item already is. |
| `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`, `shared/lib/macroScaling.ts`, `AddMealDialog.tsx`, `MealList.tsx` (#457) | #121 replaced the per-100g/portion toggle's shared weight field with a non-interactive "Portion" badge while in Portion mode (an editable multiplier there would have been wrong — Portion mode's typed total isn't scaled by it). Restored as a real, optional field there instead (`itemWeightLabel`, "Weight (g)", vs. per-100g mode's own `itemPortionsLabel`, "× 100g") — the domain math never needed to change: `CalorieItem` only ever stores absolute totals + `amountG`, and `ratesFromAbsolute()` already back-calculates a per-100g rate from those on demand (re-opening an item for edit, an autocomplete restore), so a recorded Portion-mode weight already flows into that same reconstruction for free. **Real bug found while wiring this up**: the field holds a genuinely different unit per mode (a portions *count* in per-100g mode vs. real grams in Portion mode) that the mode-switch handlers (`changeManualDraftMode` in `AddMealDialog.tsx`, `updateEditItemMode` in `MealList.tsx`) never converted between — harmless before #457 since the field was never editable in Portion mode, but switching Portion→per-100g ran the value through `portionsToGrams()` regardless, multiplying an already-real-grams number by 100 again (150g misread as "150 portions" → 15,000g → a wrong 3 kcal/100g instead of the correct 300). Fixed with a new `gramsToPortions()` (the inverse of the existing `portionsToGrams()`) and moving the conversion outside the "nothing typed yet" early-return guard in both handlers, so setting the weight *before* the amount (a normal order) still converts correctly. Deliberately scoped to just this one entry point — `RecipeEditorDialog.tsx` and `MealItemsSection.tsx` (Settings' food database editor) have their own independent copies of the identical toggle UI/gap, left untouched. |
| `domain/dailyEntry/DailyEntry.ts`, `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`, `MealList.tsx`, `exportXlsx.ts` (#248) | New `CalorieItem.brand?: string` — purely cosmetic, independent of the reusable `MealItem` name library (still keyed on `name` alone; picking a suggestion or a food from `FoodPickerDialog` never pre-fills a brand). `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx` gained a plain `Input` field right after Dish name. Threaded through every existing `name`-shaped path in `MealList.tsx` the same way #129's `emotion` field was: `EditItemDraft`/`AddRowDraft`/`blankItemDraft`/`itemDraftFrom`/`draftsToItems`/`currentAddDraft`/`resetItemDraft`/`updateEditItemField`'s field union, plus the #221 add-row draft-recovery persistence. Displayed as `Name (Brand)` everywhere a dish name already renders (the edit-mode compact row, the read-only view-mode item list). Also added as its own column in the Excel export's "Meals" sheet, between Item and Calories — the CSV/Markdown exports don't break individual meal items out at all (day-level totals only), so nothing to change there; the JSON backup carries it automatically as a plain `CalorieItem` field, no schema-version bump needed (same "purely additive/optional" precedent as `amountG`/`emotion`). |
| `MealList.tsx`, `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx` (#260) | New optional `MealItemEditorSheet` prop `todayTotalPreview?: string` — a second preview line ("Today would be: 1,850 kcal (was 1,550)") shown next to the existing per-item `totalPreview` (#98), only while a valid draft amount exists. Only wired up for the bottom "add a new meal" flow (`totalCalories(calorieEntries) + draft's amountKcal`, safe since nothing about a not-yet-saved meal is reflected in `calorieEntries` yet) — deliberately **not** passed for `MealListItem`'s existing-meal edit sheet, where the correct number is a whole-meal delta (`calorieEntries` still carries that meal's *old* total until the outer Save commits the replacement) rather than a simple sum; left as a harder follow-up rather than shown wrong. **#273** extends the same preview to `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` (new optional `todayTotalKcal?: number` prop, same add-row-only scope boundary) — summed over every currently-checked item's own `scaledValuesFor(item).amountKcal` (#264) rather than a single draft's, covering the multi-select case in the same calculation. **#278** extends both call sites' preview strings from kcal-only to also include protein/fat/carbs: `FoodPickerDialog`'s `todayTotalKcal?: number` prop was replaced with a bundled `todayTotals?: { kcal, proteinG, fatG, carbsG }` object, and its `selectedTotals` reduction now sums all four fields (via `scaledValuesFor`) instead of just kcal; `MealList.tsx`'s own `todayTotalPreview` (feeding `MealItemEditorSheet`) similarly folds in `totalProtein`/`totalFat`/`totalCarbs`. Both now append `macrosSummaryTextCompact(...)` (#75's existing compact "P/F/C" formatter) to each half of the existing `todayWouldBeLabel(new, previous)` string, rather than introducing a new Dictionary key or sentence shape — missing macro data is treated as 0 in the projection (a projection is always a real number, unlike a display value that would show "—"). **#280**: the sheet's footer (Save/"Save and add one more", #91) was `position: sticky` — reproduced live via Playwright measurements and confirmed it overlapped the last ~20px of the scrollable fields region above it (rather than sitting flush below), clipping the bottom of the Reaction row (`EmotionPicker`, the last field before the footer) right where it met the footer. Same root cause #275 already found for `FoodPickerDialog`'s own sticky footer — `position: sticky` doesn't behave correctly inside `DialogContent`'s `fixed` + `overflow-y-auto` wrapper. Fixed the same way: removed `sticky`/`bottom-0`, relying on the scroll region's own bounded height (`flex-1 overflow-y-auto`) to keep the footer flush below it with no overlap. **#399**: reported live — user wanted to see remaining calories (against the daily target) while adding food via barcode scan, Find food, or manual entry, not just after saving. Investigation found two of the three already partway there: `todayTotalPreview`/`todayTotals` (#260/#273/#278 above) already showed a running-total preview in both `MealItemEditorSheet` (covers manual entry *and* barcode scan, which prefills this same sheet's fields — #256's own scan path) and `FoodPickerDialog` — just an absolute total, not remaining-against-target. New sibling `todayRemainingWouldBeLabel` Dictionary entry, computed the identical way as the existing preview (`dailyCalorieTargetKcal - projectedTotal`, no clamping/positive-only treatment — deliberately simpler than `TodayScreen.tsx`'s own signed-delta/"over target" StatCard, appropriate for this smaller secondary preview surface) and rendered as its own sibling `<p>` line, shown only when a goal's `dailyCalorieTargetKcal` is set. New `MealList.tsx`/`MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`/`FoodPickerDialog.tsx` prop (`dailyCalorieTargetKcal?`/`todayRemainingPreview?`), read via `useGoalStore` inside `DailyEntryForm.tsx`/`DayDetail.tsx` (both already have the goal loaded by an ancestor — `TodayScreen.tsx`'s own load, or `HistoryScreen.tsx`'s `useHistoryData()` — so no new load call needed) and threaded down through `MealList`. `MealEditScreen.tsx` (the dedicated single-meal edit route) deliberately not wired — its `focusMealId` mode hides the add-row entirely, so the feature has nothing to attach to there. New tests cover the preview appearing/absent on both surfaces; verified live via Playwright against a real seeded goal + entry. |
| `data/foods.ts` | `foods: FoodItem[]` (342 items, #62 then expanded #78, #83) — `{ id, en, ru, kcal100, protein100, fat100, carbs100 }`. Combines four public sources: a RIA.ru calorie table (RU-cuisine: meat, fish, dairy, Russian breads/sausages/confectionery, beverages), a FitForFilms macros PDF (UK/international, raw weights — 100g rows only, "average portion" columns dropped), `health-diet.ru`'s prepared-dish tables (#83, RU-cuisine composite dishes: stews, vareniki, cutlets, jams), and USDA FNDDS (#83, "as consumed" prepared/composite dishes: pizza, burgers, pasta, curries, sandwiches). Not a domain entity or repository-backed concern (plain static content, like `releaseNotes.ts`), shipped values not directly user-editable but overridable per-device via `foodOverrides` (#90), not synced with backups. Near-duplicate entries between sources were collapsed to one; dried herbs/spices (2g portions, not meaningfully trackable at 100g) and a niche probiotic-food section were skipped. |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` | A native `<input type="date">` (capped at today via `max`) drives which date's entry is loaded/edited. Shows "This week's target" `StatCard` (via `useCurrentWeekInfo`) or an `EmptyState` pointing at `/goal` when no goal exists. A second `StatCard` shows the day-over-day weight delta (#42, via `usePreviousDayEntry`) with asymmetric emphasis — bold for a loss, muted for a gain/no-change, matching #29's treatment. **#100**: a third `StatCard` shows progress vs. the highest weight ever recorded (`entry.weightKg - maxWeightKg`, via the new `useMaxRecordedWeight(refreshKey)` hook in `shared/hooks/` — scans `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getAll()` for the max `weightKg` on every `refreshKey` change, `entry` is passed as the key so a freshly-saved all-time high is picked up immediately), same asymmetric emphasis (bold below the max, muted when exactly at it — the delta can never be positive since the viewed day's own weight is included in the max scan). A quiet end-of-week banner (#38) nudges toward `/goal` on the last day of the ISO week, no dismiss state. Renders `<GoalCelebrationModal />` (#55) at the top, which fires independently of the #38 banner. **#171**: a second, independent quiet banner — gated by `useDailyReminderStore.enabled` (off by default), shown only while `date === todayIso()` and `entry === null` (loading settled, nothing logged yet) — reads "No entry yet today — whenever you're ready," no action link, no dismiss state, same tone as the #38 banner. Deliberately an in-app-only note, not a real push notification: the app has no backend to schedule a push from, and a notification-style alert would cut against the app's existing no-pressure design ethos (no streaks/badges, #14/#20/#29). `key={date}` on `DailyEntryForm` forces a clean remount per date. **#126**: the Date input is `h-12` (bumped from `h-11` by #139), matching the standalone-field height established below. **#138**: `ChevronLeft`/`ChevronRight` `icon-xl` buttons flank the Date input — `shiftDate(date, ±1)` (`date-fns` `addDays`, negative for "previous") steps `date` state by one day, quicker than opening the native picker to check yesterday/tomorrow. The right arrow is `disabled` once `date >= todayIso()`, mirroring the date input's own `max` — logging a future day isn't supported anywhere in the app today, deliberately out of scope here. No lower bound on the left arrow (same as the date input itself has none) — arrowing back into an empty far-past day just shows an empty form, same as picking one manually already does. **#208**: a fourth `StatCard`, "Remaining calories," only rendered once the active goal has `dailyCalorieTargetKcal` set — `goal.dailyCalorieTargetKcal - (totalCalories(entry?.calorieEntries) ?? 0)`, unlogged calories reading as 0 consumed (not "unknown") so it fills in as the day goes rather than needing a full day's log to show anything. The card's own `value` is always the absolute difference; `unit` supplies "kcal remaining" or "kcal over" depending on sign, deliberately not a signed number (`+`/`-`) the way the weight-delta cards use, since "remaining" reading negative would be confusing on its own. **#418**: reported live with 2 screenshots — BMI, the vs-yesterday/vs-max-weight deltas, and the entire reorderable card group (#343) formed one long uninterrupted wall of stat cards between the Goal target card/Morning entries (#419) above and Meals/Water/Custom Metrics/Evening entries below. Scope confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (all of them belong in one collapsible block; the Goal target card and Morning entries stay outside it as fixed content; the collapsed/expanded choice persists across visits). Wrapped in a new `Collapsible`/`CollapsibleTrigger`/`CollapsibleContent` (`shared/ui/collapsible.tsx`, a thin `radix-ui` `Collapsible` re-export, same style as `toggle-group.tsx`/`dialog.tsx`'s own primitive wrappers) driven by a new persisted `useTodayStatsCollapseStore` (`collapsed: boolean`, default `false`/expanded — a first-time user sees the same layout as before this existed). The trigger is a single full-width row (`t.today.statsSectionLabel`, "Stats") with a `ChevronDown` that rotates via `group-data-[state=open]:rotate-180` (Radix sets `data-state` on the trigger itself; the icon reads its *parent's* state through Tailwind's `group-data-*` variant, same mechanism `label.tsx`'s own `group-data-[disabled=true]` already uses) — `aria-label` swaps between `expandStatsLabel`/`collapseStatsLabel` based on the current state. Banners (target-met/goal-renewal/daily-reminder) stay outside the collapsible too, unaffected — they're prompts, not "stat cards." No change to `sectionVisible`'s existing per-card hide/show toggles or to `todayCardOrderStore`'s drag order — a card already hidden via Settings still doesn't render at all; the accordion only affects whether the still-visible ones are collapsed as a group. **#420**: reported live — the date-navigator row's Previous/Next arrows (`icon-xl`, 48px) and Date `Input` (explicit `h-12`) sit next to the jump-to-today button (#403), which used `size="sm"` (`button.tsx`, 28px) with nothing overriding it — a visibly shorter button in an otherwise-48px row. Fixed with an explicit `h-12` on that button's own `className` (`sm`'s `h-7` loses the conflict via `cn()`'s `twMerge`, same override mechanism the Date `Input` itself already relies on). The user also asked for "same row → same height" to become a standing convention rather than a one-off fix — documented as a comment directly above `button.tsx`'s `size` variants (see `shared/ui/button.tsx` below) rather than in this file, since it needs to be visible at the exact point a future size gets chosen, not filed away in an architecture doc nobody reads mid-edit. **#421**: reported live right after validating #418 — the trigger button had its own `rounded-lg border border-border` box, while the `StatCard`s inside `CollapsibleContent` had their own independent card styling with nothing wrapping the two together, reading as two detached floating pieces rather than one cohesive expand/collapse block. Fixed by moving the border to a new outer `<div className="rounded-lg border border-border p-3">` wrapping the whole `Collapsible` (trigger and content both), removing the border classes the trigger `<button>` used to carry on its own — same grouped-section treatment `DailyEntryFormMorning`/`DailyEntryFormBottom` already use. **#420 reopened**: reported live again — the Date `Input` itself now renders taller than the (correctly 48px) arrows/jump-to-today button. A second attempt (`max-h-12` + inline `line-height`) measured at exactly 48px for all 4 elements via Playwright `boundingBox()` in both Chromium and WebKit — but the user confirmed on their actual iPhone it was still broken, a real WebKit-on-Windows-vs-real-iOS-Safari false negative, not a fixed bug. Third attempt: dropped the `h-12 max-h-12` Tailwind classes for an explicit inline `style={{ height: '3rem', maxHeight: '3rem' }}` plus `overflow-hidden` on the `Input` — `overflow-hidden` is the actual new lever, forcibly clipping the rendered box to 48px regardless of what the native `<input type="date">` control's own internal chrome wants to render at, rather than only requesting 48px via `height`/`max-height` and hoping the browser honors it exactly (apparently not reliable on real iOS Safari). Not re-verified via Playwright given this exact bug's own history of false negatives there — pending on-device confirmation before considered fixed. Fourth attempt moved the fixed height + `overflow-hidden` off the `<input>` itself onto a plain wrapping `<div>` — confirmed live that the clip *did* take effect this time (unlike attempt 3), but what it clipped was the input's own real rendered right/bottom border, not invisible overflow: the native control genuinely paints larger than 48px in both dimensions, so no box that size can hold it without cutting into its own chrome. Fifth attempt, per the user's own suggestion: stopped constraining the input's height entirely — it now renders at its true natural size (measured on-device, uncapped, at 42px — actually *shorter* than the row's 48px, not taller) — and instead shrunk the arrows/jump-to-today button to match it (`size-[2.625rem]`/`h-[2.625rem]`, 42px, scoped to just this row's `Button` overrides, not the shared `icon-xl`/48px class other Today rows like Weight/Sleep still use). Same "match the native control instead of fighting it" principle #139 established, just in the direction this control's own size actually needed. Confirmed fixed on-device. **#465** (#420 follow-up): reported live — the Date input's *width*, not height, grew once the "Сегодня" button was gone (viewing today's own date), since the input was the row's only non-`shrink-0` element, absorbing whatever flex space a missing sibling freed up. On-device measurement: 179px with the button present, 214px without — 214 exceeds `max-w-48`'s own 192px cap, meaning that constraint wasn't actually binding on the native control at all. First fix attempt (a fixed-width wrapping `<div>` + `overflow-hidden`, mirroring #420's own wrapper-div height fix) hit the identical failure mode live — the native control paints wider than whatever box it's given, so the clip cut into its own real right border again. Fixed at the actual source instead: the "Сегодня" `Button` is now always rendered (previously conditionally mounted via `date !== todayIso()`), hidden via `invisible`/`aria-hidden`/`disabled`/`tabIndex={-1}` rather than unmounted when viewing today — keeps the row's layout footprint, and therefore the Date input's available flex space, constant regardless of the viewed date, so the input's natural width never changes and needs no explicit constraint at all. `aria-hidden` keeps the always-rendered-but-hidden button correctly excluded from testing-library role queries. Confirmed fixed on-device. |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` (#220) | A fifth `StatCard`, "Remaining protein," same shape as #208's calories card and only rendered once `dailyProteinTargetG` is set — `totalProtein(entry?.calorieEntries) ?? 0` subtracted from the target. Unlike calories, `Math.max(0, ...)` clamps the value at 0 rather than reading "over" once met — more protein than planned isn't the same "went over budget" concept a calorie ceiling is, so once the target's hit it just reads "0g remaining," no second unit string needed for a surplus state. |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` (#235) | Reported live: "no notification when logging today's weight, only visible on the Goal page." Investigated by writing a real RTL test replicating the exact flow (goal active, baseline day already logged, then a live weight save crossing the target) — `GoalCelebrationModal` (#55) fired correctly, so the reactive chain (`useActiveGoalProgress` re-fetching on every `useDailyEntryStore` save) wasn't actually broken. Root issue instead: the modal is one-time and dismissible (outside-tap, Escape) with no second chance to notice it — exactly the gap a live report like this would produce even with working code. Added a persistent complement: `useActiveGoalProgress()` now also drives a `showTargetMetBanner`, same quiet always-visible shape as the existing #38 renewal-reminder banner just below it, with a "Review goal" link — stays visible for the rest of the goal window regardless of whether the modal moment was seen. |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` (#200) | The viewed `date` moved from local `useState(todayIso)` to a `?date=` URL search param (`useSearchParams`, `setSearchParams(..., { replace: true })` so browsing days via the arrows doesn't spam the history stack with one entry per click). Originally needed because a meal's pencil navigated away to `MealEditScreen` (#157) and `navigate(-1)` remounted Today from scratch — local state didn't survive. **#461** removed that route (edit is now an in-place overlay), but the `?date=` param stays: History deep-links and reloads still benefit. The param is omitted entirely when the viewed date is today, keeping the common-case URL plain `/`. |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` (#239) | The Date row (arrows + native date input) moved from below all the `StatCard`s to right under `PageHeader`, first thing on the page after the title. Reported as "jumping" — the `PageHeader` title is a fixed "Today" string that never changes, but the actual selected date further down the page did as you paged between days, several stat cards apart from the title. Considered replacing the title itself with the date instead (the issue's own alternate phrasing) but didn't: every other screen's header is a fixed screen name via the same `PageHeader`, and making this one page's title dynamic would be a bigger, more disruptive departure from that pattern than moving an existing block up — fixing the position solves the reported "jumping" either way. |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` (#499, #503) | Date navigator block (label + arrows / date input / jump-to-today, plus the rare #345 start-today-early banner) is `sticky` under AppShell's #25 header so day context and day-switching stay reachable while scrolling meals/stats. `top-[calc(2.75rem+1px)] sm:top-[calc(3.5rem+1px)]` matches the header's mobile (app-name only) vs sm+ (desktop nav) heights + border; `z-10 bg-background border-b` with `-mx-4 px-4` so scrolling content doesn't show through and the opaque strip bleeds to the main column edges. #465's always-rendered (sometimes `invisible`) Today button layout unchanged. **#503**: changing day via prev/next/picker/Today used to scroll to the top — `loadEntry` flips status to `'loading'`, and the form gate replaced the whole block with a short "Loading…" line, collapsing page height so the browser clamped scroll. Fix: `setDate` captures `window.scrollY` and a `useLayoutEffect` restores it once the new day is ready; the loading placeholder also keeps the previous form area's `minHeight` so the clamp doesn't fire mid-switch. Navigator-driven only (`pendingScrollYRef` stays null for History `?date=` deep-links / first paint). |
| `TodayScreen.tsx`, `AppShell.tsx`, `i18n/en.ts`/`ru.ts` (#466) | User-facing rename of the screen from "Today"/"Сегодня" to "Day"/"День" (nav tab + `PageHeader` title + date-neutralized subtitle). #239 had already kept the title fixed rather than date-tracking; once date-navigation (#138/#403) made browsing past days common, the fixed "Today" copy read as wrong on those days. Routes, `TodayScreen` file/component names, and jump-to-today button copy (`jumpToTodayButton`) stay unchanged — copy-only. Bottom-nav `Home` icon left as-is (open question in the issue). |
| `TodayScreen.tsx` (#233) | Two new `StatCard`s, right after "Remaining protein": **BMI** (`calculateBmi`, `domain/stats`) once `useProfileStore`'s `heightCm` and the viewed day's `entry.weightKg` both exist — age/sex not needed for this one. **BMR** (`calculateBmr`) additionally needs `age` and `sex` set too; renders `t.today.bmrUnit` ("kcal/day") next to the number, unlike BMI which is unitless. Neither is stored — both recomputed on every render straight from `profileStore` + the currently-viewed entry, same "derived, not persisted" approach as the day-over-day/vs-highest-weight cards above. Each independently hideable via #232's `sectionVisibilityStore` (`todayBmi`/`todayBmr`, same `action`-slot/`sectionTitle` collapse pattern as every other card here). |
| `MealList.tsx` (#190, previewed #202) | "Repeat yesterday's [meal]" quick action on the add row — a new `Button` (`variant="outline"`, above "Find food") only rendered when `previousMeal` is found: own `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository` instance (same no-shared-store pattern as `MealEditScreen`/`useHistoryData`) fetches the day before `date` in a `useEffect`, and `previousMeal = previousDayEntry?.calorieEntries?.[calorieEntries.length]` — the meal at the *same 0-indexed position* the about-to-be-added meal will occupy, matching #141's existing positional-identity convention (Breakfast = 1st meal) rather than fuzzy label matching.
