Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before building (a genuine 3-way design fork — this quick action vs. saved meal templates vs. copy-from-History, flagged in #190 itself): the quick action won over the other two as the smallest, lowest-risk option that still solves the reported "eats the same breakfast daily" case directly. Touches `useMealItemStore` for each named item exactly like `addMeal()`/`saveEditMeal()` already do, skipping curated food names (#150). "Yesterday" is relative to `date` (whichever day this `MealList` instance represents), not literally today — so History's past-day editing gets a correctly-relative "day before" too, not today's real yesterday. The new direct-repository fetch needed a `.catch()` (best-effort, same pattern as `usePastGoals`/`useMaxRecordedWeight`) — without it, `EntryRow.test.tsx`/`DayDetail.test.tsx` (no `fake-indexeddb` loaded, since they never needed real IndexedDB before this) threw unhandled promise rejections even though every assertion still passed. **#202**: the button no longer commits immediately (`repeatPreviousMeal()`, the original all-or-nothing clone) — it opens `RepeatMealDialog` (below) instead; the actual clone-with-fresh-ids logic moved to `repeatSelectedItems(selected: CalorieItem[])`, called with only the dialog's checked subset once confirmed. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before building (modal dialog vs. an inline checklist expansion) — the modal won for reusing #183's existing checkbox-then-confirm pattern (`FoodPickerDialog` already established it) rather than introducing a second, inline variant of the same idea. |
| `RepeatMealDialog.tsx` (#202) | Preview + selective picking for "Repeat yesterday's [meal]" — #190 shipped that as the only add-path in the app with no confirmation step. Same checkbox-list-then-confirm shape as `FoodPickerDialog`'s multi-select (#183): every dish in `previousMeal.items` starts checked (`uncheckedIds: Set<string>`, empty by default — matches the old all-or-nothing behavior unless something is deliberately unchecked), each row shows the dish's name + kcal/macros preview (`macrosSummaryTextCompact`), and the confirm button reuses `addSelectedFoodsButton(n)`'s existing copy ("Add selected" / "Add selected (N)"). Deliberately no search or quantity/emotion editing here — those already exist during the actual edit-after-add flow, this dialog's only job is picking *which* of yesterday's already-fixed dishes to bring over. `onConfirm` hands back the checked subset of the *original* `CalorieItem[]`; `MealList.tsx` still owns the fresh-id/drop-emotion cloning (`repeatSelectedItems`), same separation of concerns as `FoodPickerDialog`'s `onAdd`. |
| `CopyDayMealsDialog.tsx` (new), `MealList.tsx` (#253) | Whole-day sibling of `RepeatMealDialog` above — "Copy yesterday's meals" (`copyYesterdayMealsLabel`), shown whenever the source day has any meal with at least one item (`previousDayMealGroups`), independent of the single-position matching `previousMeal`/the per-meal "Repeat" button use and available regardless of how many meals today already has. Same checkbox-then-confirm shape, extended over every meal group instead of one: each group renders its own `effectiveMealLabel` header above its own item checklist, `uncheckedIds: Set<string>` tracked globally across all groups (ids are unique), and `onConfirm` hands back `{label, items}[]` with any group left with nothing checked dropped entirely. `MealList.tsx`'s `copyDaySelectedGroups` mirrors `repeatSelectedItems` over several groups at once — fresh ids, dropped emotion, one `setCalorieEntries` call appending all new groups together, same food-item-library `touchMealItem` calls. Only meal/food data is copied (name, macros, `amountG`) — weight, sleep, steps, note, mood, time-eaten, and per-item reactions are not, same reasoning #190 used for a single meal. Deliberately hardcoded to "the day immediately before" (matching #190's own `previousDayEntry`), not an explicit day-picker — a scope note left on the issue flagged that as a possible future refinement, not required now, since opening the action from any day in History already covers picking a different source day. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#199, redesigned #201) | The trailing "add a new meal" row (already hidden entirely when `focusMealId` is set, #157) can be collapsed behind a small "+ Add another meal" link — a "Collapse" ghost button at the bottom of the add-row card sets `isAddRowCollapsed`. #199 shipped this as plain component state that reset on any remount, not just a new day — live use showed that included ones that aren't a new day at all (a `MealEditScreen` round trip, switching tabs and back), reading as broken rather than intentional. #201 splits the default by whether `date` is in the past (`isPastDay = date < format(new Date(), 'yyyy-MM-dd')`): a past day always starts collapsed (no persistence — a manual expansion there is local to that viewing session, `isPastDayExpanded` plain `useState`, same simplicity #199 originally wanted); *today* starts expanded, but a manual collapse now persists across navigation via a new `useAddMealRowCollapseStore` (`stores/addMealRowCollapseStore.ts`, localStorage, `{ collapsedDate: string \| null }` — a single date rather than a per-date map, since it only ever applies to today; a stale stored date simply reads as "not collapsed," which is exactly the "a new day starts fresh" reset the feature wants, no separate expiry logic needed). `collapseAddMealLabel`'s copy changed from "Done adding for today"/"Готово на сегодня" to the day-agnostic "Collapse"/"Свернуть", since the same button/label now also appears on a manually-expanded past day, where "for today" no longer applies. |
| `GoalCelebrationModal.tsx` | Intentional exception to the app's usual quiet, no-badges treatment of hitting a target (compare #6/#8/#29/#34) — a real `Dialog` (#55), not a StatCard note. Backed entirely by `useWeeklyGoalCelebration`; the CTA links to `/goal` and calls `dismiss()`, same as closing via the `X` button or clicking outside. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#357) | Reported live: the add row's group-level `timeEaten` was blank by default (reverses #82's own original "blank by default" decision), so most logged meals ended up with no time at all unless manually filled in. `addTime`'s initial `useState` (and both post-save resets in `addMeal()`/`addFoodEntry()`) now default to the current `HH:MM` instead of `''` — the manual clear (`X`) button is untouched, still resets to blank on request. `addTime` also needed to join the #221 add-row draft-recovery's `isBlank` exclusion list (alongside `addAmountG`/`addMacroMode`) — otherwise a populated-by-default time made the draft never read as "blank," so a fresh, nothing-yet-typed add row always persisted (and restored) a stale draft instead of starting clean. Already-logged meals with no time at all are backfilled separately — see `db.ts`'s v12 `.upgrade()` above. |
| `index.ts` | Barrel. |

#### `dashboard/` — Epic 5/6, issues #6/#7 (done)

Fully built; no longer a placeholder.

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `DashboardScreen.tsx`, `stores/dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` (new) | Loads data via `useDashboardData`; renders `WeightTrendChart`, `CalorieTrendChart`, `MacroTrendChart`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart` (#267), `CustomChartView`, `CorrelationView`, `LateMealCorrelationView`, `SleepCorrelationView`, `StepsCorrelationView`, `ProteinCorrelationView`, `FoodReactionsView`, `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap`, `RecentAveragesCards`, `WeeklySummaryCards`, `MonthlySummaryCards`, `CompareRangesView`, or an `EmptyState` with zero entries. **#297**: order is now drag-and-drop reorderable and persisted, not fixed — `dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` holds an ordered `DashboardChartKey[]` (reusing the same key type `dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts` already defined, rather than a second parallel list), with a `merge` function that reconciles a persisted order against the current section list on load (drops removed keys, appends new ones at the end, so a future new section can't silently fail to render). `DashboardScreen.tsx` builds a `Record<DashboardChartKey, ReactNode>` of every section's JSX, then renders `order.map(...)`, each wrapped in a new local `SortableDashboardSection` (`useSortable` + a small grip-icon drag handle rendered *above* the section) — same `DndContext`/`SortableContext`/`arrayMove` shape `MealList.tsx` already established for meal reordering, reused verbatim rather than re-invented. The drag handle lives at this wrapper level, not inside any individual chart/card component, specifically to avoid touching 15+ files each with their own already-established title layout from #232/#245/#247's show/hide toggle work. **#319**: the grip handle was always visible above every section, reported live as visual clutter. Reordering is now an on-demand mode instead of always-on: `DashboardScreen` holds a local `isReordering` `useState(false)`, surfaced via `PageHeader`'s new `action?: ReactNode` slot (previously unused anywhere) as a "✎ Reorder"/"Save" toggle button. `SortableDashboardSection` only renders its grip-handle button, and only passes `disabled: false` to `useSortable` (`disabled: !isReordering` otherwise), while the mode is active — outside it sections aren't draggable at all, not just visually tidy. #297's autosave-per-drop persistence is otherwise untouched; the toggle purely gates visibility/drag-ability, nothing is staged or deferred pending the "Save" click. **#356**: reported live — no way back to the original order short of manually re-dragging every section. `dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` gained a `resetOrder()` action (`set({ order: DEFAULT_DASHBOARD_SECTION_ORDER })`); a ghost "Reset order" button sits next to the Save button, shown only while `isReordering` is true. `TodayScreen.tsx`'s own `todayCardOrderStore.ts` (#343) got the identical `resetOrder()` action and button, same reasoning. No confirm step on either — a low-stakes, instantly re-draggable preference, not a destructive action. **#355**: reported live — the grip handle rendered on its own line above the whole section (title included), since `SortableDashboardSection` rendered it itself, stacked above whatever `children` happened to render first. Investigated whether this genuinely needed a per-file fix in all ~18 different section components, or one shared fix — found that every one of them already funnels its own title through the single shared `ChartTitleWithToggle`/`SectionTitleWithToggle` pair (#245/#247, see the Features section below), so the fix landed there instead: `SortableDashboardSection`'s `children` became a render-prop (`(dragHandle: ReactNode) => ReactNode` instead of a plain element — `sectionsByKey` became a map of functions accordingly), and `SectionTitleWithToggle` gained a new `dragHandle` slot rendered immediately before its own `<h2>`, inside the same flex row. Every one of the 18 view components picked up the identical one-line addition: a new optional `dragHandle?: ReactNode` prop, forwarded straight into that section's own existing `ChartTitleWithToggle` call — no per-file custom layout work needed. **#538**: initial Dashboard open still felt ~4–5s after #537 because every reorderable section (and custom correlations) mounted its Recharts tree at once. New `useNearViewport` + `DeferredMount` — first two sections eager, the rest mount when near the viewport (sticky; reorder mode forces all on). jsdom without IntersectionObserver still mounts everything so existing tests are unchanged. |
| `domain/stats/bodyCompositionTrend.ts`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx` (#267) | Surfaces #233's muscle mass/visceral fat/water/bone mass (plus body fat % once #263 moved it in) on the dashboard — previously captured on the daily entry form and never shown anywhere else. Scope resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: a dedicated always-there chart (matching `WeightTrendChart`'s pattern, gated by `trackedFields.bodyComposition`), not just registering them in the existing generic `customChartSeries.ts` picker (`bodyFatPercent` alone had gone that route already, as `NumericSeriesKey`'s `bodyFat`) — the dedicated chart directly solves "data disappears after entry" without requiring the user to go find and select these fields manually in "Compare your data". `bodyCompositionPoints()` reuses `customChartSeries.ts`'s exact per-series 0-100 normalization idea (kept as its own standalone function, not sharing that module's public `NumericSeriesKey` union, since this chart always plots a fixed 5-field set rather than a user-picked one) — necessary because the five span kg (muscle/bone), % (water/body fat), and an unscaled 1-59 rating (visceral fat), which would otherwise flatten the smaller-scale lines to invisible next to the larger ones on one raw-value Y-axis. Reuses the app's existing 5 chart-color CSS tokens (`--chart-protein/fat/weight/carbs/calories`) rather than defining new ones. New `dashboardChartVisibilityStore` key (`bodyComposition`) for the standard per-chart show/hide toggle. **#277**: a `ToggleGroup` series picker (same pattern as `CustomChartView`'s own picker) lets the user narrow the fixed 5-key set down, persisted via new `bodyCompositionSelectionStore` (`selected: BodyCompositionSeriesKey[]`, defaults to all 5 — unchanged behavior for anyone who's never touched the picker). With exactly 2 series selected, the chart switches from the shared normalized-0-100 axis to two real-value Y-axes (Recharts `yAxisId="left"`/`"right"`, plotting `${key}_raw` instead of `${key}_norm`) — no scale-clash risk with only two series on screen, and real numbers read better than an index. 1/3/4/5 selected keeps the existing normalized single-axis approach, just filtered to the chosen subset; 0 selected shows a "pick at least one" message instead of an empty chart. **#332**: those two real `YAxis` elements had no `domain` set, so Recharts' actual default (`[0, 'auto']`) forced both to start at 0 regardless of the series' real range — same root-cause bug as `CustomChartView.tsx`'s #330 dual axis, found and fixed in the same pass even though only the "Compare your data" chart was reported. Fixed identically: `domain={['auto', 'auto']}` on both axes. **#348**: reported live — exactly 1 series selected showed no y-axis at all, unlike the exactly-2 case above. The "no scale-clash risk" reasoning behind `dualAxis` applies just as well to a single series (nothing else on screen to confuse it with), so a new `singleAxis` case gets the identical real-axis treatment for one series instead of two — a single visible `YAxis` (`yAxisId="single"`, `domain={['auto', 'auto']}`, tick colored to that one series), plotting `${key}_raw`. 0/3/4/5 selected still falls through to the shared hidden normalized axis, unchanged. **#376**: reported live — a series with real values concentrated in only a narrow recent window (e.g. muscle mass) still stretched the x-axis across the full app-wide date range, since `bodyCompositionPoints()` returns one row per date with *any* of the 5 fields logged, and the chart's `data` used that full unfiltered set regardless of which series were actually selected. Fixed by narrowing `data` to `visiblePoints` (dates where at least one *currently-selected* series has a value), computed after the series picker's selection — deliberately left the existing `MIN_TREND_DATA_POINTS`/"not enough data" gate (based on the *full* dataset) untouched, since a selection choice narrowing the visible range isn't the same as too little data existing at all (an earlier attempt that conflated the two broke the existing "deselect one at a time" test, reverted). Confirmed on-device. |
| `useDashboardData.ts` | Direct-repository hook (not routed through `dailyEntryStore` — see the "known simplification" note above): fetches all entries via `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getAll()` plus the active goal via `useGoalStore`. |
| `WeightTrendChart.tsx` | Recharts `LineChart` of weight over time, unit-aware via `unitStore`. Tap/click on a point navigates to `/?date=...` (#41, retargeted from `/history?date=...` by #442 below) via `chartNavigation.ts`; no projection line (#46 removed the pace-projection overlay #6 had originally added). **#214**: a second `Line` overlays `domain/stats/rollingAverage.ts`'s 7-day rolling average (same fixed window `CalorieTrendChart.tsx` already uses for its own overlay, no window-size picker) — `var(--muted-foreground)`, dashed (`strokeDasharray="4 3"`), deliberately *not* a second solid `--chart-weight` line the way the calorie chart's average overlay is: that chart's average sits over a differently-colored Bar series with no clash, but here both series are literally the same metric, so two same-color solid lines would be hard to tell apart — the dash reads as "smoothed variant of the line next to it" instead. The special "current value" dot on the raw weight line now anchors to the last date that actually has a *logged weight* (`weightPoints`), not just the last row of the merged chart data — `rollingAverage()` returns a point for every entry date regardless of whether that day logged a weight, so the merged array can trail past the last real weight point with average-only rows. **#217**: below a local `MIN_TREND_DATA_POINTS = 3`, returns a plain "not enough data" message (`t.dashboard.notEnoughTrendDataMessage`) instead of the chart — a straight line connecting just 1-2 far-apart points can visually read as a confident trend that isn't real (verified by screenshot: 2 points 19 days apart drew a full-width line before this change). Zero points still renders nothing at all, unchanged. `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`/`MacroTrendChart.tsx` below have the identical constant/gate, same threshold and reasoning. **#378**: reported live across three screenshots — the x-axis `tickFormatter` used `'MMM d'` with no year, unreadable once ticks span multiple years (confirmed the tick sequences themselves were correctly chronological, just missing the year that would disambiguate them). All 5 charts share the identical pattern (this one, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `MacroTrendChart.tsx`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx` above, `CustomChartView.tsx` below) — the tooltip already used year-inclusive `'PP'`, only each `XAxis`'s own `tickFormatter` needed the change, to `'MMM d, yyyy'` (matching #373's chosen format for Weekly Summary's own date range). Confirmed on-device. **#397**: reported live — the literal `'MMM d, yyyy'` token order reads backwards in Russian ("мая 7, 2021" instead of day-first), since a hand-picked format string's token order is fixed regardless of locale, only the month *name* translates. All 5 charts (plus `CustomChartView.tsx`, and every week-range call site touched by #373/#392/`WeeklySummaryCards.tsx`/`PastTargetsList.tsx`/`GoalScreen.tsx`/`TodayScreen.tsx`) switched from the literal string to date-fns' own locale-aware `'PP'` preset — confirmed to produce byte-identical English output ("Jul 24, 2026") while correctly reordering for Russian ("24 июл. 2026 г.", already the exact tokens `WeightTrendChart.tsx`'s own tooltip has used since before this fix). `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap.tsx`'s per-row week label (`'MMM d'`, no year) has the identical order bug but was deliberately left unfixed here — it's a compact `text-[10px] whitespace-nowrap` label with no room for `'PP'`'s year suffix, needs its own locale-conditional short token rather than a blind swap; not reported live, noted here as a known follow-up. **#442**: reported live — this chart's own "Open this day" tooltip link still targeted `/history?date=X`, landing on History's read-only-by-default view, same gap #389 already fixed for `OutlierPointsList.tsx`'s equivalent link. Confirmed via grep that all 4 main trend charts (this one, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `MacroTrendChart.tsx`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`) duplicate the identical `to={\`/history?date=${date}\`}` line separately, not through a shared component — retargeted all 4 to `/?date=X` (Today's full editable `DailyEntryForm`). `viewDayLink`'s copy ("View this day"/"Открыть этот день") didn't explicitly reference "History" the way #389's old copy did, so it needed no rewording. **#441**: reported live with a screenshot — an obvious single-point data-entry-error spike rendered identically to every real point, no visual flag at all. Reuses `domain/stats/outlierDetection.ts`'s existing Tukey's-fences rule (#224) directly against the raw weight values (`outlierBounds`/`isOutlier` — a single dimension, unlike the X/Y-pair shape `flagOutliers` needs for the 6 correlation views that already use this module) — scoped to Weight only per the issue's own filed scope, not extended to the other 3 trend charts. The existing custom `dot` renderer (already special-cased for the current-value marker) gained a check ahead of that logic: any outlier point always renders as a `var(--destructive)` circle regardless of whether it's also the last-logged-value index. Verified live via a seeded Playwright script (9 IndexedDB-seeded days with one deliberate 74kg spike against a ~58-59kg baseline) rather than a unit test, since Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom — confirmed exactly one flagged circle at the spike's position. **#448**: confirmed live right after #441 that the statistical version worked, but the user wanted a deterministic, simpler rule instead — resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: flag a point if it's more than 2kg away from its own *immediately preceding calendar day's* logged weight (not the 7-day rolling average or the visible range's median), same "previous day" concept #401's `isUnusualWeightDeltaKg` warning already uses elsewhere. `outlierBounds`/`isOutlier` replaced with a `Map<date, weightKg>` (canonical kg, not the display-unit-converted `weight` array, so the 2kg threshold means the same thing regardless of kg/lb setting) — a date is flagged only when `date-fns` `subDays` lands on another date that's also in the map and the absolute delta exceeds 2kg; a day right after a logging gap has no adjacent-day entry to compare against and is never flagged, even if wildly different from the last real point before the gap. Verified live via a seeded Playwright script (a small day-to-day change correctly unflagged, a +2.5kg jump flagged, the following -2.4kg drop flagged, then a 2-day gap into a 90kg value correctly *not* flagged). |
| `CalorieTrendChart.tsx` | Recharts `ComposedChart`: daily calorie `Bar` + 7-day rolling-average `Line` overlay (via `rollingAverage()` with an extractor for `totalCalories()`). Same deep-link-on-tap pattern as the weight chart. **#217**: same `MIN_TREND_DATA_POINTS = 3` gate as `WeightTrendChart.tsx` above, checked against `calorieBars.length`. |
| `WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `stores/trendChartSeriesStore.ts` (#238) | Both charts' legends were purely decorative — no way to hide either series. New `useTrendChartSeriesStore` (persisted, same shape as `useCustomChartSelectionStore` but scoped to just these two fixed built-in charts rather than arbitrary series) tracks `visible.{weight,calories}.{raw,average}`, independent per chart. Each legend `<i>` became a `<button aria-pressed>` toggling its own series (dimmed via `opacity-50` when off); the `Line`/`Bar` elements render conditionally on the same flags. Turning both off on either chart shows `t.dashboard.trendChartEmptyDescription` ("Pick at least one series to show"), same pattern `CustomChartView.tsx` already uses for zero-selected-series. **Regression, caught live minutes after shipping**: the legend (with its toggle buttons) was originally inside the same early-return as the chart itself, so turning both series off made the *only way to turn them back on* disappear along with the chart — a self-locking dead end with no recovery path in the UI at all. Fixed by restructuring both components so the legend always renders regardless of a new local `bothHidden` flag; only the chart-vs-message swap is conditional now. Also added a matching `SettingsScreen.tsx` card ("Dashboard trend charts") duplicating these same four toggles as an always-reachable safety net independent of whatever state either chart's own legend is in — explicitly requested after the lockout. |
| `ChartTitleWithToggle.tsx`, `stores/dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts` (#245) | Follow-up correction to #238 — that was series-*within*-a-chart visibility; this is hiding the *entire chart*, explicitly requested as its own issue after #238 didn't cover it. New `useDashboardChartVisibilityStore` (persisted, `visible.{weight,calories,macros}`) plus a small shared `ChartTitleWithToggle` component (title + `Eye`/`EyeOff` icon button, `aria-pressed`) used by `WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, and `MacroTrendChart.tsx` — the latter already had its own `<h2>` title, the former two didn't (`t.dashboard.weightTrendTitle`/`calorieTrendTitle` added). Scoped to just these three recharts-based trend charts, not all 15 Dashboard sections — the general "dismiss any section" idea across Today/Dashboard/Goal is #232's separate, broader territory. Applying the #238 lockout lesson directly: the title+toggle always renders regardless of the chart's own visibility state (including the "not enough data" and zero-visible branches), so the control that brings a chart back can never itself be hidden. |
| `ChartTitleWithToggle.tsx`, `CustomChartView.tsx`, `CorrelationView.tsx`, `LateMealCorrelationView.tsx`, `SleepCorrelationView.tsx`, `StepsCorrelationView.tsx`, `ProteinCorrelationView.tsx`, `stores/dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts` (#247) | Extends #245's per-chart hide/show toggle to the 6 remaining Dashboard sections that didn't have it — the custom-chart series picker and each of the 5 correlation cards. `DashboardChartKey`/`DEFAULT_VISIBLE` grew 6 new keys (`customChart`, `calorieWeightCorrelation`, `lateMealCorrelation`, `sleepCorrelation`, `stepsCorrelation`, `proteinCorrelation`) — one toggle per card, not one for the whole correlation group, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (the screenshot that prompted this issue circled all 5 as one block, but that would have been a less granular, harder-to-undo choice than matching #245's existing per-chart precedent). Each correlation card already had its own internal "Show chart"/chevron toggle (#89/#116/#167/#216, gating just the scatter plot until there's a real insight) — a genuinely different concept from this whole-*card* visibility, same distinction #238 (within-chart series) vs. #245 (whole-chart) already established. `ChartTitleWithToggle` gained an optional `extraAction?: ReactNode` slot (rendered next to the eye button) so a correlation card's existing chevron button could sit alongside the new eye toggle in the same header row, without duplicating the eye-button/`aria-pressed` logic six more times. Applies the #238 lockout lesson everywhere: hiding a card collapses it down to just its title + toggle, same as #245. |
| `shared/ui/section-title-with-toggle.tsx`, `shared/ui/visibility-toggle-button.tsx`, `stores/dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts`, `stores/sectionVisibilityStore.ts`, `FoodReactionsView.tsx`, `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap.tsx`, `RecentAveragesCards.tsx`, `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx`, `MonthlySummaryCards.tsx`, `CompareRangesView.tsx`, `TodayScreen.tsx`, `GoalScreen.tsx`, `PastTargetsList.tsx` (#232) | Extends #245/#247's per-section hide/show toggle to every remaining section across all three pages, resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: reuse the eye-icon pattern (not the issue's originally-described corner ×), applied broadly (not just the 3 examples the reporting screenshot circled). **Dashboard**: `dashboardChartVisibilityStore` grew 6 more keys (`foodReactions`, `loggingConsistency`, `recentAverages`, `weeklySummary`, `monthlySummary`, `compareRanges`) — every Dashboard section now has the toggle, same wrap-the-existing-`<h2>` pattern as #247. **Refactor**: `ChartTitleWithToggle.tsx` is now a thin wrapper around a new store-agnostic `SectionTitleWithToggle` (`shared/ui/`, takes `visible`/`onToggle` as props instead of reading a specific store) — Today/Goal's own `sectionVisibilityStore` (separate store: different pages, different key set, same underlying idea) reuses that same component directly. **Today**: 8 sections (This week's target, vs. yesterday, vs. highest weight, remaining calories/protein, and the target-met/goal-renewal/daily-reminder banners) — deliberately excludes the raw input fields #237 already made toggleable (computed insight vs. raw input is the same line #237 already drew). A `StatCard`-based section (unlike a chart with its own `<h2>`) already shows its own label — wrapping it in a *second* title row would show the label twice, which the first implementation attempt actually did and broke `getByText` uniqueness throughout the existing test suite. Fixed by extracting `VisibilityToggleButton` (just the eye icon, no title) and giving `StatCard` a new optional `action?: ReactNode` slot next to its existing label — a visible section renders `<StatCard action={<VisibilityToggleButton/>}>`, a hidden one renders `SectionTitleWithToggle` alone (no `StatCard` at all) as the label-only placeholder. Three banners (`targetMetBanner`/`goalRenewalReminder`/`dailyReminderText`) are full sentences, not short labels — reusing them as the toggle's title text would duplicate a whole sentence, so each gained a short parallel `*SectionTitle` string instead (`targetMetSectionTitle` etc.) purely for the toggle row. **Goal**: 3 sections — the weekly-target `StatCard` (same `action`-slot pattern as Today's), the #155 target-reached nudge (own short `activeGoalReachedSectionTitle`, same reasoning as Today's banners), and `PastTargetsList.tsx`'s table (wrap pattern, like a Dashboard section). GoalForm.tsx's own #244 read-only summary table is deliberately **not** covered — it's the goal-setting control's own display state, not a supplementary insight, same category as Today's Weight/Sleep/Steps fields that #237 also left alone. **#361**: #355's `dragHandle` slot (below) added a `truncate` class to this title `<h2>` so a long title wouldn't push the eye-toggle/drag-handle row off-screen — reported live as titles now clipping with an ellipsis (worst on the longer Russian correlation titles). Removed `truncate` entirely: the parent's `min-w-0` flex container still shrinks the title to whatever width is available, so it now wraps onto a second line instead of being clipped, and the pre-#355 push-off-screen bug doesn't return since wrapping (not overflow) is what bounds the row. Verified live via a real Playwright screenshot against seeded IndexedDB data at very narrow viewports (180px/260px) — no ellipsis, eye-toggle stays put. |
| `MacroTrendChart.tsx` | Recharts `LineChart` (#53), three lines — protein/fat/carbs — on one combined chart rather than three separate ones (same unit, comparable scale, keeps the Dashboard from growing three more full-width charts). Uses new `--chart-protein`/`--chart-fat`/`--chart-carbs` CSS tokens (`src/index.css`) — unlike `--chart-weight` (mood-tinted), these follow `--chart-calories`'s pattern: one consistent hue family with a light/dark pair, constant across all 5 moods, rather than 5 separate mood-specific variants. Same deep-link-on-tap pattern as the other charts; a day only appears if at least one of the three macros was logged that day. **#217**: same gate as the other two trend charts, checked against the merged `data.length` (a day counts if it logged *any* of the three macros) — unlike the other two, the title (`t.dashboard.macrosTitle`) stays visible above the "not enough data" message rather than the whole section disappearing, matching `CorrelationView`'s existing collapsed-title-stays-visible precedent. **#501**: now a `ComposedChart` with a `stores/macroChartSelectionStore.ts`-backed picker (see below) instead of three fixed lines under a decorative legend. |
| `MacroTrendChart.tsx`, `stores/macroChartSelectionStore.ts` (new) (#501) | Reported live: the chart's legend was static dots while `BodyCompositionTrendChart` right below it already had toggle chips. Rebuilt on the two existing precedents rather than new mechanics — a `ToggleGroup` chip row per series (#277) and per-series line/bar/dots icon `radiogroup`s in the legend (#137's `CustomChartView` treatment) — and switched from `LineChart` to `ComposedChart` so a series can actually render as a `Bar`. Calories joined protein/fat/carbs as a fourth series, which is why the plotted values now come from `customChartSeries.ts`' `customChartPoints(entries, MACRO_SERIES_KEYS)` (raw + per-series-normalized 0-100, already keyed by these exact four `NumericSeriesKey`s) rather than the three inline `totalProtein`/`totalFat`/`totalCarbs` calls it used before: kcal in the thousands next to grams in the tens would flatten the macros on a shared raw axis. Axis behavior therefore follows the same count-based rule #330/#348/#393 settled on elsewhere — exactly 1 selected gets one real axis, exactly 2 get a real left/right pair with each side's ticks in that series' own color, 3+ fall back to the shared normalized axis with `tick={false}` and `customChartNormalizedCaveat` displayed (shown *only* in that case, since it would contradict real axis numbers otherwise). Series unit lives on the chip (`Protein (g)`, `Calories (kcal)`) per #451, not on every tick; the tooltip always reads the raw logged value. `macroChartSelectionStore` persists both the selection (all four by default — a deliberate product choice, so the default view is the normalized 3+ mode rather than the pre-#501 raw-grams axis) and the per-series chart types under its own `turtle-steps-macro-chart-selection` key; kept separate from `customChartSelectionStore` so this chart and "Compare your data" don't share one picker state. Plotted dates narrow to those where a *selected* series has a value (#376/#384), while the `MIN_TREND_DATA_POINTS` gate stays on the full four-series dataset — which now means a day with calories but no macros counts toward it. No new i18n keys: the calories label, chart-type labels, and normalized caveat all already existed for `CustomChartView`. |
| `CorrelationView.tsx` | Recharts `ScatterChart` of weekly avg calories vs. that week's weight delta, plus the plain-language summary from `correlationInsight()` and an explicit day-lag caveat. **#89**: the scatter plot itself is collapsed by default (behind a "Show chart" toggle, same chevron pattern as `about/ReleaseNotesSection.tsx`) until `correlationInsight()` returns non-null (needs `MIN_COMPARABLE_WEEKS = 4`) — with fewer weeks it was just a near-empty plot for the first several weeks of use. The caveat/summary text below it is always visible either way; only the chart itself is opt-in before there's a real insight, and renders expanded by default (no toggle) once there is one. **#581 / #587 / #592 / #593**: all correlation scatters (this view + the day-pair / night-eating / custom ones) wrap through `ZoomableScatterSurface.tsx` + `useScatterGestureZoom.ts` + `domain/stats/scatterGestureZoom.ts` for pinch/pan zoom. Domain tuples are value-memoized so Recharts does not see a fresh `[min,max]` identity every render (#587 crash). Unzoomed charts always publish the padded full domain (or `fullDomainOverride` — LateMeal 0..1440, Steps Tukey X, NightEating categorical −0.5..1.5): omitting `domain` after #587 with `allowDataOverflow` floored Y at 0 (#593 missing weight loss) and produced garbage ticks when zooming (#592). **#596**: numeric X/Y ticks go through `correlationScatterAxis.ts` (`formatCorrelationScatterTick` = one locale decimal; Y width 52) so padded-domain edges no longer dump raw floats; time-of-day / Yes-No X formatters unchanged. |
| `LateMealCorrelationView.tsx` (#116) | Recharts `ScatterChart` of each day's latest meal time (X, minutes-since-midnight formatted as HH:MM) vs. the *next* calendar day's weight delta (Y), plus the plain-language summary from `lateMealCorrelation()`. Same collapsed-until-there's-real-data pattern as `CorrelationView`'s #89 treatment (gated on `MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 8` in `lateMealCorrelation.ts` rather than `MIN_COMPARABLE_WEEKS = 4`), and its own explicit not-a-proven-cause-and-effect caveat, separate copy from `CorrelationView`'s day-lag caveat since these are different metrics. Renders nothing at all when `lateMealPoints()` is empty (no day has ever had both a meal time and a usable next-day weight pair), same as `CorrelationView` returning null with zero comparable weeks. |
| `domain/stats/mealFrequencyCorrelation.ts`, `MealFrequencyCorrelationView.tsx` (#338) | A fourth day-pair correlation, close structural mirror of `LateMealCorrelationView`/#116 above — but keyed on meal *count* (`entry.calorieEntries.length`, counting meal groups, not items within a meal) rather than meal *timing*. `mealFrequencyPoints(entries)` pairs each day's meal count with the *next* calendar day's weight delta (same two-endpoints requirement); `mealFrequencyCorrelationFromPoints`/`mealFrequencyCorrelation` median-split into a fewer/larger-meals half and a more/smaller-meals half, `MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 8`, same `classifyCorrelationStrength` banding every other day-pair view uses. Answers the user's own framing directly: "3 larger meals a day vs. 5 smaller ones — does the count itself relate to next-day weight?" Distinct from #257 (meal *timing*, elapsed fasting window) and #322 (meal *composition*, protein share) — a genuinely different dimension from either, not a duplicate. Fits the existing hardcoded-correlation pattern directly rather than needing #336's not-yet-built generic custom-metric infra, since meal count is already fully derivable from data already logged, no new manual entry. Mounted on `DashboardScreen.tsx` right after `LateMealCorrelationView`, own `dashboardChartVisibilityStore` key (`mealFrequencyCorrelation`) and own `useOutlierExclusion` storage key (`'mealFrequency'`). |
| `domain/stats/fastingWindow.ts`, `FastingWindowCorrelationView.tsx` (#257) | Answers "does when I stop eating affect next-day weight" via the actual elapsed duration between the previous day's *last* meal and the current day's *first* meal (correctly spanning midnight), median-split same shape as `lateMealCorrelation`/`sleepCorrelation` — two days with the same "last ate at 9pm" can have very different real fasting windows depending on when the next meal starts, which #116's raw-clock-time view can't distinguish. Gated on a stricter `MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 10` (vs. #116's 8) since a point here needs *two* consecutive days to each have a logged meal time, not just one. `FastingWindowCorrelationView.tsx` is a near-identical `ScatterChart` to `LateMealCorrelationView`, X-axis in hours instead of a clock time. `fastingHours` also added as a toggleable series in the "Compare your data" custom chart (`customChartSeries.ts`) — the one series that isn't a plain per-entry property (it depends on the previous day's own last meal too), so it's precomputed once via `fastingWindowPoints` and looked up by date rather than fitting the shared per-entry `SERIES_EXTRACTORS` shape the other series use. **#286**: #257 originally also shipped a second view, `fastingCutoffComparison(entries, cutoffTime)` — a fixed, user-adjustable eating-cutoff time (`useFastingCutoffStore`, Settings `<Input type="time">`) testing the popular "stop eating by 6pm" claim directly via a `FastingCutoffComparisonView.tsx` bar chart. Reported live as confusing (an extra Settings input with no clear payoff once the window-correlation view above already answers "how long was my fast" with no configuration needed) — removed entirely: `fastingCutoffStore.ts`, `FastingCutoffComparisonView.tsx`, `fastingCutoffComparison`/`FastingCutoffComparison` all deleted, along with the Settings card and the `dashboardChartVisibilityStore` toggle key. **#287**: `lastMealTimeMinutes`/`earliestMealTimeMinutes` (both previously `fastingWindowPoints`-only internals) now back a new exported `fastingHoursBetween(previousDayEntry, currentDayEntry)` — the same elapsed-hours midnight-spanning math, minus `fastingWindowPoints`' own "both days need a logged weight" requirement, which doesn't apply to a plain duration calculation. `fastingWindowPoints` itself now just calls this new function rather than duplicating its body. Both parameters are typed `Pick<DailyEntry, 'calorieEntries'>` rather than the full `DailyEntry`, so a caller that only has a bare `{ calorieEntries }` (not a real persisted entry) can still use it. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#287) | A quiet, dismissible in-app note — "Your fasting window was 12.0h." — shown right after a save makes the day's *first* meal with a recorded time, as long as the previous day also had one. Not a background/push notification (#261, closed as infeasible without a server/native app) — this only ever fires from `addMeal()`/`saveEditMeal()`'s own save flow while the app is open. `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal(nextEntries)` compares the day's timed-meal count immediately before vs. after the save (`some(entry => entry.timeEaten !== undefined)`) so a resave of an already-first timed meal doesn't retrigger it, then calls `fastingHoursBetween` (above) with `{ calorieEntries: nextEntries }` (no full `DailyEntry` exists yet for the day being edited). Rendered as the first child of `MealList`'s own top-level wrapper, so it's visible regardless of which parent mounts `MealList` (Today, History, `MealEditScreen`). **Real bug found and fixed on-device**: the previous-day lookup originally read `previousDayEntry`, component state fetched asynchronously on mount for #190's "repeat yesterday's meal" — logging the day's first meal quickly after opening the app could beat that fetch, silently skipping the toast even though yesterday's data was genuinely there, just not loaded into state in time. `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` is now `async` and fetches fresh via `dailyEntryRepository.getByDate(previousDate)` at save time instead, eliminating the race; both call sites now invoke it as `void announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal(nextEntries)` (fire-and-forget — the toast is supplementary feedback, not part of the save's own correctness). **#295**: the add-row's button cluster (`+ Add item`, its clear button, `Scan barcode`, `Log recipe` from #251) packed all 4 triggers into one non-wrapping `flex items-center gap-2` row — reported live as a horizontal-overflow regression, worst in Russian (longer button labels). Changed to `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2` plus `min-w-0` on the `+ Add item` button (its `truncate`-able inner span otherwise can't shrink below content width, a flex child's default `min-width: auto`). Verified via a real headless-browser check at 390×844/RU locale: no `scrollWidth` overflow, row wraps onto two lines. **Reopened, second root cause**: still reported as not firing — `addFoodEntry()` (the "Find food" quick-commit path, a separate save flow from `addMeal()`/`saveEditMeal()`) sets its own `timeEaten` but never called `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` at all, so the day's first meal going through "Find food" (the add row's primary button) never showed the toast regardless of the earlier race fix. `copyDaySelectedGroups`/`repeatSelectedItems` ("Copy yesterday's meals"/"Repeat yesterday's meal") were checked too but don't need the same fix — they never set `timeEaten` (only food data is cloned), so the toast correctly stays silent there until a later `saveEditMeal()` adds a time. Fixed by wiring the same call into `addFoodEntry()`; live-verified via Playwright with a previous day seeded directly into IndexedDB. **#301**: the toast was only ever set by `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal`, never reset — reported live as still showing after deleting the meal that triggered it. `confirmDeleteMeal()` now dismisses it whenever the day has no timed meal left at all post-delete, covering the reported "deleted the only meal" case without tracking exactly which meal produced the currently-shown value. **#287 reopened again**: adding a time to an *already-logged* meal via the dedicated single-meal edit route never showed the toast — `saveEditMeal()` clears `editingMealId` right after firing the toast logic, which `MealEditScreen`'s own effect reads as "done" and immediately navigates back (`onFocusedMealDone`), unmounting the component before the toast's async lookup could ever set what was, until now, local `useState`. Fixed by lifting the toast into a new `stores/fastingWindowToastStore.ts` (`hours`/`date`, in-memory only — see its own comment) instead of local state, scoped by date so a value set while editing one day doesn't leak into an unrelated one after navigating elsewhere; `confirmDeleteMeal`'s #301 fix now calls the store's `dismiss()` rather than a local setter. **#300 follow-up**: the *add-row's own* scan path had the same "closing via X still leaves it visible" shape as the edit-mode fix above, just manifesting differently — `handleBarcodeScanned` (add-row) doesn't stage into an array, so nothing was ever added to the meal itself (0 kcal, confirmed), but the prefilled draft stayed in the add-row's own fields, and #151/#153's "preview the current draft in place of + Add item" behavior then rendered it as a chip that read like an already-added item. Fixed the same way: the add-item sheet's `onOpenChange` now resets the draft (`resetItemDraft()`) when closed via X/Escape/backdrop *and* the draft came from a scan (`addItemBarcode !== undefined`) — manually-typed drafts keep #221's original "survive an accidental close" behavior untouched. Also fixed in the same pass: the preview chip and its clear-✕ were separate flex children of the wrapping trigger row, so a long preview (a scanned product's name, especially in Russian) could push just the ✕ onto its own second line — grouped them into one `min-w-0` flex container so the wrap now treats the pair as a single unit. **#450**: reported live with exact repro steps — an already-shown toast (correctly computed from the previous day's own last meal at the time it fired) never updates again afterward, even when the user navigates back to that previous day and logs one more, later meal there, which should genuinely change the fasting-window number. Root cause: `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` only ever fires from *this* day's own first-timed-meal save — editing a *different*, earlier day never calls it at all, since it's scoped entirely to whichever `MealList` instance is currently mounted. New `reconcileFastingWindowToastForPreviousDayEdit(nextEntries)`, called alongside `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` at the same 3 save sites (`addMeal`/`addFoodEntry`/`saveEditMeal`): checks whether the currently-shown toast's own `date` is exactly this day's next calendar day (`addDays(parseISO(date), 1)`) — if so, re-fetches that day's entry fresh via `dailyEntryRepository.getByDate` (same "don't trust possibly-stale local state" reasoning the original race-condition fix above already established for `previousDayEntry`) and recomputes `fastingHoursBetween` using this save's own `nextEntries` as the new previous-day side, updating (or, if no longer valid, dismissing) the store. Deliberately not extended to the meal-deletion path (`items.length === 0` branch in `saveEditMeal`) — that's a distinct pre-existing gap (deleting a whole meal group on a previous day never called `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` either, so there's no established call site to add this alongside), out of this issue's own reported scope. New test seeds both days directly, pre-sets the toast store to simulate an already-shown stale value, edits the previous day's `MealList` instance, and confirms the store updates to the new correct hours — then remounts *today's* own `MealList` (matching the real navigate-back flow) and confirms the updated toast text actually renders, since the toast is scoped to whichever date's own component instance is currently mounted. Verified this test genuinely fails without the fix (temporarily reverted to check, stuck at the stale value) and passes with it. `tsc -b`/lint/`MealList.test.tsx` (61 tests) green. |
| `AddMealDialog.tsx` (new, #454) | Replaces `MealList.tsx`'s old inline accordion add-row with a dedicated full-screen flyout, per a reference mockup the user shared. `MealList.tsx` renders one trigger button (`+ Add another meal`) and tracks the in-progress meal's own `CalorieEntry.id` locally (`inProgressMealId`, reset each time the flyout opens): the *first* call to `appendItemsToNewMeal(newItems)` creates a fresh entry, every subsequent call (from a search pick, barcode scan, Repeat, recipe log, or manual entry — all routed through the same callback) appends to that same entry's `items`, so the flyout can stay open across several single-dish adds instead of closing after each one (confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: persistent multi-add, not #183's old check-many-then-confirm-once model). Closing the flyout (Done) just ends that session; reopening starts a new one. Inside: a header (meal label + time chip + the restored #117 Clear-time button + a meal-level note input), "Repeat yesterday's [meal]" (reuses `RepeatMealDialog.tsx` unchanged) when a same-position previous-day meal exists, one search input with the barcode scanner folded into its own icon button (`BarcodeScannerDialog.tsx`, re-wired: a scan now resolves straight to the same tap-to-confirm-quantity step a search pick uses, rather than #256's old behavior of prefilling the manual-entry sheet), a "Recent" list (top 5 most-recently-touched personal `mealItems`, shown only while the search box is empty), "Can't find it? Add manually" (the *only* remaining manual-entry route, opening `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx` unchanged) and "Log recipe" (`LogRecipeDialog.tsx` unchanged), the live "meal so far" items list with per-item delete, and the new meal-level `CalorieEntry.reaction?: Emotion` picker (`DAY_EMOTIONS`, shown once the meal has at least one item) — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` to build now rather than defer; deliberately typed `Emotion` (smiley/neutral/frown), not `MealEmotion` (thumbs/🤌) — #129 already established that a *shared* per-meal reaction is the wrong shape for "did I like this specific dish" (that's what moved `CalorieItem.emotion` to per-item in the first place), but "was this meal, as a whole, satisfying" is a different question with no per-dish ambiguity. `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` is **kept, not deleted** — it's still the "Find food" flow for adding to an *already-existing, already-saved* meal from `MealListItem`'s own edit mode, a flow this issue never touched; `AddMealDialog.tsx` duplicates its search-ranking/quantity-scaling helpers (`rankBySearchMatch`, `defaultQuantityFor`/`gramsFor`/`scaledValuesFor`, etc.) rather than extracting them into a shared module, a deliberate tradeoff to avoid risking a regression in that still-heavily-tested flow for the sake of removing a small amount of duplication. `#221`'s old add-row localStorage draft-persistence and `useAddMealRowCollapseStore` (the accordion's collapsed/expanded state) are both deleted outright, not ported — the new flyout has no equivalent collapsed state, and a manual-entry draft not surviving an accidental close is an accepted, documented scope trim. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#454) | Two real regressions were found (and fixed) only once `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`'s much older, more exhaustive add-row test coverage (predating the `MealList.tsx` extraction, #145) was ported against the new flyout — its own initial implementation pass had only been checked against `MealList.test.tsx`'s lighter coverage. (1) `AddMealDialog.tsx`'s manual-entry per-100g/per-portion mode toggle silently stopped converting already-typed values when switched — `MealList.tsx`'s own edit-mode equivalent, `updateEditItemMode()`, does real conversion math (`scaleFromPer100g`/`ratesFromAbsolute`) on switch; the flyout's first-pass `onMacroModeChange` was a bare setter with no conversion at all. Ported the same math in as `changeManualDraftMode()`. (2) The FoodPickerDialog-era "today would be" live preview (kcal *and* macros, #278) that used to show the moment a catalog food was checked (before confirming) was missing entirely from the flyout's own tap-to-confirm-quantity step — restored as `activeTodayTotalPreview`/`activeTodayRemainingPreview`, computed from the active item's own prospective scaled values on top of whatever's already confirmed. Two more gaps were found the same way and confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (restore, rather than match the mockup's simpler look, since both still work fine when *editing* an already-saved meal — confirming they were rewrite gaps, not deliberate scope trims): the shared per-meal note field (`CalorieEntry.note`, a plain `Input` in the flyout's header now threading through `MealList.tsx`'s own `newMealNote`/`updateNewMealNote`, which also writes through to the already-created in-progress entry, not just a pre-entry seed value — same dual behavior `updateNewMealTime` needed once `timeEaten` could also be edited after the first item already landed), and the #117 app-level Clear-time button (native time-picker Reset is unreliable, restored in the flyout's own header next to the Time input). |
| `AddMealDialog.tsx`, `MealList.tsx` (#459, #454 follow-up; edit host moved fully into MealList by #461) | Reported live comparing 3 fresh screenshots of the shipped #454 flyout against the original mockup — several concrete divergences, plus editing an already-saved meal still opening the old flat-list editor entirely (a deliberate #454 scope boundary, now explicitly requested to close). Visual fixes: the "Recent" list now caps at `RECENT_COUNT` (3) with a "Show all"/"Show less" toggle instead of always showing up to 5; the quick-action row is now 3 bordered `QuickActionCard`s (Add food/Scan barcode/Log recipe) with icon + label instead of small text links — resolved via `AskUserQuestion` to add a dedicated Scan-barcode card *back* even though the search bar already has its own scan icon, reversing #454's original "one entry point" decision, to match the mockup exactly (the barcode card needed a disambiguating `aria-label`, `scanBarcodeButton — mealLabel`, since its visible text duplicates the search icon button's own name); the "Was it tasty?" picker got a new `EmotionPicker` `layout="spread"` variant (large `icon-xl` buttons, `justify-between`, a visible text label under each via a new `mealReactionValueLabel` translation, distinct from the day-mood `emotionLabel`); spacing bumped modestly throughout to read less tight. `MealEditScreen.tsx` rewritten to render `AddMealDialog` directly (`open` always true, `onOpenChange(false)` → `navigate(-1)`) instead of `MealList` in single-meal-focus mode, wiring its own repository-backed handlers to three (partly new) `AddMealDialog` props: `onAppendItems` (unchanged), a new `onUpdateItem` for per-item edit-in-place (tapping a "meal so far" row or its new pencil icon opens the same manual-entry sheet pre-filled via a new `startEditItem()`, using `perPortion` mode as a direct passthrough since `totalFromPortion` already passes typed values straight through; `saveManualDraft()` branches on a new `editingItemId` state to call `onUpdateItem` instead of `onAppendItems`), and a new `onDeleteMeal`/`mealPosition` pair gating a header trash icon (same 2-step `t.history.confirmDelete*` confirm pattern used elsewhere) for whole-meal delete — removing the last item still deletes the whole meal too, the same invariant `MealList.tsx`'s own `removeItemFromNewMeal` already had. Two scope decisions made explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` given the migration's size: (1) the old `MealEditScreen` UI supported simultaneous multi-item inline editing and meal-label renaming, neither of which `AddMealDialog` has (one-item-at-a-time via a sheet, no label field) — chose to replace the old 787-line test suite with a lean new one for the new model rather than extend `AddMealDialog` to preserve every old capability; (2) the #287/#456 fasting-window toast lives inside `MealList.tsx` itself, so it no longer shows on this dedicated edit screen (still fully works via Today/History/the normal add-meal flow) — chose to skip restoring it here rather than add a second repository fetch + thread it into `AddMealDialog` as a new prop, deferred to **#460**. `tsc -b`/lint/`AddMealDialog.test.tsx`+`MealList.test.tsx`+`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`+new `MealEditScreen.test.tsx` green. Confirmed on-device. |
| `MealList.tsx` (#456) | The fasting-window toast (#287) first lost its manual close button (deliberately persistent, not user-dismissible) — then, per direct follow-up ("I still don't see the notification... I want it to display constantly"), was rebuilt entirely as a plain derived value instead of the old action-triggered store. Root issue with the old design: `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal`/`showFastingWindowToast` only ever *set* something at the moment a save made a day's first-timed-meal condition newly true — reopening the app later (with nothing new to save) never re-showed it, independent of dismiss state. Replaced the whole mechanism — `stores/fastingWindowToastStore.ts` (deleted), `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal`, `reconcileFastingWindowToastForPreviousDayEdit` (#450's own retroactive-recalc patch), and #301's explicit dismiss-on-delete call — with one `useMemo(() => previousDayEntry ? fastingHoursBetween(previousDayEntry, { calorieEntries }, dayStartTime) : null, [previousDayEntry, calorieEntries, dayStartTime])`. This is correct on page load (shows immediately if the condition already holds, no action needed), correct on every save (a real dependency change), correct on navigating between days (`previousDayEntry` naturally refetches), correct on delete (a day with no timed meal left naturally derives `null`) — all for free, no store, no explicit reconcile/dismiss calls anywhere. `useMemo` specifically (over a plain inline computation) per an explicit ask to not recompute on unrelated re-renders (opening a dialog, typing in search) — only when one of the 3 real inputs actually changes. Orphaned `dismissFastingWindowToastLabel` (Dictionary/en/ru) removed. |
| `SleepCorrelationView.tsx`, `StepsCorrelationView.tsx` (#167) | Extends `LateMealCorrelationView`'s exact day-pairing shape to two more daily metrics — each day's logged `sleepHours`/`steps` vs. the *next* calendar day's weight delta — via two new parallel (not shared/genericized, matching this codebase's existing precedent of one file per metric rather than one generic correlation engine) domain modules, `domain/stats/sleepCorrelation.ts` and `stepsCorrelation.ts`: `sleepPoints`/`stepsPoints` (raw pairs) and `sleepCorrelation`/`stepsCorrelation` (median-split summary, `MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 8`, same gate as #116). Same collapsed-until-there's-real-data pattern, same not-a-proven-cause-and-effect caveat wording pattern (metric-specific copy). Sleep's threshold rounds to the nearest half-hour; steps' threshold rounds to the nearest 100. Both mounted on `DashboardScreen.tsx` right after `LateMealCorrelationView`. **#375**: reported live — a handful of extreme step-count days (a multi-year Apple Health import, #366) stretched the scatter chart's x-axis so far that the vast majority of normal (<15,000-step) points crushed into an unreadable cluster near the left edge. Fixed by reusing `outlierBounds()` (Tukey's-fences, #224 — the same bounds already deciding which points render red/get listed below) for the x-axis `domain` (`[0, stepsBounds.upper]` + `allowDataOverflow`) instead of Recharts' raw min/max `'auto'` domain, falling back to `[0, 'auto']` exactly as before when there's too little data (<4 points) for bounds to mean anything. Also confirmed the suspected data-quality root cause behind the extreme values themselves and fixed separately in #385 (Apple Health's `StepCount` import summed every record for a date with no dedup by `sourceName`, double-counting a walk both an iPhone and Apple Watch logged). Confirmed on-device. |
| `ProteinCorrelationView.tsx` (#216) | Third parallel day-pairing view, same shape as Sleep/Steps above: `domain/stats/proteinCorrelation.ts`'s `proteinPoints`/`proteinCorrelation` pair each day's protein metric with the *next* calendar day's weight delta, `MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 8`. **Resolved a genuine scope fork via `AskUserQuestion` before building**: the issue's own notes pointed at extending this day-pair family to *both* calories and protein, but the Dashboard already has a calories-vs-weight correlation (`CorrelationView.tsx`, above) via a completely different mechanism — weekly averages, not day-pairs. Building a second, day-pair "calories vs weight" view alongside the existing weekly one would have been redundant/confusing on the same Dashboard. User chose protein-only, leaving `CorrelationView`'s calories mechanism untouched. Mounted on `DashboardScreen.tsx` right after `StepsCorrelationView`. **#322**: originally split on raw protein grams (threshold rounded to the nearest 5g) — reported live as confounded by total calorie intake, since a high-protein day is usually just a high-*everything* day, so the graph mostly read "ate a lot" rather than anything protein-specific. `ProteinPoint.proteinG`/`ProteinCorrelation.thresholdProteinG` became `proteinPercent`/`thresholdProteinPercent` (protein's share of that day's total calories via `totalCalories`, `(proteinG * 4) / kcal * 100`; a day with no/zero logged calories is skipped rather than dividing by zero), threshold now rounds to the nearest 5%. This isolates "carnivore-style, protein-heavy" days from "ate a lot of everything" days without introducing real multivariate statistics — deliberately kept to the same plain deterministic median-split every other correlation view here already uses, rather than the more rigorous (and more complex, smaller-sample) "only compare days with similar total calories" alternative that was discussed and deferred. Chart X-axis/tooltip switched from a gram amount to a new `%`-labeled metric; summary copy reworded from "ate less/more than Xg of protein" to "protein was less/more than X% of your calories". |
| `domain/stats/correlationStrength.ts` (new), `CorrelationStrengthLabel.tsx` (new), `CorrelationView.tsx`, `LateMealCorrelationView.tsx`, `SleepCorrelationView.tsx`, `StepsCorrelationView.tsx`, `ProteinCorrelationView.tsx` (#224, piece 2 of 3) | A small plain-language strength label ("Weak/Moderate/Strong pattern") under every correlation view's existing day/week-count line — deterministic, not a real statistical measure: these views compare two median-split groups' average weight change, not a Pearson `r`, so `classifyCorrelationStrength()` bands the absolute gap between those two group averages against fixed kg thresholds instead (`WEEKLY_STRENGTH_THRESHOLDS_KG` 0.15/0.35kg for `correlationInsight`'s weekly deltas, `DAILY_STRENGTH_THRESHOLDS_KG` 0.05/0.15kg for the four day-pair views — weekly deltas are naturally larger in magnitude, so one threshold set for both would misclassify one or the other). Each of the (now 6) correlation result types gained a `strength: CorrelationStrength` field; the label itself is one small shared component (`CorrelationStrengthLabel.tsx`) reused identically by all 6 views rather than duplicated. **#336**: the fixed-kg thresholds only make sense because these 6 views' dependent axis is always weight — a user-defined correlation's B side could be any unit at all. New `classifyRelativeCorrelationStrength(groupAvgDifference, allBValues)` bands the group difference against B's own standard deviation instead (Cohen's-d convention: medium=0.5/large=0.8 reused as this app's moderate/strong cutoffs, small folded into weak) — self-scaling regardless of whether B is kg, steps, or a 1-5 severity scale. Used only by `customCorrelationEngine.ts`; the 6 existing views' own fixed-kg classification is untouched. |
| `domain/dailyEntry/nightEating.ts`, `domain/stats/nightEatingCorrelation.ts`, `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx` (#383) | Design fully resolved via `AskUserQuestion` (multiple rounds) rather than reusing #336's generic custom-metric engine, despite #116's `LateMealCorrelationView` already correlating last-meal-time with next-day weight via median split — a fixed yes/no night-eating cutoff is a different, more concrete question, kept as its own view. Since the predictor (`hadNightEating()`, above) is already boolean rather than continuous, `nightEatingCorrelation.ts` does a plain two-group comparison (night-eating days vs. not) instead of every other correlation view's median split; its scatter chart plots two fixed columns (x=0/"No", x=1/"Yes") rather than a continuous scale. Same `classifyCorrelationStrength`/`MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS`-gated collapse-until-real-data pattern as the other day-pair views above. Wired everywhere `onPeriod`/`hadConstipation` already appear: a `Moon`-icon toggle on both `DailyEntryForm.tsx` (Today) and `DayDetail.tsx` (History), a 4th always-on calendar dot (`bg-indigo-500`, `CalendarView.tsx`) — always-on rather than tracking-store-gated like the other two, matching the field's own no-Settings-gate choice above — and a 3rd `BOOLEAN_SERIES` entry in `CustomChartView.tsx`'s Compare Data chart. Registered in `dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts`/`dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` like every other Dashboard section. Also added a `nightEatingColumn` to the CSV/XLSX/Markdown exports (`exportCsv.ts`/`exportXlsx.ts`/`exportMarkdown.ts`) — unlike `onPeriod`/`hadConstipation`'s raw optional field, this column exports the *effective* `hadNightEating()` value. **This "no untracked state" claim turned out wrong, and is exactly what #394 fixed** (see below) — night eating always had an untracked state, it was just silently defaulting to `false` instead of leaving the column blank like every other optional field here already does. Found and fixed one pre-existing gap while building: `dailyEntryFormSchema.ts`/`dailyEntryFormMapping.ts` needed the new field added explicitly, since react-hook-form doesn't persist a watched-but-unregistered field through `getValues()` — caught by a failing test before it shipped broken. Confirmed on-device. **#398**: reported live — the Russian label `'Ел(а) поздно вечером'` reads awkwardly; the user's real gender is already known via `useProfileStore().sex` (#233). `Dictionary.nightEatingLabel` changed from a plain string to `(sex?: Sex) => string` (same function-entry pattern `mealLabel(n)` already established) — `ru.ts` picks "Ела"/"Ел"/the neutral "Ел(а)" placeholder by `sex`, `en.ts` ignores the param (no gendered verb in English). `DailyEntryForm.tsx`/`DayDetail.tsx` (the two real toggle renderings) pass the real profile `sex`; `CustomChartView.tsx`'s legend and `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx`'s axis/tooltip both call it with no argument, staying a generic label regardless of who's viewing. **#394**: reported live, directly — a user who'd tracked night eating for one day saw a correlation view claim "2229 days of data" and a "moderate pattern." Root cause confirmed: `hadNightEating()` returned a plain `boolean`, so any day with **zero meals carrying a `timeEaten`** (an override-less day with no meals logged, or with meals logged but none timestamped — the overwhelming majority of a real historical/imported dataset, since none of #365/#366/#367's importers set meal-level `timeEaten`) silently resolved to `false`, indistinguishable from "confirmed no late meal." Return type widened to `boolean | undefined` — `undefined` specifically when there's no override *and* no meal that day has any `timeEaten` at all (a meal with a missing time doesn't prevent a *different* timed meal that day from still giving a real answer). `nightEatingPoints()` now skips a day entirely when `hadNightEating()` is `undefined`, rather than folding it into the "No" group — the same two-endpoints-requirement pattern every other day-pair correlation here already uses, just extended by one more requirement. Every other consumer already degrades gracefully with no code change needed, confirmed via `tsc -b`: `DayDetail.tsx`'s `aria-pressed`/class-name ternaries, `CalendarView.tsx`'s dot, and `customChartSeries.ts`'s marker-date filter all already treat a falsy value as "don't show," and the CSV/Markdown/XLSX exporters already render `undefined` as a blank cell for `onPeriod`/`hadConstipation` — the fix's return-type change flows through to a blank `nightEatingColumn` for these days for free. The one real UI change: `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s two-option `ToggleGroup` now shows *neither* Yes/No pressed when the value is `undefined`, instead of defaulting to "No" pressed — every test asserting the old default-to-No/false behavior as correct (`nightEating.test.ts`, `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`, `DayDetail.test.tsx`, all three export test files) was itself testing the reported bug, updated to assert the fixed behavior; a new `nightEatingCorrelation.test.ts` case directly reproduces the report's shape (8 real tracked day-pairs plus 8 more untracked ones that must NOT inflate `dayCount`). **#410**: `NIGHT_EATING_CUTOFF_HHMM` moved from `'21:00'` to `'23:00'` — live feedback questioned a stretch of ordinary evenings all flagging as night eating, and 9pm read as too early/strict for what should count as "late." Every existing test fixture using the cutoff boundary happened to already use `'23:00'` exactly (still `>=` the new cutoff, unaffected); only `nightEating.test.ts`'s own boundary-specific cases needed updating (moved its true-case from `'21:00'` to `'23:00'`, added a new just-before-cutoff false case at `'22:59'`). **#406**: reported live — once the Today toggle (`DailyEntryForm.tsx`) was tapped to Yes or No, there was no way back to the unselected/derived state. Root cause: Radix's `ToggleGroup type="single"` already fires `onValueChange('')` when the currently-pressed item is tapped again (its own built-in deselect behavior), but the handler's `value && setNightEatingOverride(...)` guard silently discarded that empty-string call. `setNightEatingOverride`'s param widened from `boolean` to `boolean | undefined`; the handler now reads `value === '' ? undefined : value === 'yes'` instead of guarding on truthiness. Deliberately **not** extended to `hadConstipation`'s identical-looking `value &&` guard a few lines above — that toggle has no visually-distinct unset state to begin with (`value={hadConstipation ? 'yes' : 'no'}` always shows one of the two pressed, unlike night eating's post-#394 "neither pressed" rendering for `undefined`), so there's nothing for the user to visibly get stuck in. Also **not** extended to `DayDetail.tsx`'s (History) own night-eating control — a single toggle `Button` that flips the effective value on each click (`nightEatingOverride: !hadNightEating(entry)`), a genuinely different widget shape with no natural third click state; has the same underlying gap but needs its own design decision for what "deselect" means there, left as a follow-up rather than bolted on here (#413). **#413**: built, keeping the single-button widget shape consistent with `onPeriod`/`hadConstipation` right next to it (rather than swapping in a two-option `ToggleGroup`, which would look visually inconsistent among three same-shaped pill buttons in one row) — a small separate "×" `Button` (`variant="ghost" size="icon-xs"`) appears next to the toggle only when `entry.nightEatingOverride !== undefined`, resetting it to `undefined` on click. Same idiom `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s own water-entry pills already use for removing one entry, applied here as a conditional "clear this override" affordance instead. New `t.dailyEntry.clearNightEatingOverrideLabel` (en/ru). New tests: no Clear button with no override yet, Clear button appears and resets once one exists. **#407**: reversed #398's own deliberate scope limit — reported live that `CustomChartView.tsx`'s legend/chip and `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx`'s axis/tooltip still showed the neutral "Ел(а)" placeholder. Both call `t.dailyEntry.nightEatingLabel()` with no argument; both now read `sex` via `useProfileStore` (already imported in `CustomChartView.tsx`, newly imported in `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx`) and pass it through — `BooleanSeriesConfig.label`'s signature widened from `(t: Dictionary) => string` to `(t: Dictionary, sex?: Sex) => string` to carry it into `CustomChartView.tsx`'s per-series config. Audited the rest of the app for other gendered-verb placeholders (grepped for the `(а)`/`(a)` pattern): `nightEatingLabel` is the only gendered Dictionary *function* — everything else that matched was either this same string's own neutral fallback or an unrelated `Array.sort((a, b) => ...)` comparator. One more genuine spot found — the CSV/XLSX/Markdown export column header (`nightEatingColumn`) is a plain (non-function) string using the same placeholder — deliberately **not** folded into this fix since it needs a `sex` parameter threaded through 3 export builder functions and all their call sites, a meaningfully bigger change; filed as its own follow-up (#414). New test in `CustomChartView.test.tsx` (switches to `ru` locale + sets profile `sex`, confirms the gendered form renders instead of the placeholder) — `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx`'s own fix isn't independently testable the same way: its `name={...}` props feed Recharts' `XAxis`/`Tooltip`, and (per #330's own note above) Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom, so nothing there is asserted on anywhere in this file already. **#414**: the deferred export-column follow-up, now built — `Dictionary.exportXlsx.nightEatingColumn` widened from a plain `string` to `(sex?: Sex) => string`, same shape `dailyEntry.nightEatingLabel` already established. `buildDailyLogCsv`/`buildDailyLogMarkdown`/`buildExportWorkbook` (`exportCsv.ts`/`exportMarkdown.ts`/`exportXlsx.ts`) each gained an optional trailing `sex?: Sex` parameter, threaded through to the one call site in each that builds this column's header. `ExportSection.tsx` (the sole caller of all three) reads the real profile `sex` via `useProfileStore` once and passes it to each. New test in `exportCsv.test.ts` (ru dictionary + `'female'`) confirms the gendered header renders instead of the neutral placeholder — the other two builders share the identical one-line call-site change, not independently retested. **#423**: reported live — deselecting on Today still didn't work, "the selection defaults to Yes" after trying to clear it. First attempt assumed the same coincidence gap #413 solved for History (derived value masking a cleared override) and added a matching explicit "×" Clear button — reported live as *still* broken, reproducing even in a plain desktop browser on a day with zero meals logged, which ruled out any device- or data-specific explanation. Root-caused via live Playwright against the real dev server rather than another guess (jsdom's own render of the identical interaction passed cleanly the whole time — a real false-negative, not caught by `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`'s existing coverage): Radix's `ToggleGroup`'s `useControllableState` treats a controlled `value` of `undefined` as "stop controlling me, keep whatever you last held internally," not "explicitly show nothing selected." The ternary computing this `ToggleGroup`'s `value` used `undefined` for the "nothing selected"/`nightEatingEffective === undefined` case — so clicking to deselect (#406's own mechanism, or the Clear button attempt) correctly cleared `nightEatingOverride` in the actually-saved data every time, but the rendered `aria-checked` never updated, silently keeping whatever option was last clicked. Fixed by using `''` instead — a real, defined value Radix accepts as "no item currently matches," which keeps it in controlled mode and correctly clears the visible selection. With the real bug fixed, #406's own built-in tap-to-deselect works reliably on its own, so the Clear button (and the `nightEatingOverride` hook-return addition that only existed to gate it) was removed again as unnecessary. **#423 reopened a second time** — still reported broken live on real Safari and the installed PWA, both on a day with a real late meal logged and (once that theory was refuted) on a completely blank day with zero food logged. Built a live cross-engine Playwright harness (Chromium + WebKit, seeded real IndexedDB directly via `indexedDB.open` inside `page.evaluate`, reading the actual persisted record after each click) covering both scenarios — the ToggleGroup's tap-to-deselect worked correctly every time, on both engines, with no console errors. Read through the full Radix chain (`@radix-ui/react-toggle-group` → `@radix-ui/react-toggle` → `@radix-ui/react-roving-focus`) directly from `node_modules` looking for an engine-dependent hazard in the click-to-deselect path; found none — it's a synchronous, purely controlled-value-driven `onClick`, with no focus- or timing-dependent branches. Since the real Safari mechanism couldn't be confirmed from this environment (Playwright's WebKit build, especially run from Windows, is a known imperfect stand-in for Apple's actual shipped Safari), restored the explicit "×" Clear button next to the ToggleGroup — `nightEatingOverride` re-exposed from `useDailyEntryFormState` to gate it — as a second, independent path to "no override" that doesn't depend on the ToggleGroup's own tap-the-active-item deselect behavior at all, same small-icon-button pattern `DayDetail.tsx`'s #413 treatment already uses. New tests: no Clear button with no override, Clear button resets the override and updates the toggle's visible selection independently of the ToggleGroup's own click handling. **#423 reopened a third time**, this time with screenshots that pinned down the actual mechanism: on a day with a real dinner logged at 19:0x (well before the 23:00 cutoff), tapping Clear switched the toggle to show "No" pressed instead of nothing selected. The toggle's `value` was still computed from `nightEatingEffective` (override, or else the meal-derived fallback) — clearing an override on any day with real logged meals just revealed the derived answer, which rendered identically to a real explicit pick. This was pure app logic with no browser dependency at all; the earlier "Safari/PWA-only" framing (this file's own text above) was wrong — a mismatch between the blank test data used while investigating and the user's real logged data, not an engine difference. Fixed per an explicit 3-state spec from the user (toggle only ever shows Yes/No/nothing — Clear always produces nothing, full stop, regardless of any meal data): the `ToggleGroup`'s `value` now reads `nightEatingOverride` directly rather than `nightEatingEffective`; the derived value moved out to a new small caption line (`t.dailyEntry.nightEatingAutoDetectedLabel(option)`, en/ru) rendered only when `nightEatingOverride === undefined && nightEatingEffective !== undefined`, so it's still visible without being conflated with the toggle's own pressed state anymore. New tests: the derived value renders as caption text with neither toggle option pressed; Clear resets to nothing selected even on a day with a real logged meal that derives a definite answer (the exact reported shape). Verified live via Playwright screenshots (real seeded IndexedDB, before/after clicking Clear) rather than relying on the test suite alone, given this exact class of bug had already once slipped past jsdom. **#428/#429**: with the real `value={''}` root cause fixed, both remaining workarounds from #423's own reopenings were removed as no-longer-needed — the "×" Clear button (Radix's own tap-the-active-item-again deselect already works reliably) and the auto-detected caption line (`nightEatingAutoDetectedLabel`, Dictionary/en/ru, now unused).
