Confirmed on-device; regression tests added asserting neither element renders even in the states that used to show them (an explicit override present, a derived value from a logged meal) — the original PR only re-tested the no-override/no-derived-value case. |
| `domain/stats/outlierDetection.ts` (new), `stores/outlierExclusionStore.ts` (new), `shared/hooks/useOutlierExclusion.ts` (new), `OutlierPointsList.tsx` (new), `outlierScatterShape.tsx` (new), all 6 correlation domain modules + all 6 correlation views (#224, piece 3 of 3) | Outlier detection + tap-to-exclude, the issue's own last deferred piece. Scope (all 6 views; exclusion is per-view via tapping a flagged point, not a global toggle) resolved via `AskUserQuestion`. `outlierDetection.ts`: deterministic Tukey's-fences rule (1.5×IQR beyond Q1/Q3) — no distributional assumptions, same fixed-threshold philosophy `correlationStrength.ts` already established; `flagOutliers(points, getX, getY)` flags a point if *either* axis is an outlier (a vacation/illness week might show up as an unusual eating time, an unusual weight swing, or both), needs ≥4 points for quartiles to mean anything. Each of `correlationInsight.ts`/`lateMealCorrelation.ts`/`fastingWindow.ts`/`sleepCorrelation.ts`/`stepsCorrelation.ts`/`proteinCorrelation.ts` gained a `*FromPoints(points)` sibling — the existing median-split math extracted so a view can filter out excluded points *before* computing the summary, without the domain layer knowing "exclusion" exists at all; the original `xCorrelation(entries)` is now a thin `xCorrelationFromPoints(xPoints(entries))` wrapper, fully behavior-preserving (no existing test needed updating). 4 of the 6 point interfaces (`LateMealPoint`/`SleepPoint`/`StepsPoint`/`ProteinPoint`) gained a `date` field they didn't have before (purely additive) — a stable per-point key for exclusion, same "date the point ends on" convention `FastingWindowPoint.date` already used; `correlationInsight.ts` newly exports `CorrelationInsightPoint { weekStart, calories, delta }` the same way (its own points were previously function-internal, never exposed). `useOutlierExclusion(viewKey, points, getX, getY, getKey)` (`shared/hooks/`) is the one piece of logic actually shared across all 6 views (same precedent `CorrelationStrengthLabel.tsx` already set for shared *support* infrastructure, even though each view keeps its own chart/JSX per this codebase's "one file per metric" convention) — returns per-point outlier flags, an `isExcluded`/`toggle` pair, and the already-filtered `includedPoints` a view feeds straight into its `*FromPoints` call. `OutlierPointsList.tsx`: real tappable `Button`s below the chart for each flagged point (not a tiny SVG scatter dot — an unreliable touch target, and consistent with #275's own "explicit interactive element beats tap-anywhere-on-the-chart" reasoning), `aria-pressed` for excluded state, `line-through` styling once excluded. `outlierScatterShape.tsx`: a flagged point still renders in `var(--destructive)` on the chart itself for visual context; an excluded one renders dimmed (`opacity: 0.3`) rather than disappearing, so "why does this pattern look different now" stays answerable at a glance. **Real bug caught and fixed while building this**: the exclusion store's selector used a `state.excluded[viewKey] ?? {}` fallback — a fresh `{}` on every call changes reference identity every render, which zustand's `useSyncExternalStore`-based subscription reads as "state changed," causing "Maximum update depth exceeded" the instant any correlation view mounted. Fixed with a module-level stable `EMPTY_EXCLUDED` constant instead of an inline literal. **#354**: reported live — with several of these views rendering back to back, and a mid-list one sometimes showing little more than its own title (the "not enough data" empty state), it was hard to tell where one view's own chart/summary ended and the next began. All 7 (the 6 here plus `CorrelationView`) share the identical `flex flex-col gap-1.5` root wrapper — a mechanical one-line addition (`rounded-lg border border-border p-3`) per file, same treatment `CustomCorrelationView.tsx` (#336) already established for its own new cards. Deliberately scoped to just these 7, matching the issue's own filed scope — every other Dashboard section (trend charts, `CustomChartView`, summary cards) is unchanged. **#360**: reported live right after confirming #354 — the inconsistency was now visible instead of invisible, since the 7 correlation views had a border and every other Dashboard section didn't. Extended the identical one-line treatment to all 11 remaining `sectionsByKey` components (`WeightTrendChart`, `CalorieTrendChart`, `MacroTrendChart`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart`, `CustomChartView`, `FoodReactionsView`, `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap`, `RecentAveragesCards`, `WeeklySummaryCards`, `MonthlySummaryCards`, `CompareRangesView`) — each shares the same root-wrapper-per-branch shape (`flex flex-col gap-1.5` or `gap-3` depending on the file), applied to every early-return branch, not just the fully-rendered one, so the border shows even in a collapsed/no-data state. Two of these files (`FoodReactionsView.tsx`, `CompareRangesView.tsx`, `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx`, `MonthlySummaryCards.tsx`) also have their own *inner* sub-wrappers using the identical class string at a smaller gap size — only the outer root wrapper (the one immediately below the section's own `ChartTitleWithToggle` call) got the border, not any nested child. Every Dashboard section is now visually consistent; `CustomCorrelationView.tsx` (#336, user-defined correlations) and the fixed `sectionsByKey` list both use the same bordered-card look now, though the former still isn't part of the reorderable list (unchanged, out of scope here). **#374**: reported live — a multi-year dataset flags 60+ outliers on one view with day+month-only labels, no scroll cap. `OutlierPointsList.tsx`'s chip container gained `max-h-40 overflow-y-auto`; all 8 call sites' shared `formatLabel` pattern changed from `'d MMM'` to `'d MMM yyyy'`. Confirmed on-device. **#389** (follow-up to #372): reported live twice — first "cannot reproduce" from two Playwright tests confirming `/history?date=X` correctly filters+auto-expands `DayDetail`, but the user then clarified directly: they want **Today**, not History — "You have to navigate to Today tab with selected date." `TodayScreen.tsx` already has its own full `?date=` deep-link mechanism (the same one its own Previous/Next day arrows use, `searchParams.get('date') ?? todayIso()`), rendering the complete editable `DailyEntryForm` for any date, not just a read-only summary. Retargeted the link from `/history?date=X` to `/?date=X`; `viewOutlierDayLabel` reworded from "View X in History" to "Edit X" (en)/"Редактировать X" (ru), matching the now-actually-editable destination. Deliberately scoped to just this link — #41's own older chart-tap-to-navigate mechanism (`WeightTrendChart.tsx` etc.) still targets History unchanged, not part of this report. Verified live via Playwright: clicking a real flagged outlier now lands on `/?date=X` with the full `DailyEntryForm` (weight, meals, night-eating toggle, all directly editable). **#524**: chips/tooltips now say *why* a point was flagged — `classifyOutlierAxes` returns per-axis Tukey hits; `outlierReasonLabel.ts` maps that to "unusual weight change" / "unusual {metric}" / both (custom corrs pass both axis labels). `useOutlierExclusion` exposes `axes` alongside `flags`; Weight trend (#455) stays unlabeled (single-axis ±2kg rule, not 2D Tukey). |
| `stores/alcoholTrackingStore.ts` (new), `domain/stats/alcoholCorrelation.ts` (new), `AlcoholCorrelationView.tsx` (new) (#607) | Opt-in alcohol day signal + correlation view, deliberately built as a close mirror of digestion tracking (the field) and `nightEatingCorrelation.ts`/`NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx` (the correlation) rather than anything new — same shape/precedent throughout, per the issue's own explicit ask. `useAlcoholTrackingStore` — exact copy of `useDigestionTrackingStore`'s shape (off by default, local-only, not in the export bundle). `alcoholCorrelation.ts` — `alcoholPoints(entries)` pairs each day's logged `DailyEntry.hadAlcohol` (a plain stored boolean, no derivation needed — simpler than `hadNightEating()`, which derives from meal times) with the *next* calendar day's weight change, skipping a day with no logged value at all rather than folding it into "No" (same two-endpoints + "no silent default" shape every other day-pair correlation here uses since #394); `alcoholCorrelationFromPoints`/`alcoholCorrelation` do the identical plain two-group comparison `nightEatingCorrelationFromPoints` does (boolean predictor, no median split), `MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 8`, `DAILY_STRENGTH_THRESHOLDS_KG`. `AlcoholCorrelationView.tsx` — near-verbatim copy of `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx` (two fixed scatter columns, `useOutlierExclusion`, `ZoomableScatterSurface`, `OutlierPointsList`, `CorrelationStrengthLabel`), with one addition every other correlation view in this app doesn't need: an early `if (!alcoholTrackingEnabled \|\| rawPoints.length === 0) return null` — the *only* Settings-gated correlation view here, so toggling tracking off in Settings hides it completely regardless of already-logged historical data, not just "no data yet." Registered in `dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts`/`dashboardPeriodStore.ts`/`dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` like every other Dashboard section. Day-input UI mirrors `hadConstipation` exactly: a Yes/No `ToggleGroup` in `DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx`'s Evening group (gated by `state.alcoholTrackingEnabled`, also added to that group's `showEveningSection` visibility check) and a toggle `Button` in `DayDetail.tsx` (History), both behind the new Settings "Alcohol tracking" toggle (`SettingsScreen.tsx`'s unified "What to track" section, `UnifiedTrackedKey`'s `'alcohol'` case). `dailyEntryFormSchema.ts`/`dailyEntryFormMapping.ts`/`useDailyEntryFormState.ts` each gained the matching `hadAlcohol` plumbing (schema, entry↔form mapping, `alcoholTrackingEnabled`/`hadAlcohol`/`setHadAlcohol`). Exported three ways: `exportBundleSchema.ts`'s current `dailyEntrySchema` gained `hadAlcohol: z.boolean().optional()` (purely additive, no version bump — the legacy `dailyEntrySchemaV6` stays frozen, predates this field entirely by definition); `exportCsv.ts`/`exportXlsx.ts`/`exportMarkdown.ts` each gained a `hadAlcoholColumn` between Constipation and the night-eating column, same raw-optional-field shape `onPeriod`/`hadConstipation` already have there (unlike night eating's derived-value column). No new UI in `CalendarView.tsx` or `CustomChartView.tsx` — out of scope per the issue's own acceptance bullets (Settings toggle, Day logging, correlation view, export/import only). |
| `CustomCorrelationView.tsx`, `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` (new, `features/custom-metrics/`), `domain/customMetric/`, `domain/stats/customCorrelationEngine.ts` (#336) | One card per user-defined `CustomCorrelation`, rendered on `DashboardScreen.tsx` after every fixed reorderable section above (not part of `sectionsByKey`/`dashboardSectionOrderStore` — those are keyed by a fixed, known `DashboardChartKey` set, and retrofitting them for an unbounded user-defined list was judged bigger than this issue's core ask; deleting a correlation is this version's only "hide" mechanism). Otherwise reuses the exact same pattern every fixed correlation view already established: collapsed-until-there's-an-insight, `useOutlierExclusion`/`OutlierPointsList`/`CorrelationStrengthLabel` (all already generic enough to take straight, keyed `` `custom:${correlation.id}` ``), a `ScatterChart`. **Five design forks resolved via `AskUserQuestion`** (see `domain/customMetric/`'s own entry above for the value-type/cap/data-entry-location/pairing-shape decisions) — the concrete result of fork #5 (same-day, not next-day-delta pairing) is `customCorrelationEngine.ts`. `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` (reached from a Settings card, same "description + link button" shape `recipes`/food-list use) is where metrics/correlations are actually defined and logged — three sections: metric definitions (`AddMetricDialog.tsx`), per-date value entry (own date-nav header mirroring `TodayScreen.tsx`'s prev/next-day arrows, per design fork #3 — a `number` field commits on blur, `boolean`/`scale5` `ToggleGroup`s commit immediately on tap, same as this app's other single-tap pickers), and correlation definitions (`AddCorrelationDialog.tsx` — a plain native `<select>` per side spanning built-in + custom metrics; this app has no dedicated Select component and defining a correlation is rare enough not to warrant building one). `shared/lib/metricRefLabel.ts` resolves a `MetricRef` to its display label (a built-in field's translated name via `builtinMetricLabel`, reusing the exact same `t.*` keys `CustomChartView.tsx`'s own `useNumericSeriesConfig` already uses per key, or a custom metric's own name) — lives in `shared/lib` rather than either feature since both need it and this codebase doesn't otherwise let one feature import from another. **#363**: reported live — a logged value (e.g. "Acne = 3") had no way to attach context for why. New optional `CustomMetricEntry.note?: string` (purely additive, no Dexie version bump, added to `exportBundleSchema.ts`'s `customMetricEntrySchema` too). `customMetricStore.ts` gained `setEntryNote(metricId, date, note)` — amends an *existing* entry only (a no-op with nothing to attach a note to, since `value` stays required); `setEntryValue` now spreads `existing?.note` into the record it writes rather than constructing a bare new object, so changing just the value no longer silently drops a previously-saved note. `MetricValueRow`'s note `Input` renders below the value widget, gated on `value !== undefined` (same reasoning `value` being required already established). **#364** (reported live moments after confirming #363): the note's blur-only commit gave no visible sign it had actually saved. First pass replaced blur with an explicit always-editable Save button — reopened live as still not what was asked for ("implement it similar to the note of the day... there is read mode and edit mode"). The actual fix mirrors `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s day note's own read/edit-mode toggle exactly: `MetricValueRow` gained `isEditingNote` (lazy-initialized `useState(!note)`, same `!initialValues.note` logic the day note uses — nothing saved yet starts directly in edit mode, an already-saved note starts read-only). Edit mode is the `Input` + checkmark-`Button` pair (`size="icon-lg"`, matching the note field's own `h-9` height, the same "button lines up with its field" reasoning `button.tsx`'s own `icon-xl` comment documents for the full-height day-note case), committed on click or Enter, which also flips `isEditingNote` back to `false`. Read mode is plain text in a muted `rounded-lg` box plus a ghost pencil `Button` (`t.customMetrics.editNoteLabel`) reopening edit mode — same visual shape as the day note's own read-mode box, just without that one's `min-h-12`/wrap handling since a custom-metric note is expected to stay short. **#362**: reported live — the dedicated screen for logging a value read as "too complex" to remember to visit regularly, reversing this issue's own original "own dedicated screen" design fork (resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before building — direct evidence a resolved fork can still need revisiting once real usage surfaces a problem the abstract question didn't). `MetricValueRow` and the per-date value-entry list moved out of `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` into a new `CustomMetricLogSection.tsx` (same feature folder, exported from its barrel), mounted at the bottom of `TodayScreen.tsx` (after `DailyEntryForm`) using that screen's own currently-viewed `date` — so paging to a past day via Today's existing prev/next arrows logs values for *that* day, not literally "today." Renders nothing at all when no metrics are defined (`metrics.length === 0`), matching the "invisible until used" treatment other optional Today sections (BMI/BMR, sleep) already get. `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` kept its own date-nav-free two sections (metric definitions, correlation definitions — administrative tasks, not daily ones) plus a short note pointing to Today once at least one metric exists. **Real bug caught by the test suite, not anticipated while writing the change**: `<CustomMetricLogSection key={date} date={date} />` sat as a sibling of `<DailyEntryForm key={date} .../>` in `TodayScreen.tsx` — an identical `key` value on two sibling elements (regardless of their different types) is a duplicate key from React's reconciliation's point of view, corrupting rendering (`TodayScreen.test.tsx` failures: duplicate "Edit weight" buttons, a "two children with the same key" console warning). Fixed by dropping the unnecessary key from the new component entirely — it has no state of its own that needs remounting on a date change (only `MetricValueRow`, nested inside, does, via its own already-distinct `` `${metric.id}:${date}` `` key). |
| `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap.tsx` (#223) | GitHub-contribution-graph-style heatmap from `domain/stats/loggingConsistency.ts`'s `loggingConsistencyWeeks()` — one square per day, shaded by how many core fields were logged (0-4), `opacity: intensity / MAX_LOGGING_SIGNALS` against `var(--chart-weight)` (0 renders `var(--muted)` at full opacity instead, the "empty" cell). Rendered as a **vertical** stack of week-rows (most-recent-first, capped at the last 12 weeks), not GitHub's horizontal year-view — 7 columns × N rows fits a narrow phone screen without horizontal scrolling, which a year-wide calendar grid would need. A weekday-initial header row (`date-fns` `'EEEEE'` token, locale-aware) sits above the grid; each cell's native `title` attribute carries the exact date and score for hover/long-press detail rather than a custom tooltip component. A "Less → More" legend below mirrors the same 5-step opacity scale. Renders nothing with zero entries — nothing to anchor a week range to. **#268**: new `domain/stats/loggingConsistencySummary.ts` (`loggingConsistencySummary(entries, weeks, today?)`) computes three plain totals shown below the legend — days-logged count and total calories over the exact window of weeks already rendered (no separate range to reconcile with what's on screen), plus a separate total over a fixed trailing 7 days. Deliberately totals, not averages (distinct from `recentAverages`), and deliberately neutral/informational copy — a curiosity stat, not a guilt/streak metric. |
| `RecentAveragesCards.tsx` (#215, #506) | New `domain/stats/recentAverages.ts` powers this — a "Last 7 days" and "Last 30 days" `StatCard`, each average calories (kcal) anchored to the *real current date* (`new Date()`, overridable in tests/domain calls) counting back, not the most recent logged entry — a multi-day logging gap shrinks the sample rather than silently reusing stale data from whenever the user last logged. This is deliberately distinct from `WeeklySummaryCards`/`MonthlySummaryCards` below, which group by calendar week/month rather than a trailing window from today. Average protein appears as the description line (reusing `formatMacroGrams`); a window with zero qualifying days is simply omitted rather than rendered with a dash. **#506**: each card keeps its relative title and prepends the inclusive from→to calendar range on the description line (`recentAverageWindowRange` + `t.dashboard.weekRange` with `'PP'`, same formatting as weekly summary) so "last 7/30 days" maps to concrete dates; empty days remain in that shown range but are still skipped in the average denominator. Mounted on `DashboardScreen.tsx` right after `FoodReactionsView`, before the weekly/monthly cards. |
| `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx` | One `StatCard` per ISO week from `weeklySummaries()`, most-recent-first; asymmetric emphasis (bold loss, muted gain/no-change, #29) and no plus sign on gains (#34); a quiet "target met" note, deliberately not a badge. Description line also includes the week's average macros (#53, via shared `macrosSummaryText`) alongside average calories, when logged. **#556**: always buckets Monday–Sunday (`weekStartsOn: 1`), ignoring Settings' first-entry week-start preference (that still drives heatmaps/correlations/`MetTargetList`). **#562**: filters via `excludeIncompleteCurrentWeek` so the in-progress current Mon–Sun week is hidden until the next Monday (months / last-30-days unchanged). **#557**: each week card gains `WeeklyNoteEditor` (edit → textarea); notes in Dexie `weeklyNotes` keyed by Monday `weekStart`, JSON backup v8. **#565**: `WeeklyNoteEditor` is a `StatCard` child (inside the beige card, add control bottom-right) rather than a sibling below on the section white background — `StatCard` gained an optional `children` slot for this. **#571**: long notes use a collapsed 80-char preview with Show full note / Show less (Edit still available). **#373**: reported live — the week-range label (`'MMM d'` for both bounds) omitted the year, unreadable across the multi-year import. Added `, yyyy` to both the week-start and week-end `format()` calls here specifically, deliberately not touching the shared `t.dashboard.weekRange`/`t.common.weekRangeLabel` template functions or their other call sites (`MetTargetList`/`PastTargetsList`/`GoalScreen`/`TodayScreen`), which each only ever show a single current/recent week, not a multi-year list. **#379**: reported live right after #373 — across ~6 years of imported data this renders 300+ cards in one unbroken vertical list. Gained the same `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto` scroll-container treatment #374 already gave the outlier chip lists, chosen over pagination for the same reason (smaller change, no new interactive state). Both #373 and #379 confirmed on-device. **#395**: reported live — every card renders only its date label, the value/description missing entirely. First theory (a WebKit-only compositing bug, `will-change-transform` mitigation) was confirmed on-device **not fixed** — a second live investigation (this time reproduced directly in a real Chromium instance against a real seeded dataset, ruling out the Safari-only theory entirely) found the real cause via live DevTools: it's a well-known CSS flexbox spec behavior, not a browser-specific compositing quirk. `Card`'s own base styles include `overflow-hidden` (needed elsewhere to clip a leading/trailing image's square corners to the card's rounded ones) — per spec, a flex item's *automatic minimum size* resolves to content-based (`auto`) only when its own `overflow` is `visible`; any other value (here, `hidden`) makes it resolve to `0` instead. Inside the `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto` scrollable flex-column list of many `StatCard`s (#379), that let a card's box shrink below its actual content height under real layout pressure — `overflow-hidden` then silently clipped the value/description text that no longer fit, with the date label (the first line, needing the least height) surviving. Confirmed interactively via DevTools on the user's own real broken case: toggling off `overflow-hidden` on the spot made the clipped value reappear immediately. Fixed at the `StatCard` level itself (`shared/ui/stat-card.tsx`), not per-caller: `<Card className={cn('overflow-visible', className)}>` overrides the inherited `overflow-hidden` for every current/future `StatCard` usage — safe everywhere, since `StatCard` never renders an image and so never needed the corner-clipping behavior to begin with. The now-confirmed-ineffective `will-change-transform` mitigation was removed from both `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx`/`MonthlySummaryCards.tsx` (dead weight — the user directly confirmed toggling it off changed nothing). Root cause and fix mechanism confirmed live via interactive DevTools testing against the user's own real broken case (not yet a full validated pass on the deployed build). **#483**: description also leads with the prior→current period averages that define the delta (`t.goal.previousToCurrentWeightLabel`, same shape MetTargetList/#408 already uses), omitted when delta is null. |
| `MonthlySummaryCards.tsx` (#226) | Same shape/component structure as `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx` above, reading from the new `domain/stats/monthlySummaries.ts` instead — one `StatCard` per calendar month, most-recent-first, same asymmetric bold/muted emphasis and average-macros description line. Deliberately **no** "target met" note, unlike the weekly card: a `Goal` in this app is always a *weekly* target (`targetWeeklyLossKg`), and there's no existing monthly-target concept to compare against — summing several weeks' worth of targets into a rough monthly figure would be inventing a comparison rather than reusing one that already exists elsewhere. No `weekStartsOn`-style preference either, since a calendar month has one unambiguous boundary a week doesn't. Rendered on `DashboardScreen.tsx` directly below `WeeklySummaryCards`. **#373**: its own `'MMMM yyyy'` month format already included the year, unlike `WeeklySummaryCards`' — no change needed here. **#379**: confirmed the same unbounded-list gap as `WeeklySummaryCards` (just ~12x fewer rows per year in practice) — given the identical `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto` treatment anyway, for consistency. Confirmed on-device. **#395**: same real fix as `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx` above (`overflow-visible` at the `StatCard` level, `will-change-transform` removed) — see that entry for the full root-cause explanation. **#483**: same prior→current averages line as the weekly cards. |
| `WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `MacroTrendChart.tsx`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`, `ElectrolyteTrendChart.tsx`, `domain/stats/trendChartPeriod.ts`, `stores/dashboardPeriodStore.ts`, `DashboardPeriodPicker.tsx`, `useChartPeriodPager.ts` (new), `ChartPeriodPagerControls.tsx` (new) (#380) | Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: one global control, mirroring #240's export-period shape, rather than a per-chart picker. New `trendChartPeriod.ts` (`resolveTrendChartPeriodRange`/`filterEntriesByTrendChartPeriod`) computes **rolling** windows anchored to today, not calendar-aligned — "last 7/30/365 days," not "this calendar week/month/year." New persisted `useDashboardPeriodStore` (`'all'` default, matching every chart's pre-#380 behavior exactly for anyone who never touches the picker). New `DashboardPeriodPicker.tsx` — a 5-option single-select (All time/Week/Month/Year/Custom), date inputs only shown for Custom. **Originally scoped to just the 4 main trend charts** (Weight/Calorie/Macro/Body composition) — deliberately excluded the 6 correlation views (their day/week-count caveats describe a full-history sample; narrowing the input would silently change what the statistic means) and `CustomChartView`. `DashboardScreen.tsx` computed one filtered `trendChartEntries` array reused by all 4 chart props; every other section kept reading the original unfiltered `entries`. Confirmed on-device. **#396** reversed that scope decision — reported live ("Setting graph range didn't affect this graph and other graphs") right after confirming #380, then resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: apply the picker everywhere, accepting the smaller-sample trade-off for correlations too, same shape #370 already chose over #240's original always-complete-backup decision. `trendChartEntries` renamed to `periodFilteredEntries` (no longer trend-chart-specific) and wired into `CustomChartView` plus all 8 correlation views (`CorrelationView`, `LateMealCorrelationView`, `MealFrequencyCorrelationView`, `FastingWindowCorrelationView`, `SleepCorrelationView`, `StepsCorrelationView`, `ProteinCorrelationView`, `NightEatingCorrelationView`) — every Dashboard section that reads `entries` at all, except `FoodReactionsView`/`LoggingConsistencyHeatmap`/`RecentAveragesCards`/`WeeklySummaryCards`/`MonthlySummaryCards`/`CompareRangesView`, which stayed outside this issue's own filed scope. New test confirms a correlation view (calorie-vs-weight) disappears under a narrowed period the same way the trend charts already did, while `WeeklySummaryCards` stays unaffected. **#443**: requested live — no way to page to the previous/next week/month/year, only this fixed picker. Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: the period *type* stays this one shared control, but *which window* of that type each of the 4 main trend charts shows is each chart's own independent local state (paging Weight back a week leaves Calorie/Macro/Body composition on the current week) — arrows render in each chart's own footer, not as a 5th shared control next to the picker. New `useChartPeriodPager(period, customStart, customEnd, entries, today?)` hook (local `periodsBack` state, reset to 0 during render when `period` itself changes — compared against a stored previous value rather than a `useEffect`, avoiding React's anti-cascading-render lint rule for synchronous `setState` in an effect) shifts the anchor date back by one window's worth of days (7/30/365) via `resolveTrendChartPeriodRange`'s existing injectable `today` param; 'all'/'custom' (`isPageableTrendChartPeriod`) reduce to exactly the old passthrough, so a caller that never passes `period` (every pre-#443 test) is unaffected. New shared `ChartPeriodPagerControls.tsx` (prev/next `ChevronLeft`/`ChevronRight` icon buttons + a `dd.MM.yy` range label, matching #444's own fixed-format fix for the identical narrow-space class of bug) renders in all 4 charts' footers, `showPager`-gated so it's invisible for 'all'/'custom'. `DashboardScreen.tsx` now passes the *full* `entries` (not `periodFilteredEntries`) plus `period`/`customStart`/`customEnd` to just these 4 — every other section is unaffected. Each chart's own "zero points" early return was narrowed to only skip rendering entirely when paging is inactive; when genuinely paged into an empty window, it now still renders the title + pager so the user isn't stranded with no way back — a real dead-end this feature would otherwise have introduced. Verified live via Playwright (3 distinct seeded weeks): paging Weight's own arrow back a week left the other 3 charts on the current week, confirming true per-chart independence. **#536**: reversed #380's "one global picker" decision — removed the page-level `DashboardPeriodPicker`; each period-scoped section (trends + correlations + custom chart) now owns its own All/Week/Month/Year/Custom under `ChartTitleWithToggle`, persisted in `dashboardPeriodStore.byChart` (legacy global shape migrates once onto every key). No dual global+individual mode; summaries/heatmaps/compare still ignore period. **#537**: after #536, switching one chart's period felt ~10s slow — root cause was `DashboardScreen` subscribing to the whole `byChart` map, so one `setPeriod` re-rendered every Dashboard section (weekly cards, heatmaps, every other Recharts chart) over multi-year data. Fix: `useDashboardChartPeriod` / `usePeriodFilteredEntries` so each chart/correlation reads only its own store key; `DashboardScreen` no longer touches the period store. Optional `period`/`customStart`/`customEnd` props remain as test overrides only. **#560**: #543's pinch-zoom / horizontal pan / double-tap-reset (`useChartGestureZoom` + `sliceByZoomWindow`) extended from Compare Data alone to all five Dashboard time-series trend charts — `WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `MacroTrendChart.tsx`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`, `ElectrolyteTrendChart.tsx` — same `touch-pan-y` surface wrapper, `data-point-count`, zoom hint, and Reset zoom button (`customChartZoomHint` / `customChartResetZoomButton` i18n keys reused). Correlation scatter charts deliberately unchanged in #560. **#581**: scatters get their own domain-based zoom (`scatterGestureZoom.ts` + `useScatterGestureZoom` + shared `ZoomableScatterSurface`) — pinch / 2D pan / double-tap + Reset zoom on all nine correlation views — because index `sliceByZoomWindow` doesn't fit x/y clouds. Steps/late-meal keep capped/fixed full x domains via `fullDomainOverride`. **#572**: tooltip suppressed while `isGesturing`; wrapper stays `pointer-events: none` (day link re-enabled) so pinch can pass through the floating tooltip. |
| `WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `OutlierPointsList.tsx`, `stores/outlierExclusionStore.ts` (#455) | Lets the user dismiss a red-flagged point (the day-over-day ±2kg rule #441/#448 established) from that flag, the same tap-to-exclude idea the 6 correlation views already offer via `useOutlierExclusion`/`OutlierPointsList.tsx`. Reuses the *store* (`useOutlierExclusionStore` — a plain `excluded[viewKey][pointKey] = true` map, persisted to localStorage) and `OutlierPointsList.tsx` (the chip-list UI) directly rather than the `useOutlierExclusion` hook itself — that hook's own flagging is `flagOutliers`, 2D Tukey's-fences over an (X, Y) pair, but this chart's #448 flagging is a flat single-axis day-over-day delta, a different shape the hook doesn't fit; only the store and the list component generalize. One stable view key (`'weightTrend'`, since there's only ever one Weight trend chart). An excluded point's dot renders dimmed (`opacity: 0.3`, `outlierScatterShape.tsx`'s exact treatment for an excluded correlation point) rather than disappearing from the chart. Deliberately doesn't cascade: excluding a point doesn't touch the rolling-average line, and doesn't stop a *neighboring* day's own delta from being flagged (that neighbor still compares against the excluded point's real logged value as "yesterday") — kept to the literal ask ("similar to how it is already implemented on the correlation charts") rather than inventing new cross-point semantics nothing requested. The dot's own dimmed rendering isn't unit-testable (Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom) — verified live via a seeded Playwright script instead (3 real days logged through the actual UI, one flagged circle confirmed at `opacity: 1`, dropped to `opacity: 0.3` after clicking Exclude, chip flipped to "Restore"). New tests cover the Exclude chip's presence/absence and the Exclude/Restore toggle round-trip. `tsc -b`/lint/`WeightTrendChart.test.tsx` (16 tests) green. **#615**: one-line factual note (`t.dashboard.cyclePeriodWeightNote`) below the chart-navigation hint, gated on `useCycleTrackingStore().enabled` only — a template fact ("weight often fluctuates around your period"), not a computed prediction, no ovulation/fertile-window logic, cycle-off users see nothing new. Audited History's `CalendarView.tsx` legend (#482/#485) for the issue's other ask ("legend only for types in use") — already correctly gated per-tracking-type, no change needed there. |
| `CorrelationChartTooltip.tsx` (new), `dayNotePreview.ts` (new), all 9 correlation scatter views (#489, #540) | Reported live with screenshots: tapping a correlation scatter point showed its values and nothing else, so a point that wasn't flagged as an outlier had no route to its day at all — navigation only existed on `OutlierPointsList`'s chips (#372/#389). Every view's plain `<Tooltip formatter={...} contentStyle={...} />` became `<Tooltip content={<CorrelationChartTooltip formatValue={...} />} wrapperStyle={{ pointerEvents: 'auto' }} />`: one shared tooltip component rendering a date header, each axis's value through that view's own existing formatting, and a `t.dashboard.viewDayLink` → `/?date=X` control, matching what `WeightTrendChart` etc. already do (#442). Shared rather than nine copies of the markup — but only the *chrome* is shared; `formatValue` stays per-view, since the units differ wildly (steps, hours, kcal, a Yes/No label). The target date comes from a new `date` field on each view's `Scatter` data objects (Recharts hands the whole data object back on every tooltip payload entry); `CorrelationView`'s weekly points use `weekStart`, and a point with no resolvable date renders no control. Carries over both non-obvious bits from #442/#33 — the `wrapperStyle` pointer-events override at each call site, and `onMouseMove`/`onTouchMove` `stopPropagation()` inside the tooltip. **On-device follow-up:** a plain `<Link>` only dismissed the tooltip (Recharts clears `active` on the touch before click can navigate). The open-day control is now a button that `navigate()`s on `pointerdown` (with stopPropagation on the tooltip box), so the route change wins against tooltip teardown. **#540**: truncated Day-note preview (`dayNotePreview` / `dayNotesByDate`) on tooltips and `OutlierPointsList` chips when that date has a note (option 1; no dedicated oddity field). |
| `CompareRangesView.tsx` (#222) | Two independent date-range pickers (native `<input type="date">` start/end pairs, plain `useState`, no persisted selection) plus a side-by-side `StatCard` pair from `domain/stats/dateRangeSummary.ts`'s two summaries — average weight/calories/macros and a "N days logged" count per range, same description-line shape `WeeklySummaryCards`/`MonthlySummaryCards` already use (`macrosSummaryText`). Defaults to this month vs. last month (`isoMonthRange()`, `date-fns` `startOfMonth`/`endOfMonth`/`subMonths`), matching the issue's own "this month vs. last month" example, so the view shows something meaningful before either picker is touched. A signed weight-delta line (`formatSignedNumber`, "Range B averaged +X kg vs. Range A") below the cards — deliberately plain `+`/`-`, not the asymmetric bold-loss/muted-gain treatment `WeeklySummaryCards` uses, since that framing assumes a chronological week-over-week weight-loss goal; two arbitrary user-picked ranges have no inherent "good direction" to color-code. Renders nothing with zero entries. Mounted on `DashboardScreen.tsx` as the last item, after `MonthlySummaryCards`. |
| `CustomChartView.tsx` (#132, #137) | Checkboxes (Radix `ToggleGroup type="multiple"`, `shared/ui/toggle-group.tsx`) toggle which logged series overlay on one chart — weight, calories, protein, fat, carbs, steps, plus "On period"/"Constipation" when those opt-in trackers are enabled (`useCycleTrackingStore`/`useDigestionTrackingStore`). Weight/calories/macros/steps don't share a Y-axis (kg vs. kcal vs. g vs. step count), so each is normalized to 0-100 within its own min/max range via `domain/stats/customChartSeries.ts`'s `customChartPoints()` rather than given a separate axis (unreadable past two or three on mobile) — the tooltip and legend always read the real number from each point's `raw` values, never the normalized one. An explicit caveat sentence below the chart says so too, since a normalized chart can otherwise misread as showing absolute values. The chart is a `ComposedChart` (#137) so each selected numeric series can independently be a `Line`, `Bar`, or "dots" — a small per-series line/bar/dots `ToggleGroup type="single"` (icon buttons, `role="radiogroup"`) sits next to that series' swatch in the legend, defaulting to line. "Dots" isn't a `Scatter` — it's a `Line` with `stroke="transparent"` and a visible `dot`, so it shares the existing category x-axis with zero extra axis wiring. Period/constipation are on/off flags, not trends, so they always render as a dot pinned to the top of the chart (`BOOLEAN_MARKER_Y = 100`, same transparent-line trick) on each flagged day — colors are `var(--destructive)` / the literal `#f59e0b` matching `CalendarView`'s `bg-amber-500` dot (no CSS token exists for that one) — replacing an earlier full-height `ReferenceLine` per flagged day that read as noisier. Steps has no dedicated `--chart-*` token the way weight/calories/protein/fat/carbs do, so it reuses one of the design system's otherwise-unused generic `--chart-1` slots. No deep-link-to-History-on-tap here, unlike the other charts — deliberately kept out of scope for the first pass. Renders nothing with zero entries; with entries but zero series selected, shows a "pick at least one" message instead of an empty chart. **#195**: the toggled series (`selectedNumeric`/`selectedBoolean`) and each series' chart-type mode (`chartTypes`) moved from local `useState` to a new `useCustomChartSelectionStore` (`stores/customChartSelectionStore.ts`, localStorage-persisted via `zustand/persist`, same shape as `weekStartStore`/`useUnitStore`) — previously the whole selection silently reset to weight+calories/all-lines every time Dashboard was left and revisited. `ChartSeriesType` and its `DEFAULT_CHART_TYPES` moved into the store file too, since the store now owns that default state; `CustomChartView.tsx` imports the type from `@/stores` instead of declaring it locally. **#198**: not a data bug — whichever day happens to be the visible range's *lowest* value for a series normalizes to exactly 0 (min-max normalization is per-series, per-window), so its bar rendered at zero height and read as "nothing logged" even with a real, just relatively low, number behind it. `<Bar>` gained `minPointSize={3}` (recharts' own documented fix for this) so every bar stays at least a few px tall; `raw`/the tooltip still always show the real value, unaffected. **#330**: a deliberate, narrow exception to the "no per-series axis" rule above — confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before building, since the ask directly touched that documented tradeoff. When exactly `selectedNumeric.length === 2` (`isDualAxis`), those two series switch their plotted `dataKey` from `${key}_norm` to the already-carried `${key}_raw` and get real `yAxisId="left"`/`"right"` axes (Recharts default `'auto'` domain, `width="auto"`, tick/axis-line color matching that series' own color, `tickFormatter` reusing `formatRaw`) instead of the shared hidden one. Any other count (0, 1, or 3+) is unchanged — the shared hidden `yAxisId="normalized"` axis still carries boolean markers always, and the numeric series too whenever `isDualAxis` is false, exactly as before. The `customChartNormalizedCaveat` sentence is now factually wrong for the 2-series case (real axes *are* absolute), so it's hidden specifically when `isDualAxis` is true. Recharts renders zero SVG internals under jsdom's 0×0 `ResponsiveContainer` (confirmed empirically — existing tests never assert on chart internals for this reason), so the actual dual-axis rendering was live-verified via a seeded Playwright script rather than a unit test; the caveat-visibility branch (a plain `<p>`, not inside the SVG) does have real `CustomChartView.test.tsx` coverage. **#332**: #330's "Recharts default `'auto'` domain" note above was wrong — Recharts' actual default `domain` is `[0, 'auto']`, so both real axes were silently forced to start at 0 regardless of the series' real minimum (reported live: a 68-72kg weight range still drew its axis down to 0.0 kg). Fixed by setting `domain={['auto', 'auto']}` on both `left`/`right` `YAxis` elements — same domain `WeightTrendChart.tsx` already uses for its own real weight axis — so each now scales from its own data's min to max. `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`'s independent dual-axis implementation (#277, below) had the identical unset-domain bug and got the identical fix in the same pass, since it's the same root cause in a second component. **#347**: reported live — fastingHours (switched to bar mode) rendered in solid black. Not a missing color: `--chart-5`, the last of the five generic achromatic `--chart-1..5` slots (see #325's entry above), is fine as a thin *line* but its own lightness (oklch L=0.269) reads as literal black once `<Bar fill={...}>` fills it solid. Fixed by giving fastingHours its own dedicated `--chart-fasting` token (a muted indigo) instead of lightening the shared grayscale ramp in place, which would only push the same "looks black as a bar" risk onto whichever of steps/waist/hip/bodyFat gets switched to bar mode next — `--chart-1..4` are untouched. **#350**: reported live right after validating #347 — bodyFat, also switched to bar mode, rendered black too. Same root cause: `--chart-4` (`oklch(0.371 0 0)`) is the second-darkest of the same five slots. Same fix — a new dedicated `--chart-bodyfat` token (teal-forest green). Checked waist/hip (`--chart-2`/`--chart-3`) as the issue suggested; left alone — their lightness (0.556/0.439) sits meaningfully closer to `--chart-1`'s already-fine gray than to `--chart-4`/`--chart-5`'s confirmed-too-dark end, and neither was actually reported. **#351**: reported live — waist/hip stayed offered in the checkbox picker even with "Body measurements" tracking disabled in Settings, unlike the boolean period/constipation series above, which already gate on their own opt-in trackers. New `availableNumericKeys` filter (`NUMERIC_SERIES_KEYS.filter(...)`, mirroring `availableBooleanSeries`'s existing shape) — waist/hip gate on `trackedFields.bodyMeasurements`, bodyFat gates on `trackedFields.bodyComposition` instead: `DailyEntryForm.tsx` moved `bodyFatPercent`'s own form field into the "Body composition" section back in #263, even though it's stored on the same `DailyEntry` as waist/hip, so the two fields now have genuinely different gates despite sharing a domain-model comment block. Matches the user's exact report (Body measurements off, Body composition on — only waist/hip should vanish, not body fat). An already-selected key stays selected if tracking is turned off afterward; the filter only changes what's offered going forward, same as the boolean series. **#384**: same root cause as #376 — `allDates` spanned every logged entry's date (via `customChartPoints()`, itself unfiltered by selection) plus every custom metric date, regardless of which series were actually selected, so a sparse/recently-added series rendered mostly as blank space across whatever range the rest of the app's data spans. Fixed with a new `relevantDates` set — union of (dates where at least one *selected* numeric series has a value) ∪ (selected boolean markers' own flagged dates) ∪ (selected custom metrics' own logged dates) — so the x-axis only spans where the current selection actually has something to draw. Confirmed on-device. **#371**: user-defined custom metrics (#336) are now selectable series here too, reusing #336's own `resolveMetricValueMap()` lookup — new `selectedCustomMetricIds` in `customChartSelectionStore.ts`, own chips cycling through the 5 generic `--chart-1..5` tokens by selection order. Always plots as a plain line on the shared normalized axis — deliberately never participates in #330's dual-real-axis exactly-2 case (a real, known v1 limitation, not yet extended). Confirmed on-device. **#391**: the "never participates in the per-series bar/dots toggle" half of that v1 trim, specifically, got lifted — chart type turned out to be purely a rendering choice, independent of axis assignment, so it extends cleanly to an unbounded id set the same dual-axis participation genuinely can't. `customChartSelectionStore.ts`'s `chartTypes` widened from `Record<NumericSeriesKey, ChartSeriesType>` to `Record<string, ChartSeriesType>` (`setChartType`'s key param widened the same way); a metric id with no entry yet defaults to `'line'` at the read site (`chartTypes[metricId] ?? 'line'`), same default every `NumericSeriesKey` already gets via `DEFAULT_CHART_TYPES`. Each selected custom metric's legend row gained the identical line/bar/dots `ToggleGroup` built-in series already have; Bar/dots-Line/line-Line render the same three-way branch as the built-in series' own, still always on `yAxisId="normalized"` — only the visual style changes, never the axis. New test covers the toggle appearing, defaulting to line, and switching to bar. **#393**: reported live — exactly 1 series selected showed no y-axis at all, same gap #348 already fixed for `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`. New `isSingleAxis` (`selectedNumeric.length === 1`), symmetric to `isDualAxis` and independent of boolean-marker/custom-metric selection the same way — those keep using the always-present `yAxisId="normalized"` regardless. The sole selected series gets a real `YAxis` (`yAxisId="single"`, `domain={['auto', 'auto']}`, tick/axis-line colored to that series, `tickFormatter` reusing `formatRaw`) and its `dataKey`/`yAxisId` switch to `_raw`/`'single'` the same way the dual-axis pair's do — `Bar`/`Line`/dots-`Line` all read the same computed `dataKey`/`yAxisId`, so no per-chart-type branching needed. Caveat-visibility condition extended from `!isDualAxis` to `!isDualAxis && !isSingleAxis` (still shown whenever a custom metric is selected, same #371 reasoning). Existing test asserting the caveat stayed visible with 1 series selected was itself testing the reported bug's behavior — inverted to assert it's now hidden, plus a new test confirming it reappears once a custom metric joins a lone series (mirroring the existing dual-axis+custom-metric test). **Confirmed still broken on-device after that fix** — the caveat-visibility logic was right, but the axis itself still didn't render. Root-caused via a real seeded Playwright run against the dev server (jsdom renders zero SVG internals for Recharts, so this class of bug is invisible to the unit-test suite entirely): the "single" axis was rendering with negative x-coordinates, off-canvas to the left of the SVG. Cause: the always-present hidden `yAxisId="normalized"` axis (used for the shared 0-100 range and boolean markers) defaults to `orientation="left"`, same as the new "single" axis — two same-side y-axes, even though one is invisible, confuses Recharts' `width="auto"` margin-auto-expansion measurement. The dual-axis "left"/"right" pair never hit this, since neither shares a side with the hidden one. Fixed by explicitly giving the hidden `normalized` axis `orientation="right"` instead, so it no longer competes with "single" for left-side margin space — verified live afterward (positive coordinates, axis visible) and confirmed the dual-axis pair + boolean-marker dots (sharing the same `yAxisId="normalized"`) are unaffected, since orientation only changes which side an axis's *own labels* draw on, not the underlying data-to-pixel mapping. No unit test added for the coordinate fix itself (same jsdom limitation as above); existing 197-test dashboard suite confirmed no regression. **#444**: reported live with 2 screenshots — Weight+Carbs selected showed no right Y-axis at all, while Weight+Calories showed one (barely). Root cause: the always-present hidden `normalized` axis is `orientation="right"`, and the dual-axis pair's own "right" key is *also* `orientation="right"` — two same-side axes confuse Recharts' `width="auto"` margin-auto-expansion measurement, the identical bug class #393 already diagnosed for the single-axis case (there, fixed by flipping the hidden axis's orientation — not repeatable here, since that orientation is already load-bearing for #393's own fix). Instead, all three real axes (`left`/`right`/`single`) switched from `width="auto"` to a fixed `width={40}`, sidestepping the auto-measurement conflict entirely — the same fix `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`'s own dual axis already uses. Confirmed live via Playwright DOM measurement: before the fix, the right axis's position depended on which series was selected (Calories barely on-canvas, Carbs 16px past the edge); after, both measure identically regardless of series. **This "fix" turned out incomplete** — reopened live again (Weight+Sleep, no right-axis values at all), and the user directly reproduced it in Chrome's own device emulator with an up-to-date checkout, then provided the actual missing piece via a DevTools screenshot: the right axis's tick-label group had a real, non-zero bounding box (text genuinely rendering) but positioned entirely outside the visible container — not a text-generation failure as the "consistent x=373 regardless of series" measurement above had wrongly been read as "fixed." **Real root cause**: the hidden `normalized` axis (`orientation="right"`, `hide`) had no `width` set, defaulting to Recharts' own ~60px default — even invisible, this still counted as a *third* right-side `YAxis` in Recharts' own layout stacking, pushing the real visible "right" axis outward by that phantom width. The earlier `width={40}` change only fixed the two *visible* axes' own reported width; it never accounted for this hidden third one silently claiming space too. Fixed by giving the hidden `normalized` axis `width={0}`. Re-verified live via Playwright, this time checking the real axis line + tick text position against the SVG's actual bounding box (not just cross-combo consistency, the earlier mistake): right axis line and tick text now comfortably within the container, zero overflow. **Follow-up in the same pass**: with both axes now actually rendering, the X-axis date labels (`'PP'`, e.g. "8 июл. 2026 г.") overlapped in the now-narrower plot area — switched to a fixed `dd.MM.yy` format ("16.07.26"), short enough to fit, with no locale-dependent month name needing `date-fns`'s locale option. **#445**: reported live — the fastingHours series' toggle-button label read lowercase "часов голодания" (correct as a sentence-tail phrase, e.g. after a number) instead of a proper standalone label, because its `label` reused `t.dashboard.fastingHoursLegend` directly — a key also used as-is by `FastingWindowCorrelationView.tsx`'s chart legend and `metricRefLabel.ts`, correct in those two contexts only. Fixed with its own dedicated `customChartFastingHoursLabel` (en "Fasting hours"/ru "Часы голодания"), same naming convention `customChartWeightLabel`/`customChartCaloriesLabel` already use — `fastingHoursLegend` itself is unchanged. **#440**: Sleep added as a new toggleable series — `sleep` (`entry.sleepHours`), a plain `SERIES_EXTRACTORS` entry same shape as steps/waist/hip/bodyFat, reusing `t.dailyEntry.sleepLabel` directly (already Title-case, same convention as Protein/Fat/Carbs/Steps). Given its own dedicated `--chart-sleep` token instead of a freed-up generic `--chart-4`/`--chart-5` slot (those two are the confirmed-renders-black-in-bar-mode slots #347/#350 already moved fastingHours/bodyFat off of) — sleep gets the identical per-series bar/dots toggle, so reusing either would risk the same bug a third time. **#451**: reported live with a screenshot — every Y-axis tick repeats its series' unit (e.g. "60,6 кг" on every single tick), cluttering the axis. Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: moved consistently for every series that has one (not just Weight/Water, which the screenshot happened to point at) — `useNumericSeriesConfig` gained a new `formatAxisTick` (bare number only) used by all 3 `YAxis` `tickFormatter` call sites, plus an optional `unit` field appended to that series' own selection `ToggleGroupItem` label as `` `${label} (${unit})` ``. `formatRaw` itself is untouched, still unit-suffixed — it's the tooltip/legend's per-point value text, a different, non-repeating context the report never flagged. Steps/Sleep have no `unit` (never had a suffix), so their toggle buttons and axis ticks are both unchanged. Verified live via Playwright: axis ticks render as bare numbers ("79.2"–"80.0") while the toggle buttons read "Weight (kg)"/"Calories (kcal)"/etc. **#453**: follow-up filed right after confirming #443 on-device — the 4 main trend charts page independently now, but this chart had no arrows at all, deliberately out of #443's own scope (no "Tap a point" footer hint to anchor them next to). Extended the same generic `useChartPeriodPager`/`ChartPeriodPagerControls` (#443) here: gained `period`/`customStart`/`customEnd` props (all defaulting so every pre-#453 caller/test is unaffected), calls the hook and aliases its `pagedEntries` to what was the raw `entries` prop, renders the pager controls as this card's own last child (no hint text to sit next to, so it's simply appended). `DashboardScreen.tsx` passes the full `entries` + period info here too now, same as the 4 trend charts. Same "don't strand the user in an empty paged window" fix applied to the `entries.length === 0` branch. Verified live via Playwright: paging only this chart's own arrow back a week left the other 4 charts' pagers on the current week, confirming the same per-chart independence #443 established. **#502**: reported live — Weight + "On period" over a multi-year range drew a thick continuous red band along the top, reading as non-stop menstruation for years. Three of the four directions the issue itself proposed were already the shipped behavior (dots-only, `connectNulls={false}` with a transparent stroke, and no chart-type picker for boolean series); the real cause was dot *density*, since each flagged day's 8px-wide `r: 4` dot vastly overhangs the sub-pixel width of a day on that axis, so neighbours merged. Resolved with the user (`AskQuestion`) as "keep dots, group several days into one," not thin per-day ticks or hiding markers when zoomed out, and staying at the top of the chart. `${key}_marker` rows are now populated from `booleanFlagMarkers(sortedDates, flaggedDates, MAX_BOOLEAN_MARKERS)` (see `customChartSeries.ts` above) instead of from the raw flagged-date set — `MAX_BOOLEAN_MARKERS = 24` is derived from the ~330px phone plot area and the 8px dot size, so neighbouring dots keep visible background between them. `relevantDates` still unions *all* flagged dates (not just marker dates), so grouping changes only which dates draw a dot, never the x-axis span. Two supporting additions: boolean flags gained a tooltip row at all — they never had one, and a grouped dot is unreadable without the `N days` count (`customChartMarkerDaysText`, reusing ru.ts's existing `dayCount()`/`ruPluralize`) — and a `customChartGroupedMarkersCaveat` line renders below the chart *only* while some dot is actually covering more than one day, so a short range says nothing about grouping. Coverage is the caveat's visibility branch (a plain `<p>`, testable under jsdom) plus direct unit tests of `booleanFlagMarkers`; the dot rendering itself is subject to the same jsdom-renders-no-Recharts-internals limitation as everything else in this row. **#543**: pinch-zoom / horizontal pan / double-tap-reset on Compare Data (`chartGestureZoom.ts` + `useChartGestureZoom`, non-passive touchmove; vertical page scroll kept; Reset zoom button + hint while zoomed). **#560** extended the same gesture stack to all five Dashboard time-series trend charts (see the Weight/Calorie/Macro/Body-comp row above); correlation scatters unchanged. |
| `CustomChartView.tsx`, `domain/stats/customChartSeries.ts`, `stores/customChartSelectionStore.ts` (#225) | Three new toggleable series — waist, hip, body fat — wired the same way steps was: a new `SERIES_EXTRACTORS` entry each in `customChartSeries.ts` (reading `DailyEntry.waistCm`/`hipCm`/`bodyFatPercent`), a `useNumericSeriesConfig()` entry each in `CustomChartView.tsx`, and a `DEFAULT_CHART_TYPES` entry each (`'line'`) in `customChartSelectionStore.ts` — the `Record<NumericSeriesKey, ChartSeriesType>` type means omitting any of the three there would be a compile error, not a silent gap. Colors reuse the next three otherwise-unused generic `--chart-2`/`--chart-3`/`--chart-4` slots, same reasoning as steps' `--chart-1`. |
| `CustomChartView.tsx` (#236) | The per-series line/bar/dots `ToggleGroupItem`s were `h-auto px-1 py-0.5` around a `size-3` icon (~16px total). A first attempt (`h-7`/`size-3.5`, reasoned from `button.tsx`'s `icon-sm` "dense inline row control" precedent) was reported still too small live. Reopened, then fixed for real by removing the custom size override entirely — the `ToggleGroupItem`s now use the exact same unstyled defaults (`px-3 py-1.5 text-sm`, ~32px) as the "Compare your data" series-picker buttons directly above them, guaranteeing pixel-identical sizing to the control the user pointed at rather than another guessed value. Confirmed by the user directly in the browser before closing. |
| `CustomChartView.tsx`, `domain/stats/customChartSeries.ts`, `stores/customChartSelectionStore.ts` (#325) | A fourth toggleable series, water — wired the same way waist/hip/body fat were in #225: a `SERIES_EXTRACTORS` entry reading `totalWaterMl(entry.waterEntries)` (#258/#271's existing helper), a `useNumericSeriesConfig()` entry, and a `DEFAULT_CHART_TYPES` entry (`'line'`). Color is the one departure from that #225 precedent: all five generic `--chart-1..5` slots were already claimed (steps/waist/hip/bodyFat/fastingHours), so there was no unused one left to hand water. Reuses `--stat-water` directly instead — water's existing mood-independent identity color from #320's Today progress bar — same reasoning #323 used to give the calories progress bar `--chart-calories` directly rather than mint an unaudited new token. |
| `FoodReactionsView.tsx` (#128) | Two ranked lists side by side (stacked on mobile via `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2`) — "Most liked" from `mostLikedFoods()`, "Most disliked" from `mostDislikedFoods()` (both `domain/stats/foodReactions.ts`). Each row shows the dish name and its reaction-type counts (emoji + number, `aria-hidden`, with an `sr-only` "Bellissimo × 2" pairing reusing `dailyEntry.mealEmotionLabel` rather than adding new translation surface just for this). Built directly on #129's per-item `emotion` — before that, one shared reaction per meal group couldn't tell a multi-item meal's dishes apart. Renders nothing when no item has ever been logged with a reaction (same empty treatment as `LateMealCorrelationView`); a food with a mixed history can legitimately appear in both lists at once, not deduplicated. |
| `chartNavigation.ts` | `resolveChartClickDate()` — resolves a Recharts click/hover event into a valid History date or `null` by looking the label up in the chart's own `data` array, rather than trusting Recharts' `activePayload`/`activeDataKey` (documented as unreliable on combo charts). Root-caused and fixed across #41/#45/#49 — tapping now only navigates via an explicit in-tooltip link (`wrapperStyle={{pointerEvents:'auto'}}`), not any tap on the chart area, so the chart stays usable for just viewing. **#33**: that same `pointerEvents:'auto'` tooltip is rendered *inside* Recharts' own move-tracking wrapper, so a finger drifting from the point that opened the tooltip toward the in-tooltip link could still bubble a mousemove/touchmove up and silently retarget the "active" point to a neighboring date the tooltip box visually overlapped — the link would then navigate to a different day than the one last shown in the tooltip text. Fixed with `onMouseMove`/`onTouchMove` + `stopPropagation()` on the tooltip's own container div, in all three charts below that have a navigation link (`WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `MacroTrendChart.tsx`); `CorrelationView.tsx` had no link at the time, unaffected — **#489** later gave every correlation scatter view the same in-tooltip link via `CorrelationChartTooltip.tsx`, including both of these guards. |
| `index.ts` | `DashboardScreen` only. |

#### `history/` — Epic 7, issue #8 (done)

Also fully built.

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `HistoryScreen.tsx` | Owns filter state (From/To date range, #40; `?date=` deep-link prefill from Dashboard, #41) and a List/Calendar `ToggleGroup` view switcher (#48). Renders `MetTargetList`, then either `CalendarView` or the sortable, filterable table of `EntryRow`s. **#172**: List view (only — same scope as the existing date filter, not extended to Calendar) also gained a note-text search (`searchText`, case-insensitive substring match against `entry.note`) and a mood filter (`moodFilter: Emotion \| undefined`, reusing `daily-log`'s `EmotionPicker` — now exported from that feature's barrel — for its existing click-again-to-clear toggle semantics rather than building new filter-chip UI). Both combine with the existing date-range filter and are cleared together by the existing "Clear filter" button. **#162**: List view's table also paginates now (`PAGE_SIZE = 20`) — `useHistoryData` still returns every entry (`getAll()`, unchanged), but only the current page's slice of `sorted` renders into the `<table>`. `currentPage` is computed as `Math.min(page, pageCount - 1)` at render time rather than reset via a `useEffect` keyed to the filter fields — self-corrects into range whether the result set shrank because a filter narrowed it or because an entry was deleted, with no dependency list to keep in sync. Previous/Next controls + a "Page X of Y" indicator render only when `pageCount > 1`. **#196**: the From/To date filters and the note-search input all gained `h-12` — `shared/ui/input.tsx`'s base `Input` is `h-8`, and these three never overrode it, unlike every standalone field on Today (`h-12` since #139). **#479**: toggleable `ReachedGoalLegend` sits next to the List table / Calendar grid (not under the view toggle). Strong tint = met day; light tint = `isHeadingTowardGoalOnDate` (day-over-day drop before met). **#490**: chips filter List to those dates (not hide tints); `rounded-full` chip look; tints always paint. |
| `useHistoryData.ts` | Same direct-repository pattern as `useDashboardData`; exposes `saveEntry`/`deleteEntry` that call the repository then reload. **#155**: also loads *every* `Goal` (own `IndexedDbGoalRepository` instance, not just the active goal `useGoalStore` already exposes) and returns `reachedWindows` (`domain/goal/reachedGoalWindows.ts`'s result over all goals + entries) alongside `entries`/`goal` — recomputed on every `reload()` too, so an edit that pushes a window's average past its target updates the highlighting without a page refresh. |
| `CalendarView.tsx` | Month-grid calendar (#48, Monday-start), a marker on days with an entry, tap-a-day opens a `DayDetail` panel; an "Edit this day" action hands off to `HistoryScreen`'s List view pre-filtered/expanded to that date. Takes an `onSaved` prop (#71, threaded from `HistoryScreen`'s `saveEntry`) passed straight through to `DayDetail` for its cycle-tracking toggle. Each day cell can show a **second** marker dot (#72) for `onPeriod` days, styled with `bg-destructive` (the mood-constant "danger" token, reused purely for its color) — that second `<span>` is only rendered at all when cycle tracking is on, so the grid doesn't reserve extra vertical space for a marker most users never see. A **third** dot, same gate/transparent-placeholder pattern, shows `bg-amber-500` for `hadConstipation` days when digestion tracking is on — a distinct color from the period dot so both opt-in trackers stay visually distinguishable when both are enabled for the same day. All dots render inside a `flex flex-row` wrapper `<span>` that's itself a sibling of the day number (#104) — they used to be direct `flex-col` children of the day button and stacked into rows; now they sit side by side under the number instead. **#155** / **#479**: strong `bg-primary/20` for `isGoalMetOnDate()`; light `bg-primary/10` only for `isHeadingTowardGoalOnDate()` (pre-met day-over-day weight drop — not whole-window membership). `showMetDayTint` / `showHeadingTowardTint` props honor the legend toggles. Aria suffixes use the updated `reachedGoalDayLabel` / `reachedGoalWindowDayLabel` copy. **#482**: legend under the grid for currently painted marker types; Markers popover checklist near the month pager toggles which dots paint via new persisted `calendarMarkerVisibilityStore` (independent of Settings track toggles). Visibility off omits the slot entirely (no transparent gap), including night eating. **#485**: legend items are themselves `aria-pressed` toggles (all trackable types stay listed, dimmed/struck when off); horizontal `flex-row flex-wrap` at every breakpoint; Markers popover removed. **#492**: same chip chrome as #490's goal legend (`rounded-full` + border/bg). |
| `DayDetail.tsx` | Shared day-detail renderer — meals, notes, and the day's overall mood (#39, extended for #44, reused by both `EntryRow`'s expand panel and `CalendarView`'s day panel). A `standalone` prop adds a date/weight/calories header for contexts (calendar) that don't already show that summary elsewhere; that header also gets the day's macro total (#52). **#145**: meals are no longer purely read-only here — whenever `onSaved` is provided, the meals section mounts `daily-log/MealList.tsx` directly (full add/edit/delete/reorder, identical to Today's), wired to call `onSaved({ ...entry, calorieEntries: next, updatedAt: ... })` on any change. Both real callers (`EntryRow.tsx`, `CalendarView.tsx`) always pass `onSaved`, so meals are editable from both History's List expand-row and the calendar day panel — reaching a focused meal editor no longer requires `EntryRow`'s full-form "Edit" mode. Without `onSaved`, the original plain-text rendering (group heading via `calorieEntryKcal`/macro summary via `calorieEntryProtein/Fat/Carbs`, an indented item sub-list with each item's own reaction emoji, #129) is kept as a fallback branch for any future purely-read-only caller. Separately, an optional `onSaved` also drives the cycle/digestion toggles (#71): when present alongside `useCycleTrackingStore`'s Settings toggle, an "On period" button calls `onSaved({ ...entry, onPeriod: !entry.onPeriod, ... })`, the same immediate-save-on-click shape #61 originally used directly on Today — its new home after #61's original placement (a button on Today, shown every day) turned out to be unwanted daily noise for something relevant only a few days a month. A second, independent toggle follows the identical pattern for digestion tracking (`useDigestionTrackingStore`, a "Constipation" button); both can show together in a shared `flex flex-wrap` row. Digestion tracking's toggle *also* now renders directly on `DailyEntryForm.tsx`/Today — unlike `onPeriod`, which stays History-only. |
| `EntryRow.tsx` | One table row with `view / edit / confirmDelete` modes. Edit mode swaps in `DailyEntryForm` (`alwaysEditable`) for Weight/Sleep/Steps/Note/meals all at once; expand/collapse toggles `DayDetail`, which since #145 offers its own focused meal editing without needing this full-form mode at all — "Edit" here is now really for the day-level fields, meals can be reached either way. Supports `defaultExpanded` for the Dashboard deep-link case. The Calories cell also shows the day's macro total as a muted line beneath the kcal number (#52), omitted when nothing was logged — uses the **compact** single-initial form (`macrosSummaryTextCompact`, #67) rather than the full-word one, since the full-word line was wide enough on mobile to push the Actions column (expand/edit/delete) off screen. The Date cell uses a fixed **`dd.MM.yy`** numeric format (#73), not the localized `'PP'` format used elsewhere (`DayDetail`'s standalone header, Dashboard chart tooltips) — `'PP'` ("15 июл. 2026 г.") was still wide enough on its own to push the Actions column's 3rd icon off screen even after #67's fix, and being locale-agnostic this format needs no `dateFnsLocale`. **#136**: edit mode's `<td colSpan={5}>` (wrapping the full-width `DailyEntryForm`) gained `block` — a `table-layout: auto` `<td>` sizes to its content's intrinsic width regardless of `max-w-full`/`min-w-0`, so `DailyEntryForm` (built for a normal page section, not a table cell) was forcing the whole table — already `overflow-x-auto` (`HistoryScreen.tsx`) — wider than the viewport on mobile, cutting content off until scrolled. `display: block` escapes the table layout algorithm for just that cell, so it behaves like an ordinary block box instead. **#155** / **#479**: Date cell strong tint for met day (`isGoalReachedDay`); light tint for `isHeadingTowardGoal` (day-over-day drop before met), not whole-window membership. |
| `MetTargetList.tsx` | A plain list of weeks where the target was met — explicitly not gamified, no badges. **#392**: reported live — week ranges showed no year (`'MMM d'`), ambiguous across the multi-year imported dataset. #373's own reasoning for excluding this file ("only ever shows one recent week") didn't actually hold — this renders a full historical list, same shape as `WeeklySummaryCards`. Changed to `'MMM d, yyyy'`, same format #373/#378 chose. **#408**: reported live — the user wanted the resulting weight itself, not just the delta, matching what `PastTargetsList.tsx` (Goal page's own past-targets table) already shows via `previousToCurrentWeightLabel`. Reused that same `t.goal.previousToCurrentWeightLabel(previous, current, unit)` Dictionary entry here (generic "X → Y unit" copy, not goal-specific wording, so cross-feature reuse is fine) rather than duplicating it under `t.history`. `WeeklySummary` already carries everything needed with no schema change: `week.averageWeightKg` is this week's average (the "current" value), and the "previous" value is derived as `week.averageWeightKg - week.deltaVsPriorWeekKg` — both are guaranteed non-null wherever `deltaVsPriorWeekKg` itself is set, since `weeklySummaries()` only ever computes the delta when both weeks' averages exist. Second line added below the existing delta, right-aligned, same stacked-value layout `PastTargetsList.tsx`'s own status column already uses. **#425**: reported live — this list used to render at the very top of `HistoryScreen.tsx`, before the List/Calendar view toggle, pushing the actual History content down the page once enough weeks qualified, with no scroll bound of its own. Moved to render *after* the Calendar/List view section instead (`HistoryScreen.tsx`); this component's own `<ul>` gained `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto`, the same bounded-scroll treatment `WeeklySummaryCards`/`MonthlySummaryCards`/the outlier chip lists already use, chosen over pagination as the smaller change (no new interactive state). |
| `index.ts` | `HistoryScreen` only. |

#### `settings/SettingsScreen.tsx` — issues #37/#43 (done)

Fully built, redesigned in #43 around a consistent `Card`-per-section layout with the shared `ToggleGroup` primitive replacing raw radios everywhere. **#283**: `PageHeader`'s existing `action` slot now carries a compact clickable version badge (`releaseNotes[0].version`, same derivation `AboutScreen.tsx` already uses) linking to `/about`. **#498**: About and Features ("Capabilities") discovery cards sit at the **top** of the page (right after the header), so trust/capabilities aren't buried under recipes/metrics. **#504**: Export stays in its prior lower placement — after preference/list-management cards and above the end-of-page destructive clear/delete group (#164) — not promoted with About/Features (that was a #498 side effect). The About card itself still uses the same "description + link button" shape #234 introduced when About left the bottom nav — just relocated. New `settings.featuresLabel`/`featuresDescription`/`viewFeaturesButton` keys (en/ru) for the Features card → `/features`. **#194**: every `ToggleGroupItem` on this page (all 8 groups below — units, week start, language, theme, light/dark, cycle/digestion/reminder toggles, 20 items total) gained `h-12` — the shared `toggle-group.tsx` primitive has no explicit height on its own (sizes purely from `py-1.5` + text, ~32-36px), well under the `h-12` standard #126/#133/#139 established for Today's controls, reported as "action items on this page don't match Today." Scoped to just this page's own instances via `className="h-12"` per item, not the shared component globally — bumping `ToggleGroupItem` itself would also affect deliberately-compact controls elsewhere (e.g. the per-100g/portion toggle inside meal editing, out of scope per #126's own density precedent).

**#599**: a dismissible backup-reminder banner (plain `role="status"` div, not a `Card` — sits above every numbered card below) renders only once `backupReminderStatus()` (`shared/lib/lastBackupReminder.ts`) says so: `BACKUP_REMINDER_THRESHOLD_DAYS` (14) days since the last successful JSON backup, or since `useLastBackupStore.firstSeenAt` if one has never happened. Its "Go to Export" link is a plain same-page `<a href="#export-section">` — the Export `Card` (10b, below) gained that `id` — rather than a scroll ref, and Dismiss snoozes via `dismissedUntil` (`BACKUP_REMINDER_SNOOZE_DAYS`, 7 days) instead of disabling the reminder permanently.

0. **About** (#234, promoted #498) — description + "View About" → `/about`.
0b. **Features** (#498) — description + "View Features" → `/features`.
1. **Units** — `ToggleGroup` bound to `useUnitStore` (kg/lb) — landed here from the Goal page in #37.
2. **Week start** (#85) — `ToggleGroup` bound to `useWeekStartStore.weekStart` ("Monday" / "Day of my first entry"), default Monday.
2b. **Start of day** (#298) — `type="time"` bound to `useDayStartStore.dayStartTime` (Today-screen effective date only).
2c. **Default meal times** (#588, apply-to-existing #595) — stacked `h-12 w-32` clocks for Breakfast/Lunch/Snack/Dinner (`useMealSlotDefaultTimesStore`). On blur after a real change, if any untimed slot-labeled meals exist, an alertdialog offers Yes (stamp `timeEaten` via `stampSlotDefaultsOnUntimedMeals` + upsert) or No (prefs only).
3. **Language** — `ToggleGroup` bound to `useLocaleStore` (en/ru).
4. **Appearance** — 5 mood swatches bound to `useThemeStore.mood`, plus a light/dark `ToggleGroup` bound to `colorScheme` (#17). **#193**: the mood-picker's section label reads "Theme"/"Тема" now, not "Mood"/"Настроение" — confused users with the unrelated day/meal emotion pickers. Copy-only (`settings.moodLabel`'s display string); the `Mood` type, `useThemeStore.mood`, and the 5 palette names are unchanged.
4b. **Layout preset** (#604) — new card, right above "What to track": Simple/Full buttons call `stores/trackingPreset.ts`'s `applyTrackingPreset(preset)`. Simple sets `trackedFieldsStore.tracked` to `{ sleep, steps, bodyMeasurements, note, mood, bodyComposition, nightEating, dayTotals, fiber }` all `false` (weight/meals/calories/weekly-target aren't gated by that store, so they're unaffected either way), turns off water tracking and all three micronutrients, and hides the correspondingly Day-scoped `sectionVisibilityStore` keys (`todayVsYesterday`/`todayVsMaxWeight`/`todayRemaining*`/`todaySteps`/`todaySleep`/`todayBmi`) so Simple doesn't leave an empty card where a hidden field used to render — `goal*` keys are untouched, scoped to Day per the issue's own title. Full restores every one of those to this app's own shipped defaults (a literal copy of `trackedFieldsStore.ts`'s `DEFAULT_TRACKED`, not a re-import — "Full" means the documented layout, not whatever the store's live default happens to be). Neither preset touches cycle/digestion tracking — a real personal-data opt-in, not a UI-density preference, so a preset click silently disabling one would be a surprising way to lose a deliberate choice. `useTodayCardOrderStore.resetOrder()` runs either way, satisfying the issue's "preset sets initial visibility/order" — every field/card stays individually editable in the cards below afterward, same "starting point, not a lock-in" framing the issue asked for. Brief "Applied" confirmation, same `justSaved`-style 2s auto-clear `GoalForm.tsx` established.
5. **Cycle tracking** (#61) — on/off `ToggleGroup` bound to `useCycleTrackingStore.enabled`, off by default. The only effect of turning it on is that `DayDetail` starts rendering the `onPeriod` toggle (moved off Today by #71); no gender field, no phase/prediction logic.
6. **Digestion tracking** — on/off `ToggleGroup` bound to `useDigestionTrackingStore.enabled`, off by default, deliberately identical shape to Cycle tracking above: turning it on makes both `DayDetail` and `DailyEntryForm.tsx`/Today render a "Constipation" toggle (`DailyEntry.hadConstipation`). Filed and built without a public GitHub issue at the user's explicit request (privacy — this repo is public); still documented normally here per the project's usual close-out process. Reframed from an earlier "Bowel movement" field (also built without a public issue, same reason) around the problem instead of the normal day, and surfaced on Today directly — users couldn't find the History-only toggle; see `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s own row above.
