7. **Daily reminder** (#171) — on/off `ToggleGroup` bound to `useDailyReminderStore.enabled`, off by default, same shape as Cycle/Digestion tracking above. Gates `TodayScreen.tsx`'s quiet "No entry yet today" banner — see that file's own entry above for why it's an in-app note rather than a real push notification.
8. **Meal items** (#50) — `MealItemsSection.tsx`: lists every `MealItem` from `useMealItemStore`, each row an inline-editable name (commits on blur/Enter via `rename()`) + a Pencil + a delete button. Empty state when nothing's been logged yet. **#570**: shows `mealItemsCount` (total) above the search field, or `mealItemsFilteredCount` while a search query is active. **#99**: the Pencil toggles a nutrition editor for that item — kcal/protein/fat/carbs/grams inputs using the same per-100g + quantity model as everywhere else (#96), pre-filled by back-calculating a rate + quantity from the item's stored `lastAmountKcal`/macros/`lastAmountG` via `ratesFromAbsolute` (blank if nothing recorded yet), with a live computed-total preview (#98's `formatComputedTotal`) and Save calling `touch(item.name, scaleFromPer100g(...))` directly — no new store action needed, `touch()` already upserts exactly these fields. A non-editing row also shows a "last logged" subtitle (same wording `FoodPickerDialog` uses for these items) when nutrition exists. `scaleFromPer100g`/`ratesFromAbsolute`/`formatComputedTotal`/`parseOptionalMacro` moved from `DailyEntryForm.tsx` to `shared/lib/macroScaling.ts` so both screens can use them. **#149**: a new "Add custom food" button + `AddMealItemForm` (same name + per-100g nutrition fields as `MealItemRow`'s own editor, blank instead of pre-filled) lets a dictionary entry be created directly here — before this, the only way a `MealItem` came into existence was as a side effect of logging a real meal somewhere else. Save calls the same `touch(name, nutrition)` upsert every other write path already uses; no schema or repository change needed since `MealItem` already has no foreign key to any day's log. **#170**: both `MealItemRow`'s editor and `AddMealItemForm` gained the same Per-100g/Per-portion `ToggleGroup` #111 added to manual meal entry — previously this screen was per-100g-only even after #149 extended the same limitation to the new create form. Mode-switch conversion (`handleMacroModeChange` in each component, not shared — mirrors `MealList.tsx`'s own `handleAddMacroModeChange`, same "convert the typed numbers, don't reinterpret them" behavior) and the Portion-mode static "Portion" badge replacing the editable grams field (#121) are duplicated per-component rather than extracted, consistent with this file's existing per-screen duplication of this exact form shape. **#179**: a filter-as-you-type `Input` above the list (same case-insensitive `.filter(name.includes(query))` shape as `FoodListSettingsScreen`'s search, no shared helper — small enough not to be worth extracting) narrows `visibleItems`; a "no matches" message shows when the query yields nothing. The `<ul>` itself gained `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto` — unlike `FoodListSettingsScreen` (its own full page, free to grow), this list lives inside a Settings `Card`, so an uncapped list would keep growing the whole Settings screen instead of scrolling independently. **#192**: also gained `overscroll-y-contain` — a touch-scroll gesture reaching the list's top/bottom edge was inconsistently "chaining" up to scroll the whole Settings page instead of stopping there. Different root cause from #74's nested-scroll fix elsewhere in the app (that one was a `vh`-vs-`dvh` viewport-height issue specific to a fixed-position dialog with the on-screen keyboard open) — this is a plain page section, not a dialog, so the standard `overscroll-behavior: contain` fix applied directly instead. **#583**: protein/fat/carbs labels now mirror kcal's mode-aware `/100g` cue (`proteinPer100gLabel` etc.; portion mode keeps plain names), plus a reused `itemNutritionSectionLabel` heading; nutrition editor panel and add-food dialog fields use a visible `border-border` frame and `bg-background` inputs so borders aren't lost on the muted panel. **#584**: dish titles are plain text by default; the same pencil that opens nutrition edit also reveals the name `Input` (closing via pencil or Save collapses back to plain text). **#589**: Cancel beside Save discards draft name/nutrition; rename commits on Save or pencil-close, not mid-edit blur. **#583 rework**: while editing, one `border border-border bg-muted/40` panel wraps the name `Input` (plus favorite/pencil/delete) together with the nutrition fields — the name no longer sits outside that border.
9. **Meal name presets** (#110, **#594**) — `MealLabelPresetsSection.tsx`: manages `useMealLabelPresetStore.presets` (a plain `string[]`, persisted like `weekStartStore` — local preference, not IndexedDB; **#594** includes them in the JSON backup `settings` blob). Lists current presets each with a delete button, a free-text add row, and one-click "+ Breakfast" etc. buttons for any of `dailyEntry.defaultMealNamePresets` (translated) not yet added — defaults are offered, not auto-seeded into the store, so a later language switch doesn't strand stale-language presets for someone who never touched the list. **#563**: the add/edit meal flyout (`AddMealDialog`) again exposes rename in-flow — free-text Meal name plus chips for the four positional defaults always, then any extra Settings presets — so a skipped-breakfast day can be labeled Lunch without opening Settings. Empty or matching the positional default clears `CalorieEntry.label` so #141 still re-translates unlabeled meals. **#567**: chips use `mealLabelSuggestionsForLocale` so Settings presets that are built-in defaults in *another* locale (e.g. leftover `Breakfast` after switching to RU) are hidden — only active-locale defaults + truly custom presets. **#568**: dialog uses `editableMealLabel` (empty string stays empty while typing); Done still stores blank as unset for #141. **#142**: "not yet added" is checked against *every* locale's translation of each default, not just the active one — `getDictionary(locale).dailyEntry.defaultMealNamePresets[index]` for each `Locale`, since the four defaults are positionally aligned across `en.ts`/`ru.ts` (same index = same concept). Previously only checked the active locale's array, so someone who'd already added the English defaults still saw all four Russian translations offered as if missing.
10. **Food list** (#90) — description + a "Manage food list" button linking to the new `/settings/foods` route (`FoodListSettingsScreen.tsx`, its own screen rather than an inline card — the curated list has 300+ items, too many to always render inline).
10a. **Using two devices** (#612) — copy-only help card directly above Export: intro line + a 3-step numbered list (`settings.twoDevicesHelpSteps`, `string[]`) explaining that this device holds the live data, Export makes a portable backup, and Import merges a backup into whatever's already on the target device. No sync backend exists or is implied — filed from the 2026-08-04 product-owner audit specifically to give phone/laptop users a correct mental model instead of them guessing at one from the Export/Import button labels alone.
10b. **Export** (#24; placement restored #504) — embeds `<ExportSection />` inside a `Card`, after preference/list-management cards and above the destructive clear/delete group (#164). Briefly sat under About/Features at the top via #498; #504 moved it back down.
11. **Delete a date range** (#377) — new `DeleteRangeSection.tsx`, sits directly before **Clear all data** below in the same end-of-page destructive-actions group. Scope resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: "daily entries + everything tied to those dates" over "just daily entries" — `CustomMetricEntry` is the one other collection with its own `date` field, so it's the only other one in scope; every definition/reference collection (goals, `mealItems`, `foodOverrides`, recipes, `customMetrics`, `customCorrelations`) stays untouched, same reasoning #370/#240 already established for what a date range does and doesn't touch. A from/to date-range picker (both dates required, unlike the export period's "blank means everything") plus a two-step confirm showing the *actual row count* about to be deleted (computed before confirming, not guessed), and a distinct "nothing to delete" message instead of a pointless zero-count confirm prompt. Reuses `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getRange()` (already existed) and a plain JS filter for `CustomMetricEntry` (no range index there, same approach #370's ranged export already uses). Confirmed on-device.
12. **Clear all data** (#164) — `ClearAllDataSection.tsx`, deliberately the last card on the page (a destructive, irreversible action shouldn't sit among routine preference toggles). Two-step confirm, same pattern `EntryRow.tsx`/`MealList.tsx` already use for deleting a single entry/meal, wording scaled up since this wipes everything rather than one row. Clears all four IndexedDB tables directly (`db.goals`/`db.dailyEntries`/`db.mealItems`/`db.foodOverrides.clear()`) — the same four `exportActions.ts` reads for a backup — then `window.location.reload()`s rather than resetting every Zustand store individually, since several screens hold their own already-loaded copies of now-wiped data in memory. Deliberately distinct from just uninstalling the app or clearing site data via the browser, which most users don't know how to find and which also wipes localStorage preferences (theme, locale, tracking-toggle state) this leaves untouched.
13. **Profile** (#233, #550, **#594**) — new `ProfileSection.tsx`, sits right after the "What to track" card (which itself gained a `bodyComposition` toggle key, see `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s own row below). Height (cm) / Age / Sex, plain local component state (not React Hook Form — a single always-editable card, not a display/edit toggle over saved history) validated on Save via `profileFormSchema.ts`'s `heightCmSchema`/`ageSchema` (generous human ranges, same style as the daily-entry body schemas) and a new persisted `useProfileStore` (`stores/profileStore.ts` — `heightCm`/`age`/`sex`, `Sex` type defined in `domain/stats/bodyComposition.ts` and re-exported from both, keeping the domain→store import direction correct). Same category as unit/theme/week-start — **#594** includes it in the JSON backup `settings` blob (copy updated; previously said device-only). Powers the two new StatCards on `TodayScreen.tsx` — see that file's own row below. **#550**: read-only view is a 2×2 labeled grid (body-composition #515 pattern) instead of one mid-dot summary line; optional BMI/BMR line when latest weight exists.

Release notes (#63) used to be a card here but moved to the About page in #66 — see `about/AboutScreen.tsx` below.

#### `settings/FoodListSettingsScreen.tsx` — new in #90

A dedicated management screen for the curated `data/foods.ts` list (reached from Settings, not a tab-bar item) — search + one row per food, each showing its *effective* values (`effectiveFoodItem`, shipped numbers or the override if one exists). Per row: a pencil opens inline kcal/protein/fat/carbs inputs (same input-row shape as `DailyEntryForm`'s meal editing) with a Save button calling `setNutrition`; a Hide/Show toggle button calls `setHidden`; a "Restore default" button (shown only when an override exists) calls `restoreDefault`, clearing the override entirely. A hidden food still renders here (with a "(Hidden)" label) rather than disappearing — this screen is where you'd go to un-hide it, so it can't itself apply the hiding filter the way `FoodPickerDialog` does.

#### `export/` — Epic 8, issue #9; folded into Settings in #24, schema bumped in #21; external-source imports (#365 Zepp Life, #366 Apple Health)

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `exportBundleSchema.ts` | Current schema is **`version: 10`** (bumped from 9 for #594's Settings prefs blob). Legacy schemas kept so old backup files stay importable: `exportBundleSchemaV9` (#578 appearance/locale only), `exportBundleSchemaV8` (weekly notes, no UI prefs), `exportBundleSchemaV7`…`V2` as before. **#113**: `mealItems`/`foodOverrides` (previously local-only, not exported — see `mealItem`/`foodOverride` domain files) added as purely optional top-level fields, no version bump — an older bundle without them still parses fine, `importAllData` just has nothing to import for those. **#135**: `goalSchema.weekStart` added the same way — purely optional, no version bump, older bundle's goal just ends up with `weekStart: undefined` on import (same as a goal never re-saved since #135). **#208**: `goalSchema.dailyCalorieTargetKcal` added the same way again — purely optional, no version bump. **#220**: `goalSchema.dailyProteinTargetG` added the same way once more. **#225**: `dailyEntrySchema.waistCm`/`hipCm`/`bodyFatPercent` added the same purely-additive/optional way — no version bump, only on the current `dailyEntrySchema`, not backported to the frozen V2-V6 legacy schemas. **#251**: `recipes` (a whole new top-level array, `recipeSchema`/`recipeIngredientSchema`) added the same purely-additive/optional way — proof that even a brand-new *collection*, not just a new field on an existing one, doesn't need a version bump as long as it's `.optional()`. **#233/#341/#252/#258/etc.**: `dailyEntrySchema`/`goalSchema`/`calorieItemSchema` each picked up further optional fields over time (body composition, fiber, water/fat/carb targets) the identical way — none are individually called out here, same reasoning. **#336**: `customMetrics`/`customMetricEntries`/`customCorrelations` (`customMetricSchema`/`customMetricEntrySchema`/`metricRefSchema`/`customCorrelationSchema`) added the same way as recipes — three new optional top-level arrays, no version bump. **#557**: live schema bumped to **v8** for top-level `weeklyNotes`. **#578**: bumped to **v9** for optional `appearance` (`mood` + `colorScheme`) and `locale` (`en`/`ru`) — UI prefs that lived only in localStorage (`themeStore`/`localeStore`); `exportBundleSchemaV8` kept so v8 files upgrade cleanly with those fields unset. **#579**: `mealLabelSchema` coerces numeric `CalorieEntry.label` values to strings on parse — some historical rows (and thus re-exported backups) stored meal-slot ids as numbers; Zod's strict `z.string()` rejected the whole ~MB backup as invalid after a few seconds of validation, which looked like a silent stop if the muted error line was easy to miss. ExportSection import errors now use a bordered `role="alert"` card. **#594**: bumped to **v10** for optional `settings` (unit, weekStart, dayStartTime, mealSlotDefaultTimes, cycle/digestion/water tracking, micronutrients, trackedFields, dailyReminder, trendChartVisible, profile, mealLabelPresets) — `settingsPreferences.ts` collects/applies; older backups omit `settings` and leave device prefs alone. Dashboard-only prefs (chart order, outliers, etc.) stay out of scope. |
| `exportBundle.ts` | `buildExportBundle(goals, dailyEntries, mealItems, foodOverrides, recipes, customMetrics, customMetricEntries, customCorrelations, weeklyNotes, appearance?, locale?, settings?)` — stamps `version: 10` + `exportedAt`. Plain positional params; **#578** added optional trailing `appearance`/`locale`; **#594** added optional `settings`. |
| `exportActions.ts` | `exportAllData()` (reads all eight repositories' `getAll()`, plus current `themeStore`/`localeStore` for #578 and `collectSettingsPreferences()` for #594), `importAllData(bundle)` (upserts every goal/entry/meal-item/food-override/recipe/custom-metric/custom-metric-entry/custom-correlation/weekly-note — merge, not destructive replace; every collection newer than the base bundle defaults to `[]` via `?? []` for older backups that predate it; **#578** also applies `appearance`/`locale` when present; **#594** applies `settings` via `applySettingsPreferences`), `parseExportBundle(raw)` — tries the current v10 schema first, then **v9** (#594: settings unset), then **v8** (#578: appearance/locale unset), then **v7** (#557: empty `weeklyNotes`), then **v6** (#271: `upgradeV6ToV7` buckets a single `waterMl` total into one legacy `WaterEntry`), then v5 (#129: `upgradeV5ToV6` folds a single-item meal's group reaction onto its item, drops it for a multi-item meal — same "unambiguous only when there's exactly one dish" reasoning as the IndexedDB v6→v7 migration), then v4 (folds flat meals into single-item groups via the shared `foldFlatMealsIntoGroups` helper — same transform as the IndexedDB v4→v5 migration, then also passed through the v5/v6/v7 upgrades), then v3 (clears old-format meal emotions, then folds into groups, then upgrades onward), then v2 (wraps `caloriesConsumed`, then folds, then upgrades onward), otherwise throws `InvalidBackupFileError` with a user-facing message. **#207**: `importAllData` looks up existing entries/meal-items by `date`/`name` first (`dailyEntryRepository.getAll()`/`mealItemRepository.getAll()`, once, before the upsert loop) and carries the existing id over onto the imported record before upserting — `dailyEntries`/`mealItems` each have a *unique* secondary index on `date`/`name` (`db.ts`) that their own `upsert()` deliberately enforces as a hard guarantee (see `index.test.ts`'s "enforces one entry per date"/"enforces unique names"), and a re-imported backup's own ids essentially never match what the importing device generated for the same date/name since the backup was taken — so almost any real re-import onto a device that's kept being used since hit a Dexie `ConstraintError`, surfacing as a generic "Import failed" with nothing logged (the error was genuinely caught, not silently swallowed). Fixed at the import layer, not the repository — every other caller (`mealItemStore.touch()`/`rename()`, Today/History's own save flows) already resolves to the existing id first, so the repository's own enforcement and its tests stay untouched. **#336**: `customMetricEntries` has the same unique-index situation (`&[metricId+date]`, `db.ts` v11) — import resolves the existing `(metricId, date)` row's id first the identical way, since a re-imported entry's own id essentially never matches. Recipes/custom metrics/custom correlations themselves have no unique index beyond `id` (which the export already carries stably from creation), so they're plain unconditional upserts with no lookup needed. |
| `ExportSection.tsx` | The export/import UI as a `Card`-content fragment (no `PageHeader` — it's meant to be dropped inside Settings' own `Card`, not rendered as a standalone screen anymore). Export downloads a JSON file via `Blob` + a synthetic anchor click; import is a hidden file input. Reports counts via a hand-written pluralizer, not a naive `+'s'`. **#123**: a second "Export as Excel" button calls `buildExportWorkbook()` (below) and downloads the result the same `Blob` + synthetic-anchor way, distinct filename (`turtle-steps-export-<date>.xlsx` vs. the JSON backup's `turtle-steps-backup-<date>.json`) so it doesn't read as re-importable — the file input's `accept="application/json"` and `parseExportBundle` both still only understand JSON. **#125**: a third "Export as CSV" button calls `buildDailyLogCsv()` (below), with an `InfoTooltip` (same primitive as the Calories field's, #16) noting CSV is the best format to paste into an LLM for analysis. Its own filename (`turtle-steps-daily-log-<date>.csv`) and its own entries-only summary (`exportedCsvSummary`, no goals count — CSV doesn't cover goals) rather than reusing the JSON/Excel exports' `summary()`. **#219**: a fourth "Export as Markdown" button, same `Blob` + synthetic-anchor pattern, calling `buildDailyLogMarkdown()` (below) — `turtle-steps-daily-log-<date>.md`, its own `exportedMarkdownSummary` (entries-only, same reasoning as CSV's). **#176**: below the card description, a small muted line shows `navigator.storage.estimate()`'s `usage`, formatted by a local `formatBytes()` helper (B/KB/MB/GB). Fetched in a `useEffect` on mount; best-effort — `navigator.storage`/`estimate()` can be absent or reject on some browsers, in which case the line simply doesn't render rather than showing an error for a purely informational number. **#191**: also reads `quota` now — originally omitted (reasoning: it's usually a large, semi-arbitrary browser-computed ceiling, not a small meaningful number, so "X of Y" would read as noise) — but real usage showed people specifically wanted to know how much room is left and whether iOS even enforces a limit, better answered by showing the real number than by the app deciding on their behalf it's not worth surfacing. `storageUsedOfQuotaLabel(used, quota)` renders whenever both are known; the original usage-only `storageUsedLabel` stays as a fallback for the rare browser that returns `usage` without `quota`. **#240**: a new "Export period" block (two `type="date"` `Input`s, same pattern `CompareRangesView.tsx` uses) sits between the JSON backup button and the Excel/CSV/Markdown ones — deliberately scoped to *only* those three, not the JSON backup, since a backup should stay complete regardless of whatever range happens to be set. **#370** (reverses this decision): re-raised once ~6 years of data made the always-complete JSON backup unwieldy — new "Export ranged backup" button added below the period picker, existing "Export backup" button untouched (still the one guaranteed-complete restore source). Confirmed on-device. Local `filterByExportPeriod(entries, start, end)` — blank start/end means no bound on that side, blank-blank means unfiltered (matches pre-#240 behavior exactly) — is applied to `bundle.dailyEntries` right before each of those three builders; `bundle.goals` is never filtered (Excel's own goals sheet always stays complete, same "period is about which days of logged data" reasoning). **#285**: `handleImportFile` now also calls `useMealItemStore.getState().loadItems()`/`useFoodOverrideStore.getState().loadOverrides()` right after a successful `importAllData` — that function writes straight to IndexedDB via its own module-scope repository instances (see `exportActions.ts` below), entirely bypassing both stores, so an already-mounted UI reading from either one (e.g. `MealItemsSection`'s Settings list, on the same page as this very Import button) kept showing pre-import data until something unrelated happened to remount and reload it. **#365/#366**: two further hidden-file-input buttons, "Import from Zepp Life" and "Import from Apple Health" — each delegates entirely to its own feature-folder module (`zeppLife/`, `appleHealth/`, below) rather than adding a third parsing path inline here; `ExportSection.tsx` itself only owns the file-picker plumbing, status/progress state, and rendering each importer's summary. Confirmed on-device. **#381**: filed asking for in-app instructions on how to actually obtain each source's export file — **correction to this issue's own filing**: the menu-path steps already existed, folded into each `importBlurb` as one dense sentence (verified against real menu paths via WebSearch — Apple's official support doc for Health, community/forum sources for Zepp Life's GDPR-style "Exercising user rights" export). The actual gap was discoverability, not missing content. Split each blurb into a short "what this does" sentence + a new collapsible `<details>`/`<summary>` "How do I get this file?" disclosure holding the existing steps verbatim — no new instructions invented. New Dictionary keys `howToExportLabel`/`howToExportSteps` on both `zeppLifeImport`/`appleHealthImport` (en/ru). Confirmed on-device. **#439**: reported live — MyFitnessPal's own `howToExportSteps` (added alongside #367's importer, same disclosure pattern as above) described the self-service Data Access Request path only, with no mention that it requires a MyFitnessPal Premium subscription; a free-tier user has to email MyFitnessPal support directly instead. Kept the self-service steps as the primary instruction, added a plain fallback sentence about emailing support directly if "Manage My Data" isn't available — deliberately not framed in formal/legal ("GDPR") language. **#526**: each import source block (JSON backup, Zepp Life, Apple Health, MyFitnessPal) gained `rounded-lg border border-border p-3` so identical chip/conflict labels no longer read as one continuous wall. **#617**: status rendering moved out of one shared card footer into the corresponding block itself — JSON backup, ranged backup, Excel, CSV, Markdown, JSON import, Zepp, Apple Health, and MyFitnessPal each render their own success/error line so feedback appears directly under the action that produced it. **#599**: `handleExport` (only — not the ranged/Excel/CSV/Markdown exports) now also calls `useLastBackupStore.recordExport()` on success; a new `role="status"` line right above the "Export backup" button always shows `t.export.lastBackupNeverLabel`/`lastBackupAgoLabel(days)`, reused by `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s dismissible reminder banner (below). **#608**: a third JSON export option, "Encrypted backup", sitting right after the ranged backup — new `encryptedBackup.ts` wraps Web Crypto (`PBKDF2`, 250k iterations, → `AES-GCM`) behind `encryptBackupJson(json, password)`/`decryptBackupJson(envelope, password)`, `EncryptedBackupEnvelope` (`{ encrypted: true, version: 1, salt, iv, ciphertext }`, all base64, `isEncryptedBackupEnvelope()` type guard) and a `WrongBackupPasswordError` thrown when `decrypt` fails. `handleExportEncrypted(password)` mirrors `handleExport` (same `exportAllData()` → download shape, same `recordBackupExport()` call — the plain and encrypted exports are two equally-valid "a backup exists" signals for #599's reminder) but writes the encrypted envelope instead of the plain bundle, filename suffixed `.encrypted.json`, via a new password+confirm `EncryptedBackupExportDialog.tsx` (mirrors `MyFitnessPalPasswordDialog.tsx`'s shape) that warns up front a forgotten password makes the file unrecoverable — there's no reset path by design. `handleImportFile` now `JSON.parse`s first and checks `isEncryptedBackupEnvelope()` before `parseExportBundle` — an encrypted file parses as JSON fine but isn't a bundle, so without this check it would've hit the generic "not a valid backup" error instead of the password prompt. A detected envelope opens `EncryptedBackupImportDialog.tsx` (single password field, near-identical to `MyFitnessPalPasswordDialog.tsx`); `handleEncryptedImportSubmit(password)` decrypts, `JSON.parse`s the plaintext, then reuses the exact same `parseExportBundle`/`importAllData`/store-reload sequence as the plain import (factored into a new shared `applyImportedBundle()` helper) — `WrongBackupPasswordError` shows inline in the still-open dialog rather than the shared status area, letting the user retry without re-picking the file. Both dialogs' passwords live only in their own local component state. **#609**: a fifth "Export PDF summary" option — a one-page, non-editable PDF for sharing outside the app (e.g. with a clinician), entirely client-side. New `exportPdf.ts`: `buildPdfSummaryData(allEntries, rangeDays, asOfDate, weekStartsOn)` is the pure, deterministically-testable data shaping (anchored to an explicit `asOfDate` string, not a live clock) — filters to the last 30/90 days (own fixed `ToggleGroup` picker, not the free-form period picker Excel/CSV/Markdown/ranged-backup share above, since the issue calls for exactly those two options), reuses `weeklySummaries()` (`domain/stats`, unmodified) for the weekly-averages table, and picks the most-recently-logged value for waist/hip/body fat % (`latestNumberField`, a small local helper — a "current snapshot," not an average, since that's what's clinically relevant). `buildSummaryPdf(data, t, locale, unit)` renders it: `jspdf`/`jspdf-autotable` (both new dependencies) are dynamically imported inside the function, same reasoning `exceljs` already documents in `exportXlsx.ts` — only pulled into a chunk when this actually runs. The weight trend is a hand-drawn line (plain jsPDF `line()`/`text()` primitives, scaled to the data's own min/max) rather than a rasterized Recharts SVG — the issue itself allows "image or simple chart," and avoids pulling in a canvas-conversion dependency for a single chart. The weekly table uses `jspdf-autotable`'s functional `autoTable(doc, options)` API (not the `doc.autoTable()` plugin-attached form). A "not medical advice" disclaimer (`t.pdfSummary.disclaimer`) is drawn on every page's footer, satisfying the issue's acceptance criterion that it be visible on the document regardless of how many pages the content spans. New Dictionary section `pdfSummary` holds the document's own baked-in content strings (title, section headers, disclaimer), kept separate from `export.exportPdf*` (the Settings-page trigger button/blurb/status strings) — same "mechanism vs. content" split as `zeppLifeImport`/`appleHealthImport`'s own `howToExportSteps` vs. their trigger copy. `weekStartsOn` is resolved via `resolveWeekStartsOn(weekStart, earliestEntryDate)` (the same pure function `useWeekStartsOn` calls internally) rather than that hook itself, since `ExportSection.tsx` only has `dailyEntries` inside the async handler, not as component-level state the hook could read. **#623**: reported live right after confirming #609 — a Russian-locale PDF rendered every string as mojibake. Root cause: jsPDF's default standard-14 fonts (Helvetica etc.) only cover WinAnsi/Latin-1-style glyphs, no Cyrillic at all — not an error, just silent garbage output, so nothing in the export flow caught it. Fixed by embedding a real Cyrillic-capable font: new `ptSansRegularFont.ts` exports a base64 TTF (PT Sans Regular, designed for Latin+Cyrillic, sourced one-time from `@expo-google-fonts/pt-sans`'s `PTSans_400Regular.ttf` — not a runtime dependency, only a font-asset source), dynamically imported inside `buildSummaryPdf` (same "only pulled into a chunk when this function actually runs" reasoning as `jspdf`/`jspdf-autotable` themselves) and registered via `doc.addFileToVFS`/`doc.addFont`/`doc.setFont('PTSans')` right after the document is created, plus `font: 'PTSans'` in the `jspdf-autotable` `styles`. Regular weight only — the disclaimer's italic styling was dropped (would have needed a second embedded weight) rather than falling back to `helvetica` mid-document, which would have silently reintroduced the same mojibake for that one line. **#624**: reported live right alongside #623 — the fixed "Last 30 days"/"Last 90 days" toggle from #609 couldn't cover an arbitrary date range. `buildPdfSummaryData`'s signature changed from `(allEntries, rangeDays, asOfDate, weekStartsOn)` to `(allEntries, rangeStart, rangeEnd, weekStartsOn)` — plain explicit ISO date strings, no more "N days back from today" concept baked into the data layer at all. `ExportSection.tsx` gained its own `pdfPeriodStart`/`pdfPeriodEnd` state — deliberately separate from the shared `periodStart`/`periodEnd` above (which Excel/CSV/Markdown/ranged-backup already use) rather than reusing it, since that shared picker's "blank means everything" convention would be wrong here: an unbounded weight-trend chart/weekly table would defeat the "one-page summary" point. Prefilled to the last 90 days (matching the old toggle's own upper option) rather than blank; two small "Last 30 days"/"Last 90 days" ghost buttons next to the two `type="date"` `Input`s just overwrite both fields as a shortcut, reusing the same `exportPdfRange30Label`/`exportPdfRange90Label` Dictionary strings the old `ToggleGroup` used (no copy changes needed — they read the same either way). Export button disabled whenever either field is cleared. **Found and fixed along the way**: `ExportSection.test.tsx`'s own `beforeEach` had `vi.stubGlobal('URL', { ...URL, createObjectURL: ..., revokeObjectURL: ... })` — the object-spread replaces the global `URL` with a plain object, silently losing its constructor behavior. `exportPdf.ts`'s dynamic `import('./ptSansRegularFont')` (added by #623, a *local* module — unlike `jspdf`/`jspdf-autotable`'s own bare-specifier dynamic imports, which resolve through a different, unaffected path) needs Vite's own module-resolution machinery to construct a real `new URL(...)` internally; with the stub in place this threw "URL is not a constructor" the moment any test actually clicked through a PDF export, intermittently masked locally by Vite's persistent dep-cache from earlier runs but reliably reproducing in a clean CI checkout — confirmed as the exact cause of a real, reported GitHub Actions failure on #623's own push. Fixed by assigning the two missing static methods directly onto the real `URL` (`URL.createObjectURL = vi.fn(...)`) instead of replacing the global. |
| `exportXlsx.ts` | `buildExportWorkbook(goals, dailyEntries, t)` (#123) — a separate, human-readable view of the same data the JSON backup covers, not meant to be re-imported (no schema/versioning concept here). Three sheets via `exceljs`: **Daily Log** (one row/day — weight, day-level calorie/macro totals via the existing `totalCalories`/`totalProtein`/`totalFat`/`totalCarbs` helpers, sleep, steps, mood, note, period/constipation flags), **Meals** (one row per logged `CalorieItem` flattened across every day/meal — date, meal label or positional `mealLabel(n)` fallback, item name, kcal/macros/grams, time eaten, that item's own reaction (#129, moved from the meal group — each row in a multi-item meal can show a different reaction now), meal note), **Goals** (one row per goal — created date, weekly target). Numeric columns hold raw `number`s (not locale-formatted strings) so Excel can sum/chart directly; date columns are real `Date` cells with a fixed `yyyy-mm-dd` format rather than locale-dependent. Column headers/sheet names come from a new dedicated `exportXlsx` Dictionary section — deliberately **not** reusing `dailyEntry.*`'s own field labels, so wording changes to one don't silently affect the other. `exceljs` itself is dynamically `import()`ed inside the function (not a static top-level import), so `ExportSection.tsx` can import this module normally without pulling the ~930KB (256KB gzipped) library into the Settings route chunk — it only loads when "Export as Excel" is actually clicked. Chosen over the more common `xlsx`/SheetJS npm package, which has unpatched high-severity CVEs (prototype pollution, ReDoS) with no fix available on the npm registry (SheetJS only publishes patches via their own CDN now) — `exceljs` has no equivalent open advisories. |
| `exportCsv.ts` | `buildDailyLogCsv(dailyEntries, t)` (#125) — same "Daily Log" shape as `exportXlsx.ts`'s first sheet, as flat CSV text, hand-written (no library — RFC 4180 quoting/escaping is simple enough that a dependency wasn't worth it, unlike the Excel export). Purpose is distinct from both other exports: pasting into an LLM conversation for analysis — CSV is far more token-efficient than JSON (no repeated key names per row) and parses more reliably than a binary `.xlsx`. Exports `CSV_BOM` (`'﻿'`, named/exported rather than an inline literal since the byte-order-mark character is otherwise invisible in a diff) — prepended to the downloaded `Blob` so Excel correctly detects UTF-8 if the file is opened there instead of pasted into an LLM, or Cyrillic notes/mood labels would mangle. **#219**: gained `waistCm`/`hipCm`/`bodyFatPercent` columns (between Steps and Mood) — a gap left by #225 minutes earlier in the same session, caught and fixed while building the Markdown export below, which needed the same complete column set from the start. `exportXlsx.ts`'s Daily Log sheet (above) got the identical three columns added the same way. |
| `exportMarkdown.ts` (#219) | `buildDailyLogMarkdown(dailyEntries, t)` — same "Daily Log" table shape and column set as `exportCsv.ts` (including the `waistCm`/`hipCm`/`bodyFatPercent` columns), rendered as a GitHub-flavored Markdown table instead: a header row, a `\| --- \|` separator row, then one row per entry. A literal `\|` in a field is escaped (`\\\|`) and an embedded newline collapsed to a space, rather than RFC 4180's quote-the-whole-field approach — Markdown tables have no quoting mechanism, so a raw pipe/newline would otherwise silently break the table structure. Purpose distinct from the CSV export: reads well pasted into a notes app or a Markdown-rendering chat tool, not meant for a spreadsheet or LLM token-efficiency the way CSV is. `ExportSection.tsx` downloads it as `turtle-steps-daily-log-<date>.md`, its own `exportedMarkdownSummary` (entries-only, no goals — same reasoning as the CSV export's `exportedCsvSummary`). |
| `mergeDailyEntryPatches.ts` (#365, generalized further in #366; **#496**) | Shared merge logic every external-source importer calls: `mergeDailyEntryPatches(patches, existingEntries, mode?)` looks up each patched date's existing `DailyEntry` (if any), spreads just the patch's fields onto it, and returns `{ daysImported, daysUpdated, entriesToUpsert }` for the calling importer's summary UI. `DailyEntryPatch` (`Partial<Omit<DailyEntry, 'id'\|'date'\|'createdAt'\|'updatedAt'>>`) is deliberately source-agnostic — originally lived in `zeppLife/` as a Zepp-specific type, pulled up here once `appleHealth/` needed the identical shape. **#367**: gained its first list-field merge — `calorieEntries` always *appends* onto whatever's already logged for that date (same in both conflict modes), accepting duplicate meals when the same meal was logged by hand and present in the import. **#496**: `DailyEntryImportMode = 'fillGaps' \| 'overwrite'` — default `fillGaps` keeps any local field that already has a value (so a manual weight correction survives a later Zepp/Apple Health/MFP re-import) and only fills blanks; `overwrite` is the pre-#496 "imported value wins" policy for when the wearable should replace local. Days where every patched field is already filled are skipped (not upserted / not counted). JSON backup restore stays its own path and is unaffected. UI: `ImportConflictModePicker.tsx` (single-select ToggleGroup chips) on each wearable/MFP import block in `ExportSection.tsx`, defaulting to Fill gaps only. |
| `myFitnessPal/` (#367, #500) | `myFitnessPalParser.ts` — pure mapping from the export's row shape (confirmed live from the user's real Data Access Request sample, including the exact 40-column header) to `DailyEntryPatch`. Unlike Zepp Life/Apple Health's one-file-per-category exports, this is a single flat sheet with an `item_type` discriminator column — `buildMyFitnessPalPatches(rows)` only maps `Foods` (→ `CalorieEntry`/`CalorieItem`, grouped by `(date, details_json.meal)` — this import's actual payoff, since #365/#366 never touch meals at all) and `Measurement` rows where `description === 'weight'`/`unit === 'kilograms'` (→ `weightKg`, the same simple scalar shape #365/#366 already established); every other real `item_type` (`Daily Nutrition Totals`/`Steps`/`User Food`/`Exercise`/`Water`/`User Recipe`/`User Preferences`) is out of scope per the user's own stated priority (meals first — steps/exercise already come from Apple Health) and is skipped, not an error. `details_json` is a per-row JSON string (`meal`, `brand_name` — the latter maps straight to `CalorieItem.brand`, #248) parsed defensively so one malformed row can't take the whole import down. No `timeEaten` — `created_at`/`updated_at` were blank on every sampled row, a confirmed real gap rather than an oversight; no `amountG` either — the export's `value`/`unit` pair mixes a quantity with a serving-size unit (grams sometimes, but also whole-item units like "pizza") that isn't reliably derivable without per-unit judgment calls, so it's skipped rather than guessed. `importMyFitnessPal.ts` — reads the file via `exceljs` (`workbook.xlsx.load`, dynamically imported the same way `exportXlsx.ts` already imports it for the write direction) rather than position-based column reads, resilient to the export's own column order (only the names were confirmed, not the order); row 1 (title) is skipped, row 2 builds a column-name-to-index map, data starts at row 3. **#500**: the live Data Access Request export is often an MS-OFFCRYPTO-encrypted OLE compound document (magic `D0 CF 11 E0…`, still named `.xlsx`) whose password arrives in the same email — #367's mid-implementation note that the sample was a plain unencrypted workbook was true for that sample, but not for the user's later file. `isMyFitnessPalEncrypted` detects OLE; when encrypted, `ExportSection` opens `MyFitnessPalPasswordDialog` (same shape as Zepp's) and `officecrypto-tool` decrypts client-side before exceljs loads; plain ZIP `.xlsx` still imports with no dialog. Wrong password keeps the dialog open (`MyFitnessPalWrongPasswordError`). `vite-plugin-node-polyfills` supplies Node `crypto`/`buffer`/`process` for that library in the browser — all three globals are genuinely required (without the `process` shim the decrypt chunk throws `ReferenceError: process is not defined`, which surfaced as the same generic "not an MFP export" error and caused #500's first on-device validation to fail). The plugin is skipped when `mode === 'test'`: Vitest already runs in real Node, and the shim's `process.cwd()` returns '/', which breaks node-side test code like `theme.contrast.test.ts`'s CSS read. Because only a production build reproduces it, verifying this path means `vite preview` + a real browser, not Vitest. Wired into `ExportSection.tsx` alongside the other two importers, same field-picker/how-to-export-disclosure/summary pattern. **#580/#588**: Foods rows still have no clock time — `#580` stamped built-in slot defaults; `#588` opens `MyFitnessPalSlotTimesDialog` before import (prefilled from `useMealSlotDefaultTimesStore`, also editable in Settings), remembers the chosen clocks, and passes them into `buildMyFitnessPalPatches` / `importMyFitnessPalExport`; encrypted exports still go through the password dialog after the times step. |
| `parseHealthTimestamp.ts` (#365, generalized further in #366) | Shared `parseHealthTimestamp(raw): { localDate, epochMs }` — parses the `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss±ZZZZ` timestamp format both Zepp Life's CSV (`...12:00:00+0000`, no space before the offset) and Apple Health's XML (`...12:00:00 +0300`, confirmed from a real export to have a space before the offset — a real gap this shared parser had to be fixed for once Apple Health needed it too) use; neither is strict ISO 8601, so both need normalizing before `new Date()`. Returns `epochMs` alongside the local date specifically so same-day tiebreaks (`buildZeppLifePatches`, `AppleHealthPatchBuilder.setIfLatest`) compare real chronological instants rather than the raw strings — safe for Zepp's always-`+0000` export, but Apple Health's offset varies per record (device timezone at the time), where naive string comparison gets the ordering wrong. |
| `zeppLife/` (#365) | `zeppLifeParser.ts` (`parseZeppBodyCsv`/`parseZeppActivityCsv` — hand-written CSV parsing, no library, same "simple enough not to need one" reasoning as `exportCsv.ts`; `buildZeppLifePatches` folds `BODY`/`ACTIVITY` rows into one `DailyEntryPatch` per date, last-`time`-wins on a same-day conflict via `parseHealthTimestamp`'s `epochMs`, maps Zepp's `muscleRate` column straight onto `DailyEntry.muscleMassKg` — the column name says "rate" but the value is already kg, matching the in-app "Muscle X kg" reading; **#458** fixed a pre-#458 bug that wrongly treated it as a % of body weight via `weight * rate / 100`, which produced ~22kg values that looked confusingly like BMI for this user's scale range; **#616** parses BODY `height`, `summarizeZeppBodyProfiles` / `filterZeppBodyRowsByHeight`, and optional `USER/USER_*.csv` nickName for labeling), `importZeppLife.ts` (zip.js orchestration: `getEntries()` → find `BODY/BODY_*.csv`/`ACTIVITY/ACTIVITY_*.csv`/`USER/USER_*.csv` by filename regex → `entry.getData(new TextWriter(), { password })`, small files so no streaming needed here unlike Apple Health below → `mergeDailyEntryPatches` → repository upserts; throws `ZeppLifeWrongPasswordError`/`ZeppLifeInvalidFileError`/`ZeppLifeMultipleProfilesError` for the UI to distinguish — multi-height BODY exports require a `selectedHeightCm` before writing; ACTIVITY is not height-keyed and imports as-is), `ZeppLifePasswordDialog.tsx` (the export is always AES-encrypted — password entered fresh each import, held only in this dialog's own state, never persisted), `ZeppLifeProfileDialog.tsx` (#616 — radio picker when more than one height cluster is present, e.g. a shared household scale syncing into one Zepp account). `SLEEP` deliberately out of scope — its column units were unconfirmed from the real sample this was built against, same "don't guess, ship what's confirmed" reasoning that also kept it out of #366. |
| `appleHealth/` (#366) | `appleHealthParser.ts` — `AppleHealthRecordScanner` incrementally extracts `<Record/>` elements from streamed XML chunks (self-closing tags only; per the export's own DTD, every record — including ones nested inside a `Correlation` — also appears flat at the top level, so this is a complete view without needing nesting-awareness). **Found and fixed live against the user's real ~1.35GB export.xml**: an early version let its internal buffer grow unbounded through a long `Record`-free stretch (e.g. inside a `Workout` block), and regex-matching an ever-larger accumulated string forced V8 to repeatedly re-flatten it — a genuine O(n²) blowup (10+ minutes and still climbing on the real file) even though the *scan position* was already being tracked correctly; fixed by actively keeping the buffer bounded to a `TAG_SAFETY_MARGIN` (4096 bytes, comfortably longer than any real tag) instead, confirmed after the fix: 14.6s in plain Node (1.89M total records, 142K tracked), 57s through the real browser UI. `AppleHealthPatchBuilder.addRecord` maps `HKQuantityTypeIdentifierBodyMass`→`weightKg`, `BodyFatPercentage`→`bodyFatPercent` (HealthKit stores this as a 0–1 fraction despite a declared `unit="%"` — confirmed from a real record, non-obvious), `WaistCircumference`→`waistCm` (only accepted when that record's own `unit` attribute says `cm` — HealthKit records self-describe their unit per-record, unlike Zepp's bare CSV numbers, so there's no need to assume), `StepCount`→`steps` (summed per day — records are intraday chunks, not one per day; **#385**: multi-source double-counting fix below), `DietaryWater`→one `WaterEntry` per record. `importAppleHealth.ts` — picks the primary Apple Health XML entry out of the zip (locale-agnostic as of **#618**: largest non-`export_cda.xml` `.xml` entry, so localized names like `экспорт.xml` still import; `workout-routes/*.gpx` and the CDA export are ignored, no password needed since this export is never encrypted), streams it via a `TransformStream` (zip.js writes to the `writable` side, this reads from `readable` chunk by chunk — chosen over handing zip.js a raw `WritableStream` sink directly, which hit a `WritableStream is closed` runtime error mid-development for reasons not fully root-caused), decoding with `TextDecoder({ stream: true })` so a multi-byte UTF-8 character split across chunks decodes correctly. `SLEEP` originally deliberately out of scope, same reasoning as `zeppLife/`'s — HealthKit represents it as overlapping start/end intervals across multiple possible stage vocabularies, not one row per night; see **#368** below for the actual implementation once that reasoning was worked through. **#385**: `StepCount` records used to sum across every `sourceName` for a date, double-counting a walk both an iPhone and Apple Watch logged. `AppleHealthRecord` now captures `sourceName`; `AppleHealthPatchBuilder` sums same-source intraday records keyed per `(date, sourceName)`, then `build()` keeps only the single highest-total source per date and discards the rest — resolved via `AskUserQuestion` as a best-effort dominant-source pick rather than true interval-overlap merging, since no real multi-source sample existed to validate the latter against. A record with no `sourceName` falls into its own "unknown" bucket rather than merging into whichever named source is seen first. Confirmed on-device: re-exporting after the fix showed matching step counts between the Health app and this app's import. **#368**: `HKCategoryTypeIdentifierSleepAnalysis` — a Category-type record, unlike every other type here: `value` is a stage identifier string (`AsleepCore`/`AsleepDeep`/`AsleepREM`/`AsleepUnspecified`/the older unqualified `Asleep`/`InBed`/`Awake`), not a numeric measurement, and the actual "amount" is the record's own `startDate`–`endDate` interval — handled as its own branch in `addRecord()` before the generic numeric-`value` check would otherwise reject it. Bucketed by the **wake** date (the calendar day a night's final interval *ends* on), matching how a person actually describes "how did I sleep" the following morning, not the day they fell asleep. `Awake` intervals contribute to neither total. `InBed` is used as a fallback total only when a source recorded no real `Asleep*` interval at all that night (the only signal pre-stage-tracking sources ever wrote) — never summed alongside real sleep stages from the same source. `AsleepDeep` intervals are tracked separately as `deepSleepHours`, additive with (not separate from) the `sleepHours` total. Same dominant-source-per-night pick as `StepCount`/#385 above and for the identical reason — a phone, watch, and third-party sleep app can each log their own overlapping interval for the same real night; summing across sources would double the total. HealthKit's exact category-value set (`AsleepUnspecified` replacing the older unqualified `Asleep` in iOS 16, though either can appear depending on OS version at recording time) is implemented from stable public Apple documentation, not confirmed against a real multi-source sleep export the way #366's other field mappings originally were — flagged for on-device confirmation. `ImportFieldPicker`'s Apple Health field list (`ExportSection.tsx`'s `APPLE_HEALTH_FIELDS`) grew from 5 to 7 entries (`sleepHours`/`deepSleepHours` added), and the import blurb copy now mentions sleep. **#411**: `HKCategoryTypeIdentifierMenstrualFlow` — another Category-type record, same shape as sleep (no numeric `value`, just a flow-level identifier string), handled as its own branch in `addRecord()` alongside sleep's. Maps directly onto the existing `onPeriod` boolean (#61): any record whose value isn't `HKCategoryValueMenstrualFlowNone` marks that date as a period day; a date with no flow record at all (or only a "None" one) is simply left unset — same "no confirmed-false state modeled" shape `onPeriod`/`hadConstipation` already have elsewhere, not a tri-state like night eating's derived value. Bucketed by the record's own `startDate` (no interval math needed, unlike sleep). `APPLE_HEALTH_FIELDS` grew to 8 entries (`onPeriod` added) — offered unconditionally like every other field here, not gated behind `useCycleTrackingStore`'s Settings opt-in; this picker has no precedent for gating any field behind a tracking-enabled store (`waterEntries` isn't gated behind water tracking either). Also researched during #411: `LeanBodyMass`→`muscleMassKg` is a possible future candidate but needs validation against a real export first (HealthKit's "lean body mass" is total fat-free mass, not isolated muscle tissue), and body water %/bone mass/visceral fat/mood/meal data were all considered and ruled out — no clean HealthKit mapping exists for any of them. **#412**: reported live — deep sleep missing for one specific night despite that night's *total* sleep importing correctly, confirmed by the user to be a single-source export (ruling out the multi-source dominant-source-pick as the cause). Real root cause: each sleep record is bucketed by its own `end.localDate`, but the class-level intent ("keyed by the wake date... a night's final interval ends on") assumes every segment of one continuous night ends after midnight — untrue when the night's *first* segment itself both starts and ends before midnight, which deep sleep is disproportionately likely to do since it concentrates in the first sleep cycle. That segment's seconds silently landed in the *previous* calendar day's bucket instead of the actual wake date. Fixed with a `mergeSleepSessionsAcrossMidnight()` pass in `build()`, run once before the existing per-date dominant-source pick: `SleepTotals` gained `firstStartMs`/`lastEndMs` (min/max real timestamp folded into that bucket so far); for each source, if a date's bucket's `lastEndMs` and the very next calendar date's bucket's `firstStartMs` are within `SLEEP_SESSION_MERGE_GAP_MS` (4h), the earlier bucket is folded forward into the later one and removed — a real two-nights-apart gap is always many hours (a full waking day), so this cleanly distinguishes "one session split by a technicality" from "two real, separate nights." New tests cover both the exact reported shape (pre-midnight deep segment merges forward, total 8.5h/deep 0.75h on the wake date) and that two genuinely separate nights a full day apart don't get merged. `tsc -b`/lint/`appleHealthParser.test.ts`+`importAppleHealth.test.ts`+`ExportSection.test.tsx` (45 tests) green. **#412 reopened (still broken on-device for Jul 2, 2026)**: the midnight-merge fix was real for its own shape, but the Jul 2 example was a different bug. Inspecting the user's real `export.zip` showed two sources for that wake date — AutoSleep with `AsleepUnspecified` totaling exactly 7h19m and **zero** `AsleepDeep` records (its own UI "Deep 1:15" never lands in HealthKit as staged deep), and the Apple Watch with real `AsleepDeep` stages (~50m) but a shorter asleep total (~5h55m). Dominant-source pick for total sleep correctly chose AutoSleep, then took deep **only from that same source** → deepHours unset → UI `—`. Fixed by keeping the dominant-source pick for `sleepHours`, but taking `deepSleepHours` as the **max** `deepSeconds` across sources for that night (never a sum — two staged devices would otherwise double-count). New tests cover the AutoSleep+Watch Jul 2 shape and the no-sum guard. Confirmed on-device after re-import: deep sleep displays (Watch stages; AutoSleep’s higher UI deep figure remains unavailable from HealthKit and is accepted as by-design). **#411 reopened**: reported live — real period days visible in the user's own Apple Health Cycle Tracking showed no marker at all on any of those dates after import. Real root cause, found by inspecting the scanner's own regex rather than the `MenstrualFlow`-specific mapping logic (which tested correctly in isolation all along): `RECORD_TAG_RE` only ever matched the self-closing `<Record .../>` shape — but Apple Health writes the *paired* `<Record ...>...</Record>` shape whenever a record carries `<MetadataEntry>` children, which is routine for manually-logged `MenstrualFlow` records (`HKMetadataKeyMenstrualCycleStart`) and for any `HKWasUserEntered`-flagged record generally. Every metadata-bearing record was silently dropped by the scanner itself, before ever reaching `AppleHealthPatchBuilder` at all — not a period-specific bug, a general parser gap that happened to surface there first, since manually-tracked period data routinely carries metadata while sensor-logged quantity samples (weight, steps) often don't. Fixed by widening `RECORD_TAG_RE` to match both shapes in one pattern (self-closing, or a lazy match up to the first `</Record>` — `Record` elements don't nest, so this stays a complete, correct view). New tests cover the metadata-child shape (single/multiple entries, split across a chunk boundary, no false nesting past one record into the next) plus a perf-regression guard confirming the new lazy alternative doesn't reintroduce the buffer's already-once-fixed O(n²) trap, and an end-to-end scanner+builder test reproducing the exact reported scenario. **#525**: reported live — sleep correlation axis stretched to ~100h; backup had 20 days >24h (e.g. 2025-09-05 = 95.98h). Root causes: (1) within one source, Asleep* intervals were **summed with no overlap handling**, so duplicate/reprocessed HealthKit samples inflated totals and then won the max-hours dominant-source pick; (2) #412's ≤4h midnight merge **chained multi-day short-gap naps** into one wake date. Fix: store raw intervals per bucket, derive covered time via `unionIntervalSeconds`, only midnight-merge when the previous day is a **small fragment** (≤4h covered, `SLEEP_MIDNIGHT_FRAGMENT_MAX_SECONDS`), and cap imported `sleepHours`/`deepSleepHours` at 24 (same as the form schema). User re-imports after the fix. |
| `index.ts` | `ExportSection` only — no `ExportScreen` anymore. |

#### `about/AboutScreen.tsx`, `about/PrivacyScreen.tsx`, `about/FeaturesScreen.tsx` — issue #23 (new feature area), #312 (privacy policy page), #346 (features overview page)

Static page: project intro/philosophy/privacy copy from the dictionary, an author credit linking to `github.com/ZhannaM85` (#35). **#213**: full copy rewrite, user-supplied verbatim (sent twice with two different drafts within minutes — the later one was used). Grew from three paragraphs (`intro`/`philosophy`/`privacy`) to five: `intro` (now opens on "many factors, not just calories" rather than the app's own name), a new `tracking` paragraph (names every field the app logs — calories, protein, carbs, fat, sleep, activity, cycle, meals, notes — and why: seeing patterns, not just recording numbers), `philosophy` (steady weekly progress, not perfect days), a new short `privacyHeading` ("Private by design.", rendered `font-medium text-foreground` as a small heading rather than folded into the `privacy` paragraph's own sentence, matching how it reads standalone in the source copy), then `privacy` itself (unchanged in spirit, reworded to "No accounts. No cloud. No tracking."). Translated to Russian following the same en/ru pattern every other dictionary entry uses — not machine-translated key-for-key, since "Turtle Steps" needed to become the app's own established Russian name «Черепашка идёт к цели» (already used elsewhere, e.g. the original `intro`) for the copy to read naturally rather than as a literal translation. Reachable via the "Heart" nav item, in the tab-bar slot #24 freed by folding Export into Settings. Also hosts **`ReleaseNotesSection.tsx`** (#63, moved here from Settings by #66) — collapsed by default (same chevron-toggle pattern as History's row expand, `#39`), reveals brief bilingual entries from `src/data/releaseNotes.ts` (`{ version, issue, date, en, ru }[]`, most-recent-first) when opened. That data file is a top-level `src/data/` folder — plain static content, not a domain entity or a repository-backed concern, so it sits outside `domain/`. **Updating it is a required step whenever a GitHub issue closes** (see `CLAUDE.md`), alongside `docs/issues-priority.md` and this file. The #66 move was a straight relocation, not a rename pass: its dictionary strings stayed under `settings.releaseNotesLabel`/`showReleaseNotes`/`hideReleaseNotes` rather than being renamed to an `about.*` namespace. **`version`** (added live per direct instruction, 2026-07-19): a simple incrementing counter, oldest entry = 1, backfilled across all 158 then-existing entries via a scripted transform with a round-trip verification (same approach #131 used for `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own long rows) rather than 158 manual edits. `AboutScreen.tsx` shows the current version (`releaseNotes[0].version`) prominently near the top of the page, not just inside the collapsed list — so a bug report can be pinned to a specific version at a glance, which a date alone can't do once multiple versions ship the same day (routine here). **#312**: a new sibling `PrivacyScreen.tsx`, routed at its own stable `/privacy` (`router.tsx`/`lazyRoutes.ts`, same lazy-chunk pattern as every other non-Today screen) — the full privacy policy both the App Store and Play Store submission flows (#313/#316) need a fixed URL for. `AboutScreen.tsx`'s existing one-line `privacy` blurb stays as-is and now links to this page (`readPrivacyPolicyLabel`) instead of being replaced by it — the blurb is the at-a-glance summary, `/privacy` is the complete policy. New `privacyPolicy` Dictionary block (en+ru): what's collected (nothing automatic), where it lives (device-only, no account/server/cloud), third-party sharing (none), how data can leave the device (only the user's own Settings export), children, policy changes, and contact sections. Doesn't cover the stores' own separate Data Safety/privacy-nutrition-label questionnaires — those are filled out per-store in #317/#315, this issue is only the hosted page itself. **#346**: a third sibling, `FeaturesScreen.tsx`, at `/features` — a scannable "what can this app do" summary, distinct from this file's own intro/philosophy copy and from `ReleaseNotesSection.tsx`'s chronological changelog (a log of changes over time, not a current snapshot). Content is a new `featuresOverview.categories` Dictionary array (`{ id, heading, items: string[] }[]`, en+ru), 8 categories covering everything shipped as of this session, each rendered as its own `Card` with a bulleted `<ul>`. Deliberately a plain hand-maintained array, not generated from any manifest/route registry (none exists) — will drift out of sync with reality unless updated by hand whenever a materially new capability ships, same tradeoff `about.tracking`'s own feature list already accepts; called out in a code comment on the component itself rather than solved with new tooling. `AboutScreen.tsx` gained a `viewFeaturesLabel` link to it (placed after the `philosophy` paragraph, before the privacy section), and the features page links back to `/about` the same way `PrivacyScreen.tsx` does. **#495** refreshes the About summary and both language versions of the Features overview to reflect the current nutrition, body-composition, custom-metric, History, and external-import capabilities rather than the early weight-and-calories scope. The public README now uses the same current framing and four deterministic, Tortoise-theme screenshots generated by `scripts/screenshot-seed.mjs` and `scripts/capture-screenshots.mjs`; the capture script imports a real v7 backup through the UI and verifies all demo entries reached IndexedDB before taking images. **#497**: each Features category card also shows a matching screenshot from `public/screenshots/` (same folder the README uses — capture output moved there from `docs/screenshots/` so assets don't drift). Stable category `id`s map to eight PNGs (the four README shots plus new goal/correlations/export/appearance captures); `screenshotAlt(heading)` supplies accessible alt text. Images are excluded from the Workbox precache (`screenshots/**` in `vite.config.ts`) and load lazily only when `/features` is opened. **#498**: Features / Privacy / Version are no longer quiet inline text links under the prose — they lead the page as the same "description + outline button" `Card`s Settings uses, with narrative intro/tracking/philosophy/privacy copy below and release notes last.

---

### App shell & routing (`src/app/`, `src/main.tsx`)

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `router.tsx` | `createBrowserRouter`, all screens nested under `AppShell`: `/` (Today), `/dashboard`, `/history`, `/goal`, `/settings`, `/settings/foods` (`FoodListSettingsScreen`, #90 — the app's first drill-down sub-page, not a tab-bar item; `NavLink`'s default non-`end` matching still highlights the "Settings" tab for it), `/about` (#23), and `/export` → `<Navigate to="/settings" replace />` (#24 — kept as an explicit redirect for anyone with the old route bookmarked, rather than a dead link). **#461** removed `/entry/:date/meal/:mealId` (`MealEditScreen`, #157) — meal editing is now an in-place `AddMealDialog` overlay inside `MealList`, so Today/History never unmount for it. `basename: import.meta.env.BASE_URL` so routes resolve correctly under the GitHub Pages subpath. **#102**: the top-level route object also sets `errorElement: <RouteErrorFallback />` — React Router wraps the whole subtree it's attached to in an error boundary, so any uncaught render error anywhere below `AppShell` shows a friendly fallback instead of silently unmounting to a blank screen (there was previously no error boundary anywhere in the app). **#102 (code splitting)**: every route except `/` (`TodayScreen`, imported eagerly since it's the default route) is now `React.lazy()`-loaded from `lazyRoutes.ts` rather than imported directly — the production bundle was previously a single ~1.3MB (380KB gzipped) file blocking first paint on every route; splitting brought the main chunk down to ~480KB (~147KB gzipped), with Dashboard/History/Settings/About/Goal each fetched on first visit instead. |
| `lazyRoutes.ts` | The `React.lazy()` definitions consumed by `router.tsx`, split into their own file because `react-refresh/only-export-components` flags a file that exports both components and non-component values (`router.tsx` also exports `routes`/`router`). Each `lazy()` call unwraps a named export into the default-export shape `lazy()` requires (e.g. `import('@/features/dashboard').then(m => ({ default: m.DashboardScreen }))`). **#461** dropped the `MealEditScreen` lazy export along with its route. |
| `RouteErrorFallback.tsx` | The `errorElement` above — title/description/Reload button (`window.location.reload()`), reads from a new top-level `t.error` Dictionary section. Deliberately generic (doesn't render the actual error/stack to the user) since the audience is a non-technical end user, not a developer. |
| `RouteLoadingFallback.tsx` | The `Suspense` fallback for the lazy routes above (rendered by `AppShell.tsx`, wrapping `<Outlet />`) — a small centered spinner, same visual language as `PullToRefreshIndicator`. Only ever visible for the brief window while a route's own chunk is downloading. |
| `AppShell.tsx` (#234) | `useNavItems(t)` dropped its `/about` entry — 6 tabs read as too crowded on mobile. Still a real route (`router.tsx`), just reached via a new "About" card in `SettingsScreen.tsx` (same "description + link button" shape as that screen's existing Food-list card) instead of a nav slot — the mobile header has no icon row of its own to move it to, unlike the alternative the report floated. |
| `AppShell.tsx` | One shared `useNavItems(t)` array (now Today/Dashboard/History/Goal/Settings — no About, no Export entry) drives both a header `<nav aria-label="Main">` (hidden below `sm:`, **sticky** — `sticky top-0 z-10 border-b border-border bg-background`, #25) and a fixed bottom `<nav aria-label="Tabs">` tab bar (visible only below `sm:`, `lucide-react` icon + label per route). **#112**: tabs grown from `min-h-14`/56px for bigger tap targets, and the nav accounts for all three horizontal/vertical safe-area insets (`env(safe-area-inset-left/right/bottom)`, previously only bottom), plus a `px-2` on the tab `<ul>` so the edge tabs aren't flush against the viewport edge even without a safe-area inset. **#119**: #112's `min-h-[106px]` (+50px) read as an oversized empty gap above the icons once seen live — tuned down to `min-h-20` (80px, +24px), still a meaningfully bigger target than the original 56px without the excess gap; `<main>`'s bottom padding correspondingly reduced from `pb-44` to `pb-32 sm:pb-10`. **#115**: renders `<AppUpdateBanner />` above the header (see below). **#120**: the fixed bottom `<nav aria-label="Tabs">` is hidden entirely (`!isTextInputFocused &&`) while a text-entry control anywhere in the app has focus, via the new `useIsTextInputFocused()` hook (`shared/hooks/`, `focusin`/`focusout` on `document`, checking `document.activeElement` directly). Mitigation, not a root-cause fix: iOS Safari is known to render `position: fixed` elements at an unpredictable spot relative to the shrunk visual viewport once the on-screen keyboard opens, which isn't practically fixable/verifiable without a device — the keyboard already covers most of the screen during typing, so hiding the bar sidesteps the bug rather than fighting WebKit's viewport math directly. **#188**: reproduced again live (two users, in the web app specifically) with the same symptom — didn't reproduce in Chromium, so widened the mitigation by reasoning rather than confirming on-device. `hideTabBar` is now `isTextInputFocused || isViewportShrunk`, the latter from the new `useVisualViewportShrunk()` hook — the keyboard's open/close is an *animated* viewport resize on iOS Safari that can lag a focus/blur event by a couple hundred ms, a gap plain focus-tracking alone can't cover. Same "not verifiable without a device" caveat as before. **#185**: React Router doesn't reset scroll position on a client-side navigation the way a traditional multi-page site does — landing on a new, shorter route (e.g. `MealEditScreen`, #157) while still scrolled down from the previous page left the new content starting mid-page. Fixed with `useEffect(() => window.scrollTo(0, 0), [pathname])`, keyed on `pathname` from `useLocation()` rather than the full `location` object so a search-param-only change (e.g. History's own filters) doesn't also jump the page back to the top. **#484** / **#493**: mobile active tab uses `bg-muted text-primary` on an **inner** `rounded-2xl` pill around icon+label only — #484's first readable tint painted the whole `min-h-20` flex cell as a tall slab; #493 keeps that tall tap target on the `NavLink` but moves the visible highlight onto the pill. Tab `<ul>` padding is `px-4` (was `px-2`) so edge tabs align with `<main>`'s content padding. Inactive tabs stay `text-muted-foreground`. |
| `shared/hooks/useIsTextInputFocused.ts` (#262) | Root-caused via a live Playwright event trace (every `pointerdown`/`mousedown`/`focusout`/`focusin`/`pointerup`/`mouseup`/`click`, with real target), found while verifying #233's new Settings Profile card: tapping the Sex `ToggleGroupItem` right after typing in the Age field above it could silently fail to register on the first tap. Old code updated state **synchronously** on both `focusin` and `focusout` — fine for `focusin` landing on a real text input (hiding the bar can't steal a click), but `focusin` landing on a *non*-text control (e.g. the very button just clicked) also fired synchronously, flipping `isFocused` back to `false` and re-inserting the fixed bottom tab bar into the DOM *before* that same click's `pointerup`/`mouseup` had been dispatched — the reappeared bar then received those events instead of the tapped control (confirmed in the trace: `pointerup`/`mouseup`/`click` all landed on a tab-bar nav link, not the button). Fix: only the transition *into* a keyboard-opening element applies immediately; any transition *out* of one — via `focusout` or via `focusin` on a non-keyboard element — now waits for that same gesture's own `pointerup` (100ms fallback for a focus change with no pointer at all, e.g. Tab key) before applying, so the bar can't reappear mid-gesture regardless of which event reports the change first or how long the gesture actually takes. A fixed delay alone (tried first, 150ms) wasn't sufficient — event dispatch timing isn't a constant, so waiting for the real up-event rather than guessing a duration is what actually closes the race. |
| `AppUpdateBanner.tsx` (#115, Reload fixed #205, still unreliable on-device #211) | Renders nothing until `useAppUpdateAvailable()` (`shared/hooks/`) reports a newer deploy, then a dismissal-free bar with a Reload button, calling the shared `shared/lib/reloadForUpdate.ts` (see below). Split from #102's investigation — the "how do I even refresh this" question doesn't have a good answer without some form of update detection, and this is that mechanism. **#211**: #205's fix still needed up to 6 presses on a real device. User asked whether to remove the button in favor of pull-to-refresh — #118 already added a real pull gesture that also works in the standalone PWA context (this banner's own doc comment claiming pull-to-refresh "does nothing" there was stale, predating #118), so the recommendation was to keep both rather than remove one: the banner is the visible, no-gesture-required affordance, the pull gesture has no discoverable call-to-action of its own. The real remaining gap traced to `registration.update()`'s own fetch of `sw.js` being subject to whatever caching GitHub Pages' CDN applies to that file — outside this app's control from client code, and not reproducible against a local `vite preview` server (no CDN in front of it), so not independently confirmed, only inferred from every other explanation for the reported symptom being ruled out. Mitigated (not fully fixable client-side) two ways: `reloadForUpdate()`'s own click-time check moved to `shared/lib/` so `usePullToRefresh` can share it too, not just this banner; and `useAppUpdateAvailable` now also proactively calls `registration.update()` on its own 5-minute poll once it knows an update exists, rather than only ever checking once at click-time — spreading independent checks out over time gives a much better chance one lands after the CDN cache naturally clears, rather than depending entirely on how many times a button gets pressed. **#242**: `reloadForUpdate()`'s bounded wait (up to `CONTROLLER_CHANGE_TIMEOUT_MS`, 5s) had no visible feedback in between, reported as looking broken and prompting repeated clicks — exactly the symptom #211 already independently traced to real devices sometimes needing several presses. New local `reloading` boolean, set the moment Reload is clicked; while true the button is replaced entirely (not just disabled) by a `RefreshCw`+`animate-spin` indicator (same icon/animation `PullToRefreshIndicator.tsx` already uses) with a "Reloading…" label — nothing resets it back to the button, since every code path through `reloadForUpdate()` ends in a real `window.location.reload()`. |
| `PullToRefreshIndicator.tsx` + `hooks/usePullToRefresh.ts` (#118, reload logic shared #211) | A real drag-down-to-refresh gesture, since dragging down previously did nothing at all in the iOS home-screen standalone context (no Safari chrome to catch the native gesture) — the user explicitly asked for the real gesture over a simpler explicit-button alternative. `usePullToRefresh` attaches raw `touchstart`/`touchmove`/`touchend`/`touchcancel` listeners on `document` (gesture-tracking state lives in refs so the effect only attaches once, not on every pull-distance update) — only activates when the drag starts at `window.scrollY === 0`, so it doesn't interfere with normal scrolling or in-page drag gestures elsewhere on the page (e.g. meal reordering, `@dnd-kit`). Crossing a 70px pull threshold on release calls `reloadForUpdate()` (**#211**, was a plain `window.location.reload()` — the same staleness risk `AppUpdateBanner`'s Reload button had before #205 fixed it there specifically, now shared instead of only living in that one component) so "drag down" gets the same update-aware reload as every other refresh affordance in the app, not just the same underlying action. `PullToRefreshIndicator` renders nothing at rest; while pulling, a small floating badge (`RefreshCw` icon) rotates in proportion to pull progress, then spins continuously (`animate-spin`) once the threshold is crossed and reload is imminent. |
| `index.ts` | Barrel. |
| `src/main.tsx` | Entry point: `StrictMode` + `RouterProvider`. |
| `index.html` (#102) | A CSS-only `#root:empty::before` spinner shows while `#root` is literally empty — i.e. before React has mounted anything into it — and disappears automatically the instant it does, no JS coordination needed. Approximates the pond-mood light/dark colors directly in a plain `<style>` block since `index.css` hasn't loaded yet at that point. Addresses the ~60s-blank-with-nothing-visible symptom from #102's original report; doesn't address *why* that window is so long specifically in the iOS home-screen-shortcut context, which remains open. |

---

### Design system (`src/shared/ui/`) — Epic 2, issue #3; extended by #12/#16/#43

shadcn-style primitives (Nova preset, `radix-ui` primitives, `cva` variants, aliases at `@/shared/ui`).

| File | Exports | Notes |
|------|---------|-------|
| `button.tsx` | `Button`, `buttonVariants` | Variants: `default/outline/secondary/ghost/destructive/link`; sizes incl. icon variants. **#420 convention**: no named size is guaranteed to match a sibling row element's height on its own — when a button shares a row with another button/input of a different size (e.g. a text-content button next to an `icon-xl`/`h-12` field), override the mismatched one with an explicit height (`className="h-12 ..."`) rather than leaving visibly uneven heights in one row. **#474 convention**: a **primary footer action** — the full-width CTA at the bottom of a sheet/dialog/edit card (Save, Done, Find food, Repeat meal, Copy yesterday…) — uses the named `size="xl"` (48px, `h-12 … text-base`) with `className="w-full"`. This replaced a hand-written `size="lg" className="h-12 w-full text-base"` string repeated across 5 call sites, which is exactly how `AddMealDialog`'s Done drifted back to the default `h-8` with only `w-full` and rendered visibly smaller than the Save button beside it. `xl` reuses `lg`'s geometry (`gap-1.5 px-2.5`) so that migration was pixel-identical; the old string no longer exists in the codebase to be copied. Right-aligned dialog footers that pair a CTA with a ghost Cancel (`RecipeEditorDialog`, `LogRecipeDialog`) are a *different* pattern and deliberately stay `size="lg"`. |
| `card.tsx` | `Card` + `Header/Title/Description/Content/Footer/Action` | Standard shadcn card family, now the standard section wrapper across Settings/Dashboard/History (#43). |
| `input.tsx` | `Input` | Bare styled `<input>`. **#241**: converted to `React.forwardRef` — was a plain function component, so a `ref` passed in from a consumer (`NumberInput`, ultimately react-hook-form's `register()`) never reached the real DOM node, silently breaking any ref-dependent behavior (found via `GoalForm.tsx`'s `reset()` not clearing its fields). |
| `label.tsx` | `Label` | Radix `Label.Root` wrapper. |
| `text-field.tsx` | `TextField` | Labeled input with `hint`/`error`, wires `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby`, id via `useId`. |
| `number-input.tsx` | `NumberInput` | Same labeled-field pattern as `TextField`, plus a unit suffix slot and an optional `InfoTooltip` (#16). Renders `type="text"` `inputMode="decimal"` (issue #12 — was `type="number"`, which silently rejected the comma decimal separator on some mobile keyboards/locales; pairs with `parseNumberInput()` accepting both `.` and `,`). |
| `info-tooltip.tsx` | `InfoTooltip` | Radix `Popover`-based tap-triggered info icon (#16) — e.g. the Calories field's day-lag-with-weight explanation. **#422**: new optional `icon?: ReactNode` prop overrides the default `Info` glyph, defaulting to `undefined` (existing callers unaffected) — for a caller whose trigger already carries its own meaningful icon (Today's date-navigator checkmark badge, #405) that shouldn't be replaced by a generic "i" just to become tappable. |
| `toggle-group.tsx` | `ToggleGroup`, `ToggleGroupItem` | Radix `ToggleGroup` wrapper (#43) — replaces raw radio inputs for unit/language/mood/color-scheme (Settings) and List/Calendar (History) toggles. |
| `dialog.tsx` | `Dialog`, `DialogTrigger`, `DialogContent`, `DialogTitle`, `DialogDescription` | Radix `Dialog` wrapper (#55) — the app's first real modal primitive; every other "detail" surface (History's expand panel, `InfoTooltip`) had used inline/popover patterns instead. `DialogContent` requires a `closeLabel` prop (no visible text on the icon-only close button).
